"Continues from where it left off" is a property of the *world*, not of the gate: the next cycle re-runs the whole ranked walk from the top over live data, and it reaches further only because the candidates it emitted last cycle have since been picked up, closed or relabelled. **A resume cursor would be a cached selection determination**, which is the same class of object as a cached authorization, and §6.2 pins its absence. **The consequence to state honestly: if the head of the ranked queue never clears, the early exit re-evaluates the same head every cycle and never reaches the tail.** ~~That is the reachability trade AM-29's `updated_at DESC` key exists to manage~~ — **CORRECTED IN REVISION 6: that key is withdrawn (TP-1), so nothing manages the trade dynamically any more.** Under the binding walk key **`(rank, number DESC)`** the head of each band is **fixed** — the highest-numbered, i.e. most recently filed, record in that band — and it does not move for any act short of a rank change. **So the consequence is sharper than revision 5 stated it: a never-clearing head is now a PERMANENT head, and the records behind it are starved deterministically rather than transiently.** That is **R-8**'s subject, and §7.2 R-8 is rewritten around it rather than around an attacker.

##### The three bounds

Revision 3's authorization-check cap **had no FR and no AD**. AD-10's per-cycle cap is the *assessor's* (ESC-5); this one was introduced by this document at self-rated MEDIUM confidence, "not derived from measurement", and was never put to the human — while Amendment 2 §3(b) H1 item 2 assured the human that the queue's true size *"is knowable only by running the gate, which is what S2 ships."* **That assurance was not true under a cap of 25**: a gate that evaluates 25 of 97 records cannot report the queue's size. That is the sharpest of the panel's three aggravators, because it is the point at which a design-stage number reached the human's clearance text wearing an architect's authority. The bounds now have an architectural home, and **each cites the ruling that authorizes it and the property that makes its truncation safe** (AM-23's extension of AR-5).

| Bound | Value | What it bounds | What it may truncate | Harm class (AM-31) | Report | Authorized by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **List pages** (Step C) | `--paginate`, **5 pages / 500 records**. Not a flag — a module constant. | The candidate **set** | **Unordered** — `created desc`, so the **oldest** records | **Completeness-affecting.** No truncated record can be wrongly *authorized*; some can be wrongly *omitted*. **Exit 0 on a stated ground, NOT "under protest"** (AM-31) | `WARN candidate-list-truncated pages=5` — **the loudest class** — **stating *"at least 500 records matched; the total is unknown"*, never a count the gate cannot derive**, plus `complete=no` | **AM-22 / AF-8**, criterion by **AM-31** |
| **Per-cycle API request ceiling** | **≈100 requests.** `--max-api-requests`, **may only lower**: `min(100, max(0, provided))` | The **cost** of the determination, in the **rationed resource** | The **ranked tail** (AM-19/AM-29) | **Completeness-affecting**, and **ordered**: nothing the stop leaves unreached can outrank anything admitted (*revision 7 wording, RP4-1; includes a clamp that bites*) | `unevaluated:cost-ceiling` on the summary and `UNEVALUATED` lines. **NO `WARN` — dropped from the WARN class in revision 5** (AM-30 point 3), plus `complete=no` | **AM-30**, replacing AM-19's check-denominated ceiling |
| **Events pages per issue** (D5.2) | 10 pages / 1000 events. Not a flag. **Additionally clamped by the remaining request budget** (§1.4 rule 4) | **One record's** determination | **Page bound: nothing** — the record is gated (a determination). **Budget clamp that bites (revision 7): the walk's TAIL.** The clamped record is `unevaluated:cost-ceiling`, never gated, and the walk ends there, so this is the ceiling row's truncation arriving mid-record | Page bound: fail-closed for exactly one issue, every other candidate unaffected. Clamp: as the ceiling row | Page bound: `WARN events-page-bound-reached issue=#<n>`. Clamp bite: `unevaluated:cost-ceiling issue=#<n>` + `WARN budget-clamped-events-fetch issue=#<n>`, and **not** `events-page-bound-reached` (§1.4 rule 4(b)) | **AM-5**, unchanged and explicitly confirmed by AM-30. The clamp's disposition is this document's (TP-2, RP4-1(i)) |

**Two bounds are deliberately NOT in this table, and the reason differs for each.**

- **The early exit** (`--max-candidates`, default 5) is a **sufficiency** stop, not a bound on cost or on the queried set. It ends the walk when the answer is *already good enough to act on*, it reports `unevaluated:sufficient` and `complete=no`, and it **cannot coexist with the `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` condition**, which requires `eligible == 0`. It is specified above rather than here because it is now the **routine** end of a healthy walk rather than an outer limit.
- **The collaborator page bound** (`COLLABORATOR_PAGE_BOUND = 3`, §1.3.2) bounds a **configuration** read, not the candidate set or a record's determination. Exhausting it is not an error: resolution proceeds over what was fetched, `WARN collaborator-page-bound-reached pages=3` fires, and the effect can only ever be to **under**-trust. **It costs 0 requests when no tier is listed**, which is every deployment that does not use the feature, including this one.

**REVISION 5 — the report class of the cost ceiling CHANGES, and the change is the opposite of what a reader would guess (AM-30 point 3, binding).** Revision 4 gave the ceiling a `WARN` on the premise that reaching it was exceptional. **AM-30 withdraws that premise in terms** — *"the ceiling WILL bind in normal operation"* — and **§0.0.4's measurement shows the population (101) has already crossed it (100)**. A `WARN` on that condition would have started firing on the first fully-gated cycle after merge and never stopped, which is AM-24's own argument turned on its author: *"a warning that fires on every cycle of correct operation is not a warning; it is a banner, and by the second week it is invisible."* **So the routine ceiling report drops out of the `WARN` class and keeps its two fields.** `unevaluated:list-truncated` and `unevaluated:query-failed` are **anomalies** and keep the loudest class. **Do not "restore" the ceiling's WARN for symmetry** — the asymmetry is the ruling.

> **REVISION 7 CORRECTION (RUN4 RP4-6(j)): "the population (101) has already crossed it (100)" compares RECORDS with REQUESTS, and the derivation was invalid even though the conclusion is now true.** The ceiling is denominated in requests (AM-30). A fully-gated walk over `N` candidates costs `P + (N − F) × e` requests, where `P` is the Step C list pages, `F` the trusted-authored (free) records, and `e ≥ 1` the events pages per paid record. At revision 5's 101 records, with `F = 4` and `P = 2`, that is `2 + 97 = 99 ≤ 100`, so **the ceiling did NOT bind at 101 (or at 102)**, and revision 5's *"it arrived"* was premature. **It binds now.** At RUN4 M-1's 149 candidates with `F = 4` it is `2 + 145 = 147 > 100`. The correct test is `P + (N − F) > C`, never `N > C`. That is exactly the class §2.2.1's own provenance paragraph warns against (a derived number laundered as a measured one). **The same records-versus-requests comparison appears at §0.0.4 settlement 2, §1.2 consequence 5, §2.2.1's pagination correction below, §2.3's AM-30 withdrawal note and D5.1, F32, §6.2's `test_request_ceiling_stops_the_walk_QUIETLY` and HRR (xv).** Each is corrected by this paragraph rather than by editing the record (AM-38). §7.2 R-8's instance is corrected at the site, because R-8's content is what goes into #1539's comment. **Nothing in the design turns on the correction.** AM-30 point 3 withdrew the ceiling's `WARN` because the ceiling *will* bind in normal operation. That was true as a forecast at 101 and is true as a measurement at 149.

##### The request counter — its semantics, defined here because AM-30 changed its unit (this subsumes RP-21)

RP-21 found an off-by-one in revision 4's `auth_checks` accounting. **AM-30 makes that finding moot by changing what is counted**, and routes the replacement definition to me. It is stated as a contract rather than as an implementation note, because *"increment before or after"* is exactly the kind of detail two readers resolve differently:

| Property | Binding definition |
|---|---|
| **What is counted** | **One unit per GitHub API request actually issued**, by any part of the gate: each Step C list page, each events page in D5.2, and each collaborator page in the tier resolver. **Not checks, not records, not candidates.** |
| **Field name** | `api_requests=<A>` on the summary line, **replacing `auth_checks=<A>`.** A coder who finds `auth_checks` anywhere in this document should treat it as a defect in the document |
| **When it increments** | **After a request returns**, successfully or not — a request that fails still consumed rate limit. A request the gate declines to issue because the budget is exhausted is **not** counted |
| **The stop test** | **Before issuing a request**, the gate requires `api_requests < effective_ceiling`. If not, it **does not issue it**, stops the walk, and reports `unevaluated:cost-ceiling`. **This is the definition that removes RP-21's off-by-one: the ceiling is the number of requests that MAY be issued, and `api_requests` never exceeds it** |
| **Interaction with the events page bound** | A record's events fetch may consume at most `min(EVENTS_PAGE_BOUND, effective_ceiling - api_requests)` pages (§1.4 rule 4), so **a fetch already in flight can never carry the counter past the ceiling.** This is what makes the ceiling a ceiling rather than a target. **Revision 7: a clamp that bites before a match IS the ceiling stop.** The record is `unevaluated:cost-ceiling issue=#<n>`, never gated, and nothing is admitted after it, trusted or otherwise (§1.4 rule 4(a)–(c)) |
| **`--max-api-requests 0`** | Legal, and it means *"evaluate only the free path"*: Step C still runs (its pages are requests, so in practice a ceiling of 0 fails at Step C and the gate reports `complete=no` with an empty set). **It is a coherent if useless cost position and it is not rejected**, unlike `--max-candidates 0`, which is incoherent and is clamped to 1 |
| **What §6.2 asserts** | `api_requests <= effective_ceiling` **in every fixture in the file**, as a property-style assertion rather than a single test — the form that catches an off-by-one wherever it is reintroduced |

**On pagination — the ruling, its price, and the fact that the premise has EXPIRED (AF-8, corrected in revision 5).** Step C fetched a single unpaginated page in revision 3, preserved as R-6 on the stated grounds that it *"preserves today's documented 100-issue single-page ceiling exactly."* **AF-8 showed that ceiling was three records from binding and moving toward the oldest issues monotonically. It is no longer approaching: it has been crossed.**

> **The correction that revision 5 must carry, and must not soften into the amendment's present tense.** `ADR-1540-AMENDMENT-4` §0.1 Correction 1 states that widening the list bound *"displaces nothing today"*, that RP-2's interaction has *"at this moment, zero effect"*, and that the defect *"arms the moment the population exceeds the ceiling, **and the population is two records away**."* **The candidate population is 101** (worker, measured 2026-09-16T~15:55Z through the gate's own Step C query — §0.0.4), against 98 (`pm-agent`/panel, 2026-09-16) and 97 (architect, 2026-09-15). **101 > 100. It is not two records away; it arrived.** *(Revision 7, RP4-6(j): true of the 100-record LIST PAGE, which is a records-to-records comparison. False of the 100-REQUEST ceiling at 101 records; see the correction paragraph above. The last sentence of this block is the false half.)* **A single unpaginated page would truncate this repo's own candidate set today.** This strengthens AM-29 and AM-22 rather than weakening either — both were ruled in anticipation of exactly this state — but **every "zero effect today" framing is struck from this document**, and the request ceiling now binds on a fully-gated walk on this repository, today, rather than within weeks.

The prior measurements are retained for their *trend*, which is the part that generalises: **97 non-PR records on a 100-record page with #1539 at position 88** (architect 2026-09-15; re-derived by me 2026-09-16 — §0.0.3), **96 at position 86** (panel, 2026-09-14), **98** (`pm-agent`, 2026-09-16), **101** (worker, 2026-09-16). A `created desc` list moves old issues toward the truncation edge with every new filing, and #1539 has now passed the point where a single page would have held it. **A P-1 fix that changed the cap and left the page would have fixed the instance and left the class** — the failure would return in weeks with an identical user-visible signature (*#1539 is authorized and never gets built*) but a different cause and an absent diagnostic. **Binding: Step C paginates, bounded at 5 pages / 500 records**, normalising concatenated page arrays with the `re.sub(r"\]\s*\[", ",", out)` helper §1.4 already specifies for events. The cost is **≤4 extra list requests per cycle** — negligible beside the 100 the ceiling already permits. **R-6 is closed (§7.2), not carried.**

**The division of labour between the three, stated so the next reader does not "simplify" one into another:** **pagination buys *ordering completeness*** — it is what lets a `priority:critical` record filed 150 issues ago be ranked at all; **the cost ceiling buys *cost containment***; **and the ranking is what makes the ceiling safe.** Dropping pagination re-opens AF-8. Dropping the ranking re-opens P-1. Raising the ceiling above the population buys nothing and costs rate limit. They are one design, in the same way AM-18's skip rule and AM-4's title derivation are one construction, and the same warning applies.

**Why the list bound exits 0 — REPLACED in revision 5, and the replacement is the point (AM-31).** Revision 4 justified this row as an **exception granted under protest** to AM-21's enumerable-and-ordered criterion. **AM-31 replaces the criterion the row was failing, and the architect is explicit that this is the fix:** *"RP-5's objection — that the concession fails both limbs, not one — is answered by replacing the criterion it fails, not by strengthening the exit."* Under the per-harm criterion the row is **ordinary, not exceptional**: no truncated record can be wrongly *authorized*, some can be wrongly *omitted*, so the harm is **completeness**, and completeness-affecting bounds exit 0 and report. **The design must stop presenting this as a concession; it is the rule.** Two obligations survive the change of register, and both are binding: the report stays in the **loudest** class, because unordered truncation is still the worst kind the gate has; and the report **may not state a count the gate cannot derive** (AM-31) — *"at least 500 records matched; the total is unknown"*. The bound stays at **500 and is re-derived rather than re-asserted** (AM-33): the list bound is **cheap** (≤5 requests) while the request ceiling is **expensive** (≈100), so equalising them — the obvious "simplification" — would trade a nearly-free ordering-completeness guarantee for an expensive one and re-open AF-8.

**What this costs to run — RESTATED IN REQUESTS, and with the early exit priced separately from the worst case.**

| Case | Requests per cycle | Provenance |
|---|---|---|
| **Worst case (fully-gated walk, no usable candidate found)** | **≤100 by construction** — the ceiling is denominated in requests and clamps every fetch, including one already in flight | **A hard bound.** This is the ceiling's definition, not an estimate. **It replaces revision 4's "≈105", which was a floor described as a ceiling** (AM-30) |
| **Typical case after the early exit, at the present population** | **1–5 list requests + 1 events request per untrusted record walked before the first usable candidate** — as few as **2** when the head of the ranked queue is usable | **Derived, not measured.** It depends on how far down the ranked walk the first usable candidate sits, which nobody can measure before the gate exists |
| **Tier resolution (H3)** | **0** when the roster lists no tier — every deployment today, including this one. **1–3** when it does | **A hard bound** (`COLLABORATOR_PAGE_BOUND = 3`), one query per cycle for the whole gate, §1.3.2 |
| **At the documented five-minute cadence** | **≤1,200 requests/hour**, ≈24% of a 5,000/hour installation limit | **AM-30's derivation**, which I carry as ruled |

**Provenance, stated precisely because this chain has now logged five instances of a derived number laundered as a measured one.** The **≈100-request ceiling is a hard bound** in the unit that is actually rationed — that is the whole of AM-30's correction, and it is why revision 4's "≈105" is retired rather than re-cited. **What is NOT measured is the budget *share*:** AM-30 derives ≈24% from a rate limit and a cadence ~~and **does not account for the overseer's own cron consumption**~~ *(corrected in revision 7, RP4-6(h): run 3's M-5 showed the overseer is a separate App with its own bucket, so it never needed accounting for. The share is still a choice, not a measurement; §0.6 gap 9)*. **The 5,000/hour figure is also cited to the wrong site** (§0.6 gap 9): Amendment 4 attributes it to `docs/OVERSIGHT-RUNBOOK.md:56`, which is a crontab example line. The bucket is stated at `docs/specs/UNATTENDED-WORKER-PROTOCOL.md:448` **and** `docs/specs/UNATTENDED-WORKER-TECH-DESIGN.md:256` *("the only in-repo statement" corrected in revision 7, RP4-6(k))*. **I carry the bound as ruled and do not launder its derivation.** It is handed to the panel as §9/**P1-b**.

**The lever, named so nobody looks for it in the wrong place — and it is now load-bearing rather than precautionary (AM-30 point 5).** This cost is **FR7's** price, not the ceiling's: *"no state file, no memo"* means every cycle re-derives every authorization from live events. **The gate's cost is O(population) per cycle while the rate limit is fixed, so no choice of bounds is stable against a growing queue** — bounds relocate the problem, they do not resolve it. **The only lever that resolves it is FR7's "never cache an authorization", and that is `pm-agent`'s and the human's, not mine.** Amendment 4 §4 item 1 recommends it be filed as its own work item **now rather than at the rate limit**, and I second that. **Any future proposal to move the ceiling in either direction MUST cite this paragraph and state what it is trading.**

> **What the early exit does and does not do to that lever, because it is the obvious thing to get wrong.** The human's directive **reduces the pressure that motivates FR7's revisit without removing it**: it changes the *typical* cost from O(population) to O(distance to the first usable candidate), and leaves the *worst* case — every candidate externally authored and unauthorized — at exactly O(population), bounded by the ceiling. **A queue that is entirely gated still costs the full ceiling every cycle, and the post-merge drain state is exactly that queue.** So the early exit makes the ordinary week cheap and leaves the expensive weeks expensive. That is a real improvement and it is not a resolution, and §0.0.5's row on Amendment 4 §4 item 1 says so.

**The cost disclosure to the human is DISCHARGED, not pending.** Revision 4 routed the envelope to H1 item 2 as an open disclosure. **The human answered on 2026-09-16: *"the manual two-act labeling process, `/approve` going inert, and the ~105-request worst-case API cost are all accepted as designed."*** §7.3 carries the affected-clearance analysis — including the fact that they cleared the **≈105** figure that AM-30 then corrected, and why that clearance still holds rather than needing to be re-asked.

**Flags that deliberately do not exist (AD-13 — no knob may widen the trusted set or disable the gate):**

- **No `--repo-root`.** The repo root is derived from `Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]`. A `--repo-root` flag would let a caller point the gate at a directory containing a forged `.github/CODEOWNERS` or a populated `trusted-requesters.txt` — a complete bypass through an ergonomics flag. The *library* functions in `requester_trust.py` remain `repo_root`-parameterised (probe.py and the tests need that); the *entry point* is not.
- **No `--exclude-label`.** The exclusion set is the module constant `EXCLUDED_LABELS = ("needs-human",)`. A flag could remove the `needs-human` exclusion. **This constant is now the bound landing site for `ADR-1604` AD-5's queue exclusions (AM-9)** — the architect amended AD-5's *mechanism* bullet so the exclusion is expressed **once, here**, with no inlined twin in `worker-cron-prompt.md` and no lock-step divergence test, because after S2 there is no second implementation to diverge from. AD-5's *semantics* are unchanged and still binding. Note the scope precisely, so #1604's author is not misled: AD-5 makes one narrow marker authoritative for **both** the stuck-count and the queue exclusion; only the **exclusion** half lands here. The count half stays in #1604's stuck-set query. See §4.5.
- **No `--trusted`, `--allow`, `--skip-gate`, `--dry-run-trust` or any equivalent.** A test enumerates the parser's actions and asserts the flag set is exactly the **five** above (`--repo`, `--milestone`, `--label`, `--max-candidates`, **`--max-api-requests`**). **The figure changed from four to five in revision 4** because AM-19 adds `--max-candidates`; **revision 5 renames the fifth and does not add a sixth.** A coder who finds a stale "four", or a stale `--max-authorization-checks`, anywhere in this document should treat it as a defect in the document, not in the parser.
- **No `--trusted-tier`, `--collaborators`, `--resolve-tiers` or any equivalent — NEW in revision 5 (H3), and this is the flag a well-meaning implementer will want.** Tier membership is resolved from the **committed roster's policy** and the live collaborator API, and from nothing else. A flag naming a trusted tier would be AD-13's forbidden widening knob in its purest form: one command-line argument, no commit, no review, and every `write` collaborator becomes a trusted requester for that invocation. **The roster file is the only place a tier may be named**, and it is a protected surface precisely so that naming one requires a human-gated merge. A flag supplying the *collaborator payload* is equally forbidden on the entry point for the same reason `--repo-root` is: it would let a caller hand the gate a forged membership list. **The `collaborators` parameter on `load_trusted_set` (§1.3) is a library affordance for tests and for `probe.py`'s `None`, not a CLI surface**, and §6.2's anti-knob sweep asserts no such flag exists.
- **No `--max-list-pages`.** The 5-page / 500-record list bound (§2.2.1) is a module constant, not a flag, for the same reason `EXCLUDED_LABELS` is: a flag that lowers it would let a caller re-create AF-8's truncation on demand, silently, in the one region of the order that is *unordered*. Lowering the **cost ceiling** is safe because its truncation is ranked; lowering the **page bound** is not, and the asymmetry is the whole content of §2.2.1's table.
- **`--label` is permitted** because it cannot widen trust: it changes only *which records are queried*, and every returned record still passes the identical trust and authorization tests. It exists so the `needs-ai` → `needs-worker` rename (#1349) is a one-line change rather than a sixth hardcoded literal (FR26). If the label is renamed and the default is not updated, the query returns nothing → zero candidates → fail-closed (AD-4's stated rename-survivability property).
- **`--max-api-requests` may only lower.** Effective value is `min(100, max(0, provided))`. **The ceiling changed from 25 to 100 in revision 4 and its *meaning* changed with it** (AM-19); **in revision 5 its *unit* changes and its value does not** (AM-30). It is a **per-cycle API request budget**, not a coverage budget and not a count of checks. It does not decide *which* records are evaluated — the ranked walk decides that — only *how much of the rationed resource* the cycle may spend. A reviewer who reads either change as "the cap was raised" has both rulings backwards: **the cap's value was never the defect, and it has not moved since revision 4.**
- **`--max-candidates` may only lower.** Effective value is **`min(5, max(1, provided))`** — the upper clamp drops from 10 to 5 in revision 5 (`bin/hos-cron:1892-1895`'s own `head -5`), and the lower clamp is **1**, not 0, because an early exit at zero would emit an empty list on every cycle while reporting `complete=no`, which is a gate that does nothing while claiming to have worked. `--max-api-requests 0` remains legal and is a coherent if useless cost position; `--max-candidates 0` is not coherent and is rejected by the clamp. **Note what the lower clamp of 1 permits and what §2.2.1 warns about: `--max-candidates 1` is legal and is the literal reading of the human's directive, and it re-creates the head-of-line stall `worker-cron-prompt.md:96` is written against.** It is not forbidden — lowering a bound is always permitted — but it is not the default, and the reason is recorded where an operator setting it will find it.

**Environment.** The gate requires `gh` to be authenticated already (`GH_TOKEN` exported by the cron launcher, or the worker's ambient session auth). It **MUST NOT mint or revoke tokens** — putting credential handling on the selection path adds a failure mode and a secret-handling surface to a gate whose whole value is being boring and deterministic.

**GitHub access.** The gate shells out to `gh api` directly, with `subprocess.run`, following the precedent already set in the same directory by `require_human_approval.py:_fetch_reviews`. It does **not** import `scripts.automation.lib.github._run_gh`, which is functionally the right helper but sits on an unprotected surface (§1.1). Search performed before writing this: `scripts/`, `bootstrap/`, `bin/`, and `scripts/automation/lib/*.py` — found `scripts/automation/lib/github.py::_run_gh` (right behaviour, wrong trust direction), `bootstrap/query_issues.sh` (mints its own token per call, emits a summary line with no `.user` fields, so unusable here), and `require_human_approval.py::_fetch_reviews` (right directory, right pattern, review-specific). If a third `scripts/framework/**` consumer ever needs `gh api`, the helper should be promoted **into** `scripts/framework/`, never imported out of `scripts/automation/`.

### 2.3 Algorithm (ordered; the order is part of the contract)

**Step A — arguments.** `--repo` must match `^[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+/[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+$`. `--milestone` must parse as a positive integer. This second check is load-bearing: `bin/hos-cron` substitutes `@@MILESTONE_NUMBER@@` into the worker prompt **only when `HOS_TARGET_MILESTONE_NUMBER` is non-empty** (`bin/hos-cron:1808-1815`), so an unresolved milestone leaves the literal placeholder in the fallback command. `--milestone @@MILESTONE_NUMBER@@` must fail closed, not be coerced. Any violation → exit 2.

**Step B — configuration, all fail-closed.**

1. `bots = load_bot_accounts()` — empty → exit 2 **`bot-accounts-empty`**. (The house precedent: `require_human_approval.py` exits 2 on empty `BOT_ACCOUNTS`.)
2. `codeowners = codeowners_humans()` — empty → exit 2, **with the token chosen by WHY it is empty (AM-34, binding — new in revision 5)**. An absent, unreadable, or team-patterns-only CODEOWNERS means **no actor can ever authorize anything**, so running is pointless and silence would be dangerous. Note this is stricter than the shared loader, which returns an empty set; the primitive stays permissive-of-absence for `probe.py` compatibility and the *gate* enforces. **The three conditions no longer share one token** — see the distinct-token requirement below.
3. `apps = load_trusted_apps()` — empty → exit 2 **`trusted-apps-missing`** or **`trusted-apps-empty`**, distinguished the same way (AM-34 point 2).
4. `policy = load_trusted_requesters()` — **returns a `RosterPolicy`, not a set (§1.3, changed in revision 5).** Absent → empty policy, continue. A non-`FileNotFoundError` read error → exit 2 `roster-unreadable`.
5. **`collaborators = fetch_collaborators(repo) if policy.tiers else None` — NEW in revision 5 (H3, §1.3.2).** **Lazy and conditional: when the roster names no tier the fetch is not issued at all and costs zero requests**, which is every deployment today including this one. **Any failure yields `None`**, which yields `tier_members = frozenset()`; it sets the **degraded** condition (`complete=no`, `WARN tier-resolution-failed tiers=<n>`) and the walk **continues**. It **never** exits 2, and §2.2's reconciliation note explains why this is the only row in §1.3.1's table that does not propagate: a failed tier resolution can only ever **shrink** the trusted set, which puts *more* records through the authorization check, not fewer. It is completeness-affecting, so AM-31 routes it to exit 0 (or 3 if the gate ends with nothing to emit).
6. `trusted_set = load_trusted_set(collaborators=collaborators)` — composes the five categories. **`load_trusted_set` performs no network I/O** (§1.3): the caller fetched at step 5 and passes data in, so AD-1's no-network-in-the-predicate rule holds and the whole trust path stays testable with no stub.
7. Any other exception in Step B → exit 2 `config-error`.

**Distinct, self-describing tokens with a remediation line each (AM-34 point 1, binding — new in revision 5).** A consumer whose `.github/CODEOWNERS` contains only team handles (`@org/core-devs` — the common enterprise configuration) gets an empty `codeowners_humans` and exits 2 **forever**. **The halt is correct and it stays** — a gate that cannot possibly authorize anything should refuse to run rather than emit `eligible=0` every cycle while appearing to work. **What was defective is that the halt was mute and its token lied:** `codeowners-empty` reads as *"your CODEOWNERS file is broken"*, and for that consumer the file is fine — it simply names no individual. **Binding: each exit-2 condition below gets its own token and one line naming the act that resolves it. The strings are mine; that they are distinct and remediating is the ruling.**

| Condition | Token | Remediation line the gate MUST emit |
|---|---|---|
| `.github/CODEOWNERS` absent (F1) | `codeowners-file-absent` | *"create `.github/CODEOWNERS` naming at least one individual GitHub user who may authorize work"* |
| CODEOWNERS present and readable, but names **no individual human** — only `org/team` patterns (F2) | **`codeowners-no-individual-human`** | *"this file is valid but lists only team handles; team handles do not confer authorization authority. Add at least one individual user handle."* **This is the row AM-34 exists for: the consumer is told their configuration is unsupported, not that their install is broken** |
| CODEOWNERS present, unreadable (F3) | `codeowners-unreadable` | *"the file exists but could not be read; check permissions"* |
| `machine-accounts.env` absent (F4a) | **`machine-accounts-file-absent`** | *"this file is required by the work-selection gate, is not shipped by HOS and is not produced by the installer; create it with `BOT_WORKER_USERNAME`, `BOT_OVERSEER_USERNAME`, `BOT_HUMAN_USERNAME` and `COPILOT_BOT_LOGIN`"* |
| present but yielding no bot accounts after the env union (F4b) | `bot-accounts-empty` | *"no bot accounts resolved; the gate cannot distinguish a bot from a human and refuses to run"* |
| present but naming none of the three HOS App roles (F4c) | `trusted-apps-empty` | *"no HOS App identities resolved"* |

**These strings are not only for this repo.** AM-34 point 3 binds the **FR29(b) artefact** to carry both preconditions in prose as well — *(a)* that at least one **individual** human handle must appear in CODEOWNERS and that team handles confer no authorization authority, and *(b)* that `machine-accounts.env` must exist — so the consumer who cannot run the gate is told **why** and **what to do**, in the same document that tells them what the authorizing act is. §4.3 carries that obligation. **A token an operator can act on and a document that says what to do are two halves of one fix; shipping either alone is what revision 4 did.**

**Step C — the list query, PAGINATED (changed in revision 4 — AM-22 / AF-8).**

```
gh api --paginate "repos/<repo>/issues?state=open&milestone=<n>&labels=<label>&per_page=100"
```

Bounded to **5 pages / 500 records** (§2.2.1), normalising `gh --paginate`'s concatenated page arrays with the `re.sub(r"\]\s*\[", ",", out)` idiom §1.4 specifies for events — the same helper, for the same reason, and **not** a naive `replace("][", ",")`, whose failure mode `require_human_approval.py`'s own comment records.

Non-zero exit, non-JSON output, or a non-list result → exit 2 `list-query-failed` (F12, unchanged).

**Exhausting the 5-page bound is NOT an error, and revision 5 changes BOTH how it is justified and what it may say.** It is the unordered-truncation case of §2.2.1: the walk proceeds on the 500 records it has, sets `complete=no`, and ~~counts the unreached region under `unevaluated:list-truncated`~~ **sets the flag `unevaluated:list-truncated=yes`** *(revision 7, TP-3: the unfetched region is outside `N`, its size cannot be derived, and it is not summed into `U`; §1.2 consequence 6)*. Under **AM-31** it exits 0 **on a stated ground rather than under protest** — no truncated record can be wrongly *authorized*, so the harm is completeness, and completeness-affecting bounds report and continue.

> **Binding, and it is the limb most likely to be implemented wrong (AM-31): the gate MUST NOT report a truncation count it cannot derive.** GitHub's `/issues` endpoint returns **no total**. The gate therefore emits
>
> ```
> select_work_candidates: WARN candidate-list-truncated pages=5 — at least 500 records matched; the total is UNKNOWN
> ```
>
> **and never a `records=<N>` figure computed by assuming the next page is full.** Revision 4's `WARN candidate-list-truncated pages=5 records=500` is exactly that defect in miniature: `500` is the number the gate *fetched*, presented in the position where a reader expects the number that *matched*. A `Link: rel="last"` probe could bound the true total; it is **permitted and explicitly not binding** (AM-31), and it must not become a reason to delay.

**Reaching this bound is an operational emergency, not a residual** — the report stays in the loudest class for that reason. Revision 3 preserved a single unpaginated page as R-6 on the grounds that it *"preserves today's documented 100-issue single-page ceiling exactly"*; **R-6 is CLOSED (§7.2)**, and §2.2.1 records that the ceiling it preserved **has now actually been crossed** (101 records, §0.0.4) rather than merely being approached.

*Traceability (AR-5):* Step C implements **FR8** (the gate must make an unapproved untrusted issue ineligible **at every path that selects work** — this is the only path after §4.1's deletion) and **FR26** (the `--label` flag is what makes the `needs-ai` → `needs-worker` rename a one-line change; AR-6 marks FR26 *structurally yes* for S2).

**Step D — per record. Free filters first, then RANK, then pay (restructured in revision 4 — AM-19).**

> **The one-sentence statement of the ruling, carried here in the architect's terms because the sentence *is* the design:** *rank every candidate before spending a single authorization check, walk the ranked list, stop when enough eligible candidates have been found or when the cost ceiling is reached, and never admit a candidate found after the stop — so that whatever the gate emits is always an exact prefix of the answer a complete walk would have produced.*

> **REVISION 6 — the final clause of AM-19's sentence is SUPERSEDED IN ITS OBJECT and is quoted unedited (AM-38).** *"Whatever the gate emits is an exact prefix"* was written when the walk order and the emission order were the same thing; **AM-29 separated them and the clause did not survive the separation** (TP-4). **Read it as: whatever the gate ADMITS is always an exact prefix of the answer a complete walk would have produced — and what it EMITS is that admitted set, re-sorted by Step E.** **The rest of the sentence — rank first, walk the ranked list, stop, never admit after the stop — is untouched and is still the design.**
>
> **REVISION 7 qualifies the revision-6 reading, because it is false under two mechanisms revision 5 introduced (RUN4 RP4-1).** "An exact prefix of the answer a complete walk would have produced" fails when a record's events query fails (quarantine, AM-31) and when the budget clamp cut a fetch short. **Read it as: whatever the gate admits is the first `j` records the walk DETERMINED eligible, in walk order. A complete walk under the same per-record query outcomes admits the same `j` first.** Every record stepped over without a determination is named and forces `complete=no`. The clamp half no longer arises, because a clamp that bites is now the stop (§1.4 rule 4). §2.3 D5.1 carries the restatement.

| Step | Rule | Cost | Implements (AR-5) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **D1** | `record.get("pull_request")` present → **skip** | free | DEV-1, §7.1; **approved by AM-12 subject to §6's binding added test.** Scope boundary of **AD-14** — the *issue* gate must not adjudicate PR eligibility |
| **D2** | any label name in `EXCLUDED_LABELS` → skip | free | The one landing site (AM-9 / §2.2); `ADR-1604` **AD-5**'s exclusion half. **This is also the human's 15:46:50Z third directive — *"exclude anything already carrying `needs-human` … free, since label data is already in the same list response"* — and it is already implemented. Measured effect today: ZERO records removed** (§0.0.4). Keep it as a free correctness guard; **do not credit it with any cost saving** |
| **D3** | `verdict = requester_verdict(record, trusted_set)` — the **trust** determination | free | **FR1, FR2, FR4 (as amended by AR-3 and now RATIFIED BY THE HUMAN AT Q10), FR5.** FR4-as-amended governs *here* and only here: trust is a function of the issue **author** as reported by the API, and no metadata act by any actor confers it. **Q10's ratification means this is no longer provisional** — see below |
| **D4** | `verdict.trusted` → **eligible**, `authorization = "requester-trusted:" + verdict.reason`, **zero extra API calls**. *Revision 7 (RUN4 §3 item 6, noted for this revision): D3/D4 compute the verdict, and that may be done for the whole set before D-rank because it is free. A D4-trusted record is **ADMITTED only when the walk reaches it in walk order**, and never after a stop (D5.1, limb (d)). "D4 before D-rank" is the order of computing verdicts, not of admitting records* | free | **FR1, FR2, FR3** (roster, **including H3's tier entries via `tier_members`**), **AD-2** (the marker narrowing). **D3+D4 ARE the human's "already-trusted identity needs zero further verification" fast path** — see the narrow-reading note below and §8/E-10 |
| **D-rank** | **It precedes D5 and must never be moved below it.** Rank the **whole** candidate set: `priority:critical`→0, `priority:high`→1, `priority:medium`→2, else 3. **Then order by the WALK key `(rank, number DESC)` — which is NOT the emission key** (AM-29 as amended by the human's TP-1 ruling; **key CHANGED in revision 6**) | free | **AM-19 + AM-29 + the human's TP-1 ruling.** Ranking is free: `priority:*` is a label, labels are in the Step C payload, and the rank function reads nothing else. **The walk key is free for a stronger reason than revision 5's: `number` is on every record by construction, so the key needs no measurement to be available and no measurement to be total.** ~~`updated_at` … so AM-29's primary binding applies~~ — **withdrawn in revision 6: AM-29's stated `(rank, number DESC)` fallback IS taken, on security grounds (the `updated_at` key is advanced by any account posting a comment — TP-1), not on availability grounds (§0.0.4 settlement 1, §0.6 gap 10)** |
| **D5** | Walk the ranked list. An untrusted record costs **one events query (1–10 requests)** — the **authorization** determination (below). **The walk STOPS at the first `--max-candidates` usable candidates** (the human's directive) | **paid** | **FR7** (never cached), **FR8**, **FR16**, **FR28.** Per AR-4, **D5 does not narrow** under the FR4 ruling: it is the mechanism #1539's 2026-09-10 human ruling item 1 names, word for word |

**Q10 is answered, and D3 stops being provisional (the human, #1540 2026-09-16T07:14:38Z).** *"**Yes.** FR4 narrows to match #1539's already-ruled mechanism (trust determined by who performed the authorizing action, not by the issue's broader origin). If a broader origin-trust model is ever actually needed, address that if/when the need materializes — not built speculatively now."* Three consequences, and the third is the one that matters to a coder. (i) **`pm-agent`'s AR-3 narrowing is ratified**, so the "a no restarts the design chain from the ADR" branch is closed. (ii) **AR-4 still governs the division of labour**: FR4 lives at D3 and **D5 does not narrow**. (iii) **The human explicitly declined to build a broader origin-trust model speculatively**, which forecloses a whole class of "while we are here" widenings — a future proposal to make trust a function of anything other than `issue.user` as the API reports it is a **requirements** change and goes back to `pm-agent`, not a design refinement.

> **THE TRUSTED-AUTHOR FAST PATH: WHICH READING IS IMPLEMENTED, AND WHY (the human's 15:46:50Z directive 2; §8/E-10; §0.0.4 settlement 4).**
>
> The human's directive reads: *"Any candidate authored by an already-trusted identity needs **zero further verification** — skip the per-issue events-lookup entirely for those."* **D3+D4 already are that fast path** — a trusted-authored record is eligible at zero extra API cost and always has been. **The whole question is what "already-trusted" denotes**, and the two available readings differ by 97 records out of 101.
>
> **BUILT: the narrow reading. "Already-trusted" means what `requester_verdict` already means** — a CODEOWNER (AD-1(a)), an entry in the requester-trust roster including an H3 tier match (AD-1(c)), or one of the three HOS App identities **whose title also carries an enumerated machine-filing marker** (AD-1(b) as narrowed by AD-2). **Measured effect today: the fast path skips 4 of 101 records** — `ScottThurlow`'s own filings — because the roster ships empty and none of the 97 bot-authored records carries a marker.
>
> **NOT BUILT: the literal reading, in which the three HOS App identities are themselves "already-trusted".** Under it the gate would perform **zero verification on 97 of 101 records — it would be inert across effectively this repository's entire present queue** — and it would exempt the exact block that **AM-1 / H1 item 2 states must be authorized**: *"which, with option C ruled (AM-1), includes the human-proxy App's own filings"*, which is 49 of the 101. **That is not an optimisation of the control; it is the removal of the control**, and it would land inside an efficiency directive where the consequence is easy not to see.
>
> **The human was asked to confirm the narrow reading and has NOT answered.** The question was put on #1540 at 2026-09-16T15:53:35Z with the measurements attached, and **the comment thread ends there.** **Revision 5 therefore proceeds on an unanswered question, deliberately and on the record**, exactly as the worker's comment said it would: *"What is being built in the meantime, so revision 5 is not blocked on you: the narrow reading."* **The safe reading is the narrow one because it fails closed** — it puts *more* records through the authorization check, and a "yes, the bots are trusted" answer later is a configuration change (roster entries), not a redesign. **§8/E-10 carries this as a notification with the numbers attached; it is not a gate, and it asks the architect nothing.**
>
> **One thing this must not be read as doing: it does not make D3/D4 a *new* mechanism.** No code changes shape under the narrow reading, because the narrow reading **is** the existing D3/D4. What revision 5 adds is the statement of which reading is implemented, which nobody had written down.

**Why the cap's *value* was never the defect, and why D6 became D-rank.** Revision 3 spent a paid operation (D5) before a free ordering operation (D6). That is the error, and it is visible only once the two are put side by side. Measured against the live queue by me in revision 4: **3 `priority:critical` records among 97** (#1678, #1643, #1539) — so under a ranked walk **#1539 is among the first three records evaluated**, inside a ceiling of 25, inside a ceiling of 5, inside any ceiling a cost model would plausibly choose, **and inside an early exit at 5**. **It was evaluated at position 88 of 97 under revision 3 as written.** The panel's "not fixable by raising the cap" is correct, and its converse is the interesting half: **once the order is right, the budget almost stops mattering** — which is the strongest available evidence that order, not budget, was the defect, and which is also why the human's early exit is safe to add on top of it.

> **Revision 5 corrects one sentence of the paragraph above rather than rewriting it, because the correction is AM-29's whole subject.** Revision 4 said *"the walk's key is `(rank, number)` ascending — so **#1539 is the FIRST record evaluated**"*. **That was a claim about the walk made with the emission key**, and AM-29 is the ruling that the two are different decisions. Under the walk key the three criticals are still the first three records walked — **rank is the first key and dominates absolutely** — but **which of the three is first is not what revision 4's sentence said it was.** The *safety* claim (a critical record is reached before any non-critical one) is unchanged and is the only claim the design rests on; the *identity* claim (#1539 specifically is first) was never load-bearing and is withdrawn. **The population figure in that measurement is also superseded: 97 → 101** (§0.0.4).
>
> **REVISION 6 CORRECTS THE CORRECTION, and the second version is both simpler and checkable.** Revision 5 wrote that *"which of the three is first now depends on recency, not on issue number, and it is not fixed."* **Under the binding walk key `(rank, number DESC)` it is fixed, and it is computable from the numbers alone: the three criticals walk `#1678`, `#1643`, `#1539`.** Two consequences worth stating because a reader will check them. **(a) #1539 is now LAST of the three rather than first** — it was first under revision 4's `(rank, number ASC)` mistake and indeterminate under revision 5's key. **That is not a safety regression:** all three sit inside the first three records evaluated, inside any ceiling ≥ 3, and inside the early exit at 5, which is the only property the design rests on. **(b) The emission order is unaffected** — Step E re-sorts to `(rank, number ASC)`, so if all three were eligible stdout would read `#1539`, `#1643`, `#1678`. **This is the smallest available worked example of why the two orders must not be fused**, and it is exactly the shape §6.2's emission-order test pins.

**Why ranking alone is not enough.** With a budget spent in rank order, the gate evaluates the criticals first and then works down. If every evaluated record is gated and the human has authorized one beyond the budget, the gate reports `eligible=0`, emits `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED`, and the authorized issue is never built. **That is P-1 moved, not removed** — a smaller region, the same permanent unselectability.

> **Revision 4 answered that by requiring the ceiling not to bind in normal operation. AM-30 WITHDRAWS that requirement as unsatisfiable, and the replacement is the honest one.** *"At a population of 98, ≈95–103 requests per cycle, the ceiling is at its boundary today, and the population grows monotonically while the rate limit does not"* — and **§0.0.4 measures the population at 101, so it is over the boundary rather than at it.** The replacement requirement: **the ceiling WILL bind in normal operation, and what the design must prove is that the gate is CORRECT when it binds.** That proof is AM-29's and AM-33's: what the budget drops is the **ranked tail**, nothing ~~omitted~~ *the stop left unreached* can outrank anything ~~emitted~~ *admitted* (*revision 7, RP4-1: quarantine holes are the named exception; a budget clamp that bites is the stop itself*), and the gate **says so** (AM-20) — `unevaluated:cost-ceiling`, `complete=no`, and an `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED INCOMPLETE` line when the held-everything block also fires. **Two consequences travel with the withdrawal.** The routine ceiling report **leaves the `WARN` class** (AM-30 point 3), because a warning that fires every cycle is a banner. And **§8/E-8 is CLOSED by this ruling** — E-8 was precisely the observation that AM-19's derivation and AM-19's own no-bind requirement could not both survive AM-22, and AM-30 resolves it by repairing the unit and withdrawing the requirement rather than by re-deriving the number (§8).

> **What the early exit changes about this, and it is a genuine improvement rather than a restatement.** With the walk stopping at the first `--max-candidates` usable candidates, **the budget is reached only on a walk that found nothing usable** — which is the fully-gated drain state, not the ordinary week. The ceiling is now the **worst-case** backstop rather than the routine terminator, and the routine terminator is the early exit, which reports `unevaluated:sufficient` and is quiet by design. **AM-30 point 3's premise is therefore more true after the human's directive, not less** (§0.0.5).

**D5 — live actor-derived authorization.** For each untrusted-authored record, **in walk order** (`(rank, number DESC)` — AM-29's stated fallback, **binding as of revision 6** on the human's TP-1 ruling):

> **RENUMBERED IN REVISION 5 (RP-17, binding), and a reader following an older cross-reference needs the map.** Revision 4 carried **item 2 twice, verbatim** — a duplicated paragraph nobody had noticed through two panel runs — and every `D5.n` citation downstream of it was off by one against one of the two copies. **AM-35 then deleted the subject of item 2 entirely** (the milestone-object skip). The new numbering is **three items, not five**:
>
> | Revision 4 | Revision 5 | Disposition |
> |---|---|---|
> | D5.1 — the two stops | **D5.1 — the two stops** | Retained, with the sufficiency stop promoted to the early exit and the ceiling re-denominated |
> | D5.2 — milestone-object skip (**stated twice**) | — | **DELETED (AM-35).** The duplicate goes with the original; **row F17 is retired with it** |
> | D5.3 — fetch events | **D5.2 — fetch events** | Retained, budget-clamped |
> | D5.4 — `verify_codeowner_actor(…, expected_milestone_title=…)` | **D5.3 — the determination** | Merged with old D5.5; **the parameter is deleted** (AM-35) |
> | D5.5 — actor `None` → skip, else eligible | **D5.3** | Merged. **`no-codeowner-actor` is therefore a D5.3 token**, which is what §1.2 and §1.4 cite |

**D5.1 — the two stops.**

- **The early exit (the human's 15:46:50Z directive 1; formerly the sufficiency stop).** When the eligible set reaches `--max-candidates` (**default 5**), **stop the walk.** Report `unevaluated:sufficient`. **Routine, expected, and QUIET: it emits no `WARN`** — it is the normal termination of a healthy walk, and a warning on it would be a banner. §2.2.1 states why the stop is at five rather than at one.
- **The per-cycle request ceiling (AM-30).** Before issuing any GitHub API request, require `api_requests < effective_ceiling` (**default 100 requests**). If not, **do not issue it**, **stop the walk**, and report `unevaluated:cost-ceiling`. **Revision 7 (TP-2 / RP4-1(i)): a budget clamp that bites mid-fetch is this same stop, reached one request later.** The clamped record is `unevaluated:cost-ceiling issue=#<n>`, never gated (§1.4 rule 4). **No `WARN` — dropped from the WARN class in revision 5** (AM-30 point 3), because AM-30 withdraws the premise that reaching it is exceptional and §0.0.4 shows the population has already crossed it. The ceiling means the gate's answer was bounded by **budget** rather than by the **queue**; that is now a routine fact carried in two fields rather than an alarm.
- **On either stop, the walk ends COMPLETELY. No further record is admitted — trusted or otherwise.** This is the part revision 3 got wrong in a way that only a ranked walk exposes: revision 3's D5.1 skipped *"this and every remaining **untrusted** record"* and kept admitting trusted ones. Under a ranked walk that would emit a trusted record at walk position 90 while an authorized record at position 30 was never evaluated. **Under the early exit this rule matters MORE, not less, because the stop now fires on nearly every cycle** — a stop that keeps admitting cheap records is a stop that silently reorders the output on the most common path in the design.
- **Neither stop is an error, and neither is a security event.** Under **AM-31** both are **completeness-affecting**: they change *how much* of the queue the gate looked at, never *whether any record was treated as authorized*. Exiting 2 on a budget hit would mean anyone who can get N untrusted-authored issues into the milestone denies the worker **all** work, including trusted work — a cost control turned into a denial-of-service. **Exit 0, or exit 3 when the stop leaves nothing to emit** (§2.2).

   > **The property that makes a partial answer safe — RESTATED WITH ITS SCOPE, because revision 4 claimed it over the repository and it holds only over the fetched set (AM-33, binding).**
   >
   > **THE CLAIM AS REVISION 7 STATES IT. THIS GOVERNS; the revision-6 wording below is retained as the record and is superseded where it differs (RUN4 RP4-1(ii)).** Revision 6's ADMISSION property was false under two mechanisms revision 5 introduced. Under **D5.3's quarantine-and-continue** (AM-31), a record whose events query fails is stepped over and a lower record is admitted. Under **§1.4 rule 4's budget clamp**, a record was recorded as `gated` without a determination. RUN4's counterexamples: `#30/#20/#10` same-rank with `#30`'s query failing at `--max-candidates 1`, and the same three at `--max-api-requests 2` with `#30`'s authorizing event on events page 2. **The clamp half is fixed at its cause** (§1.4 rule 4: a bite is the stop, so no record is stepped over). **The quarantine half is fixed by saying what the property is about.** AM-31's behaviour is not changed. Halting the walk on quarantine would re-open AM-31, which is an architecture question, so it is not done. Let **`W`** be the sequence of records the ranked walk over **the candidate set Step C returned** *determines eligible*, in walk order, **when no stop fires, under the same per-record query outcomes**. Then, for any stop:
   >
   > 1. **ADMISSION, over determinations.** *The SET of candidates the walk ADMITTED equals the first `j` elements of `W`, where `j` is the number admitted.* Within one run the statement is: **the walk admits every record it DETERMINES eligible, in walk order, until it stops, and nothing after.** **A record the walk reached without a determination is not in `W`.** Its events query failed, it is named on its own `unevaluated:query-failed issue=#<n>` line, and it forces `complete=no`. **A record the budget clamp cut short is never stepped over, because it is where the walk stops** (§1.4 rule 4(c)).
   > 2. **Relation to the complete answer: AM-33's words are retained, with how they are read stated at §8/E-12.** *It equals the first `j` eligible records of the complete answer if and only if `complete=yes`.* The operative content is limb (e): **the gate asserts equality with the complete answer only when `complete=yes`, and asserts nothing global otherwise.** A quarantine, a list truncation and a degraded tier resolution each force `complete=no`. Under any of them a determination may differ from the one a healthy run would make.
   > 3. **Presentation.** stdout is the admitted set re-sorted by Step E into `(rank, number ASC)`. **It is NOT a prefix of anything and must not be described as one** (TP-4, unchanged).
   >
   > **The claim, in the words AM-33 requires — RESTATED IN REVISION 6 ~~on the human's TP-4 ruling~~ *(as a correction under the human's TP-4 classification, on the panel's recommended limb; RP4-8)*, because AM-29's own separation of the two orders made the revision-5 wording arithmetically false:** *for any stop point `k`, the **SET of candidates the walk ADMITTED** equals the first `k` eligible elements of the ranked walk over **the candidate set Step C returned** — and it equals the first `k` of the complete answer **if and only if `complete=yes`**. **stdout is that admitted SET, re-sorted by Step E into the emission order `(rank, number ASC)`, and is therefore NOT a prefix of the walk and must not be described as one.*** **An unqualified claim of prefix-correctness is a claim the gate cannot make and must stop making — and after revision 6 that includes the unqualified word "prefix" applied to stdout.**
   >
   > > **WHY THE REVISION-5 WORDING WAS FALSE, carried in the panel's own arithmetic rather than paraphrased, because this is the finding that returned DO-NOT-BUILD and the next reader must be able to check it.** **TP-4:** *"Taking the top-k under order A and re-sorting that subset by order B does not, in general, yield a prefix of the full set under either order. That is arithmetic, not judgement."* Worked, on three same-rank records under the **binding revision-6 keys** (the panel's own worked example used the revision-5 key and comes out the same way, which is the point — **TP-1's ruling does NOT dissolve TP-4**): walk `(rank, number DESC)` gives `[#30, #20, #10]`; the unbounded run admits all three and Step E emits `[#10, #20, #30]` — **the exact reverse of the walk, not a prefix of it**; a bounded run stopping at 1 admits `{#30}` and emits `[#30]`, which is **not** a prefix of `[#10, #20, #30]`. **The ADMISSION claim holds in every one of those cases** — `{#30}` is exactly the first 1 of the walk — **which is why the property is scoped to the walk rather than weakened.**
   > >
   > > **What is NOT weakened, stated because "we rescoped the property" is how a safety claim quietly shrinks.** **Rank dominance is a property of the SET, not of the print order**, so re-sorting cannot touch it: nothing omitted outranks anything emitted, before or after Step E, because Step E permutes a set it does not change. **Input completeness is a property of Step C**, likewise untouched. **The two halves AM-33 named are intact verbatim; the only thing that moved is which object the word "prefix" attaches to.**
   > >
   > > **REVISION 7: the paragraph above was half right, and the wrong half was the one revision 6 leaned on (RP4-1).** Step E cannot touch rank dominance; that stands. But *"nothing omitted outranks anything emitted"* was never a property of the SET the walk produced. **The walk can produce a set with a hole in it.** RUN4's counterexample 3: `#1678` (critical, authorized) has its events query fail, so it is quarantined and the walk continues. `#1600` (medium, authorized) is admitted and emitted. An omitted record outranks an emitted one. **The stop did not do that; the quarantine did.** The halves are restated below over determinations. Revision 6's *"This half is unconditional"* is withdrawn.
   >
   > **Why the qualification is not a technicality.** The adversarial lens reconstructed AM-19's four-limb proof and could not break it — a real result, not re-derived here. **RP-6 is not an attack on the proof; it is the observation that the proof's INPUT is a truncated set.** Step C truncates by `created desc` **before** ranking, so an omitted record **can** outrank an emitted one, and a four-limb test suite stays green while that happens.
   >
   > **The two halves have different owners and different failure modes, and AM-33 requires them to be named separately:**
   > - **Rank dominance** — ~~*nothing omitted by the walk outranks anything emitted.*~~ **RESTATED IN REVISION 7, over determinations (RP4-1(ii)):** ***no record the walk left UNREACHED outranks any record it admitted.*** This includes the at-most-one record a budget clamp cut short, which is where the walk stopped. ***A record the walk REACHED but could not determine (quarantined, `unevaluated:query-failed`) MAY outrank an admitted record. When one does, it is named on its own line and `complete=no`.*** A **safety** property of the walk. It is preserved by **any** order of the form `(rank, X)`, which is exactly what lets AM-29 change the tie-break and what lets the early exit stop at any position. ~~**This half is unconditional.**~~ **This half is unconditional over records the walk determined or left unreached. Its one exception, the quarantine hole, is observable (`query-failed=<z>`, `z > 0`) and named per record.** *Why not RUN4's literal wording, "nothing the walk DETERMINED and did not admit outranks anything admitted": a GATED record is determined and not admitted, and it can rank above an admitted one by design (a gated critical above an admitted high). Gated records are determinations, not omissions, and are outside this half. The restatement says what RUN4's remedy means.*
   > - **Input completeness** — *the walk saw every record there is.* A property of **Step C**, **not** preserved when the list bound binds, and reported as `complete=<yes|no>`. **This half is conditional, and the gate knows which it has.**
   >
   > **The old F15 error is retained as the lesson rather than deleted:** F15 accepted the cap as safe because *"a partial answer is a subset of the eligible set."* **A subset is NOT sufficient; a PREFIX is** — and revision 5 adds the second half: **a prefix of WHAT.** Revision 3 reasoned about subsets when the hazard was order; revision 4 reasoned about order when half the hazard was input. **A design that keeps answering the previous version of the question is the pattern here, and §9 hands it to the panel in that form.**

   **The five limbs, as separately checkable claims** — four from AM-19, the fifth from AM-33: (a) the rank function reads only the Step C payload, so a record's rank cannot change between D-rank and emit; (b) the walk key **`(rank, number DESC)`** is a **total** order on the candidate set — **and in revision 6 that is a structural fact rather than a measured one: issue numbers are unique within a repository by construction, so the key is total on its own with no tie-break at all and no dependence on the present population**. *(Revision 5's key was total only because §0.0.4 happened to measure 101/101 `updated_at` values distinct on one day — a property of the population, not of the key. **Losing that dependence is the one unambiguous technical gain from the human's TP-1 ruling**, and it is recorded as such rather than as consolation.)*; (c) D1/D2's free filters are applied **before** ranking, so the ranked set and the eligible universe are drawn from the same population; (d) no record is admitted after a stop, **and in revision 7 a budget clamp that bites is a stop** (§1.4 rule 4(c)); and **(e) the claim is scoped to the fetched set, and the gate asserts nothing global when `complete=no`.** **Each is a separate test in §6.2, and limb (e)'s is an ABSENCE test** — a single test over a conjunction reports "prefix-correctness broke" and leaves the next reader to find out which limb.

**D5.2 — fetch the events.** `gh api --paginate "repos/<repo>/issues/<n>/events?per_page=100"`, bounded to **10 pages / 1000 events** (AM-5, confirmed unchanged by AM-30), normalising concatenated page arrays per §1.4, **and additionally clamped to `min(EVENTS_PAGE_BOUND, effective_ceiling - api_requests)` pages** so a fetch already in flight can never carry the request counter past the ceiling (§1.4 rule 4, §2.2.1).

**Exhausting the page bound without a match is *not* an error**; it is "not authorized" → gate that candidate, **and it MUST emit `WARN events-page-bound-reached issue=#<n>` to stderr** (AM-5). That is a **determination** about one record (it counts toward `gated`), it truncates nothing in the walk's order, and every other candidate is unaffected. A stall nobody can see is R-5 becoming permanent. ~~**If the budget clamp is what bit rather than the page bound, the outcome is identical and a second line fires: `WARN budget-clamped-events-fetch issue=#<n>`** — the two are indistinguishable to the record and completely different to an operator.~~ **REPLACED IN REVISION 7 (run 3's TP-2; RUN4 RP4-1(i)): if the budget clamp bit rather than the page bound, the outcome is NOT identical.** The page bound is a determination: the gate looked at 1,000 events and found no qualifying actor. A clamp bite is the absence of one: the gate stopped looking because the cycle's budget ran out. So the clamped record is **`unevaluated:cost-ceiling issue=#<n>`, never gated**. `WARN budget-clamped-events-fetch issue=#<n>` fires, `WARN events-page-bound-reached` does **not**, and **the walk stops there**, admitting nothing further, trusted or otherwise. "When the clamp bites" is defined exactly at §1.4 rule 4(a), including the rule that an ambiguous last page counts as a bite. Revision 6's *"indistinguishable to the record"* was the defect. The gate's verdict on the record depended on how much budget remained when the walk reached it, not on the repository's state.

**D5.3 — the determination, and the one rule that CHANGES in revision 5 (AM-31, which REPLACES AM-21).**

1. **Transport error, non-zero exit, or unparseable output → QUARANTINE THE RECORD AND CONTINUE THE WALK.** Count it under **`unevaluated:query-failed`**, report it **with its issue number**, **never** count it as `gated`, and move to the next record in walk order.

   > **This reverses revision 4, which exited 2 here, and the reversal is the architect's with the reasoning attached.** *"The determination is per-record: one query per record, no shared state, and its failure direction is not authorized. A failed events query on record X cannot corrupt record Y's determination, and it cannot authorize X. So exit 2 there buys **zero** security and costs **all** new issue work."* **`probe.py:298-303` already implements exactly this** (`try/except GitHubError: return None`) — **the gate's exit 2 was the novel behaviour, not the quarantine.**
   >
   > **What AM-21's exit 2 was actually protecting, named so it is not lost:** *"a partial list can silently demote a `priority:critical` item"* is an **ordering** concern, not a security one, and AM-21 paid for it with a total stop. **Under AM-20's vocabulary the demotion is no longer silent** — `unevaluated:query-failed issue=#<n>` names the record and `complete=no` says the answer is partial. **The concern is real and it is now met by reporting instead of by halting.**
   >
   > **Read in the direction a reviewer will suspect.** An attacker who can make one record's events query fail causes that record to be **not selected** — fail-closed, for that record only. An attacker who can make *every* record's query fail causes `eligible=0, complete=no`, **exit 3**, loudly reported, with the cycle proceeding to its other work. **There is no input to this path that produces an authorization.** What changes is that ordinary transience stops silently deleting a cycle's work selection.
   >
   > **`unevaluated:query-failed` is the one unevaluated reason that is an ANOMALY rather than a routine** (§1.2): it keeps the loudest report class, and it is **per-record**, so it MUST carry the issue number. The other three describe why the walk stopped or was short; this one describes one named record the walk stepped over.

2. `actor = verify_codeowner_actor(events, codeowners, bots, label)` — **four arguments. `expected_milestone_title` is DELETED (AR-7 / AM-35), not passed as `None`.** Under AR-7 there is exactly one authorizing signal: *a `labeled` event, for the dispatch label being queried, whose GitHub-reported actor is a verified individual human CODEOWNER.* **Revision 4 passed `record["milestone"]["title"]` here and required it to be non-`None`; that argument no longer exists and a coder who finds it in any call site has found a deletion that was missed** — which is exactly what §9's third-run item 5 is instructed to look for.
3. `actor is None` → **gated**, reason token `no-codeowner-actor`. **This is a determination** (the gate looked and found no qualifying actor), so it counts toward `gated`, never toward `unevaluated`. Otherwise **eligible**, `authorization = "codeowner-actor:" + actor`.

**What D5 no longer does, stated as an absence because absences are what get silently re-added.** It does not read `record["milestone"]` for any purpose. It does not skip a record for lacking a milestone object. It does not derive an expected title. It does not emit `WARN milestone-object-absent`. **Step C still selects by milestone** — *"the milestone loses the power to authorize, not the power to scope"* (AM-35) — and an implementer who removes `&milestone=<n>` from the list query has broken selection entirely while every authorization test still passes.

**Step E — ordering, and in revision 5 it is LOAD-BEARING rather than redundant (AM-29, binding — this is a reversal of revision 4's own characterisation).** `sorted(eligible, key=lambda c: (c.rank, c.number))`. Emit `#{number} [{priority}] {title}` with `title` defaulting to `""` and **no truncation**. Degenerate records (absent or `null` `labels`) must not raise — they rank `low` and stay eligible, matching the jq filter's `(.labels // [])` and its explicit test.

> **The ruling, in the sentence that is the ruling:** *"Walk the candidate set in `(rank, most-recently-updated first, number ascending)` order; **emit** the eligible set in `(rank, number ascending)` order — the order `next_candidates.jq` already documents — and never let the walk's tie-break be 'simplified' into the emission's."*
>
> > **REVISION 6 — the walk half of that sentence is SUPERSEDED by the human's TP-1 ruling; the emission half and the never-simplify clause are UNTOUCHED.** The ruling is quoted unedited (AM-38). **The binding walk order is `(rank, number DESC)`** — AM-29's own stated fallback, adopted because `updated_at` is advanced by any account posting a comment (TP-1, §7.2 R-8). **Read the sentence as: *walk in `(rank, number DESC)`; emit in `(rank, number ASC)`; never let one be simplified into the other.*** The never-simplify clause is the load-bearing half and it is now **more** exposed, not less: the two keys differ by a single token, so the "simplification" an editor would reach for is a one-character change.
>
> **Why they are different decisions, which is the whole finding.** Within a rank band, "outrank" is **undefined** — the records are of equal priority — so the within-band tie-break is not a correctness choice at all. It decides two unrelated things that AM-19 accidentally fused. **In the walk**, the tie-break decides which records are ever *evaluated* when the budget or the early exit ends it — a **reachability** decision. **In the emission**, the tie-break decides which issue the worker *builds first* — a **fairness** decision, `#901`'s deliberate FIFO-within-band, and **not the architect's to reverse, nor mine.**
>
> > **REVISION 6 — what the reachability decision costs, now that the ruling has fixed it.** AM-29 rejected `number ASC` for the walk because *"a record's number never changes and nothing the human can do moves a starved record forward"* — a **permanent, deterministic starvation of the high-numbered (newest) tail.** `(rank, number DESC)` carries **the same objection with the sign flipped: the starved region is the LOW-numbered (oldest) tail**, and nothing an author or a human does moves a record within its band. **The human accepted that trade explicitly and named what it buys:** closing *"a zero-privilege, indefinitely-repeatable starvation vector in a security-fix mechanism."* **Two things make DESC the right half of the pair to take, and neither is decorative.** (1) **It keeps the two orders different**, so Step E stays load-bearing and §6.2's re-fusion test stays meaningful — under `number ASC` the walk and the emission would be the *same* order and Step E would become the no-op revision 4 wrongly called it. (2) **It starves the oldest records rather than the newest, which aligns the walk with the list bound — and that alignment cuts both ways, so both halves are stated.** Step C's `created desc` list bound also truncates the *oldest*, so an operator now has **one** region to watch rather than two. **The cost of the same fact: the two bounds COMPOUND on that region rather than covering for each other** — the oldest records in a band are both the ones a list truncation drops and the ones the walk reaches last, and under revision 5's key an old record could at least be pulled forward by activity on it. **Rank dominance still holds absolutely, so this is a within-band reachability cost and never a safety one**, and §7.2 R-8 carries it. **(2) is my observation, not the ruling's, and I record the compounding rather than only the tidiness — a "both bounds fail the same way" note that omits "and therefore stack" is the shape of claim §7.3 exists to catch.**
>
> **I read `next_candidates.jq` in full this revision (§0.0.4) and it is a documented product policy, not an implementation detail:** `:1` attributes the filter to **`#901`**; `:12` says *"Tie-break: ascending issue number (**preserves FIFO-within-band behaviour**)"*; `:31` is `sort_by(.rank, .number)`. **Step E is the single line that converts the walk order into that contract** ***within the admitted slice*** *(qualified in revision 7, RUN4 RP4-5).* Under the early exit, Step E sorts **the newest ≤ `--max-candidates` records the walk admitted** from a band, not the band. **Across cycles, selection within a busy band is newest-first (LIFO), and `#901`'s FIFO-within-band holds only inside the slice a cycle admits.** Step E is still load-bearing, because it decides what the worker builds first among what was admitted. It does not make selection FIFO across the band, and §7.2 R-8 carries what that costs. Revision 4 called it *"a no-op on a correct implementation"* and told the coder to keep it as a tripwire. **That characterisation is withdrawn: under AM-29 the eligible set is accumulated in the WALK order — `(rank, number DESC)` as of revision 6 — and Step E genuinely re-orders it.** Deleting it as redundant now **changes what the worker builds first** while every membership test stays green. **Revision 6 makes Step E's re-ordering total rather than occasional: under `(rank, number DESC)` the accumulated order is the exact reverse of the emission order within every band, so Step E reverses each band rather than sometimes permuting it. It has never been less of a no-op than it is now.**
>
> **Binding (AM-29 point 2): §6.2 gains a test asserting the emitted order is `(rank, number ASC)` even when the walk order differs** — a fixture in which the two orders disagree, which is the only fixture that can catch a future edit "simplifying" Step E away on the strength of revision 4's own sentence.
>
> ~~**Why this closes RP-2 rather than relocating it:** under AR-7 the authorizing act is exactly one thing — a human applying the dispatch label from their own account — and **applying a label updates `updated_at`. So the act that authorizes a record is the same act that moves it to the head of its walk band.** Under `number ASC` it was not, and there was no second act that would be. **That property costs one sort key**, and it is why an early exit is safe to layer on top: the human has a recovery act that works *with* the exit rather than against it.~~
>
> > **STRUCK IN REVISION 6 — the property it describes is WITHDRAWN, and the sentence is struck rather than deleted so a reader who remembers it lands here.** The promotion-on-authorization property was real; it was also **available to anyone who can post a comment**, because `updated_at` is advanced by a comment as surely as by a label write (TP-1, verified live on #1709). **So the recovery act was never exclusive to the human, and an attacker could undo it for free, indefinitely, from a zero-privilege account.** The human ruled the trade: *"Accept losing the promotion-on-authorization property as the cost of closing a zero-privilege, indefinitely-repeatable starvation vector in a security-fix mechanism. Security posture over convenience here."*
> >
> > **Where that leaves RP-2, stated exactly rather than left implicit.** RP-2's defect — *"the ceiling binds and the ranked tail is starved with no act that moves a record forward"* — **is NOT closed by the walk key any more.** It is **bounded** by rank dominance (~~nothing omitted outranks anything emitted, unconditionally~~ *restated in revision 7: nothing the walk left unreached outranks anything admitted; quarantine holes are named. §2.3 D5.1*) and by the early exit's typical-case cost, and its within-band residue is **R-8**. **A reader must not carry "AM-29 closes RP-2" forward from revision 5; the honest statement is that AM-29 closed RP-2 with a key the human has since replaced, and what remains is a recorded residual rather than a closure.**
>
> **The residual, stated rather than left to be found — R-8 (§7.2), and its SUBJECT CHANGES in revision 6.** ~~An untrusted author can raise their own record's walk position by editing their issue, because an edit bumps `updated_at`.~~ **That attack is CLOSED: `number` is assigned at creation, never changes, and no act by any actor moves a record within its band.** What replaces it is the thing AM-29 was trying to avoid and the human has now accepted: **a deterministic, permanent within-band starvation of the low-numbered (oldest) tail, with no recovery act short of changing a record's rank** *(revision 7, RP4-5: and for rank 0, none at all. For lower ranks the rank change is only partial; §2.2.1)*. §7.2 carries the two surviving mitigations, ~~the one surviving recovery act~~ the partial re-rank act and its cost, and §9 hands R-8 to the panel to attack directly **in its new form** — the old form is closed and attacking it would be attacking a retired surface.

**Step F — the stderr summary, the completeness contract, and AM-13's held-everything line (binding; substantially rewritten in revision 4 — AM-20, AM-24).**

Always exactly one summary line. **Four fields are new:**

```
select_work_candidates: repo=<slug> milestone=<n> scanned=<N> evaluated=<E> eligible=<M> \
    authorized=<K> gated=<G> unevaluated=<U> api_requests=<A> complete=<yes|no>
```

| Field | Meaning | New? |
|---|---|---|
| `scanned=<N>` | Records returned by Step C after D1/D2's free filters — the candidate **set** | no |
| `evaluated=<E>` | Records the walk reached a **determination** on: eligible or gated | yes (AM-20) |
| `eligible=<M>` | Candidates emitted on stdout | no |
| `authorized=<K>` | Of the eligible, how many are eligible **via D5** — a verified human CODEOWNER's `labeled` event — as distinct from eligible via **D4** (trusted authorship). `K ≤ M` | yes (AM-24) |
| `gated=<G>` | Records the gate **determined** are held. **Determinations only** | semantics changed |
