| `unevaluated=<U>` | Records **Step C returned** that the walk **never reached, plus records it QUARANTINED** (`query-failed`), **plus the at-most-one record a budget clamp cut short** (revision 7, TP-2). `U = sufficient + cost-ceiling + query-failed`. `N = E + U` and `E = M + G`. **Records beyond a truncated list are NOT in `U`**. They are outside `N`, and `list-truncated` is a flag (revision 7, TP-3) | yes (AM-20), **widened by AM-31; partition made exact in revision 7** |
| **`api_requests=<A>`** | **GitHub API requests spent this cycle — list pages + events pages + collaborator pages. RENAMED from `auth_checks` and RE-DENOMINATED in revision 5 (AM-30).** Semantics are defined once, in §2.2.1's counter table, and **`api_requests <= effective_ceiling` is an invariant, not an aspiration** | **renamed (AM-30)** |
| `complete=<yes\|no>` | `yes` iff `U == 0` **and** no bound truncated the candidate set **and** no tier resolution was degraded | yes (AM-20), **third clause new (H3)** |

**`complete=yes` is an invariant, not a field.** It asserts that the gate looked at every record the query returned and that the query returned every record there is. Emitting it when either is false is the single failure this pair of rulings exists to make impossible, and §6.2 tests it as an invariant — every early-stop and every truncation path asserts `complete=no`, and the complete path asserts `complete=yes` — rather than as one happy-path assertion.

**When `U > 0` or the list was truncated, one additional line, always** *(the "or" is new in revision 7, so the truncation flag always has its line)* — separate from the `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` block and emitted whether or not that block fires:

```
select_work_candidates: UNEVALUATED <U> of <N> records were not evaluated: \
    unevaluated:sufficient=<w> unevaluated:cost-ceiling=<x> unevaluated:query-failed=<z> \
    unevaluated:list-truncated=<yes|no>
select_work_candidates: WARN unevaluated:query-failed issue=#<n>        # one line PER RECORD
select_work_candidates: unevaluated:cost-ceiling issue=#<n>             # AT MOST ONE: the record a budget
                                                                        #   clamp cut short (routine; no WARN)
```

**Revision 7 changes this grammar in two places, and both are reinstated run-3 findings (RUN4 RP4-3).** **(TP-3)** `list-truncated` was `=<y>`, a count summed into `U`. It is now `=<yes|no>`, a flag **outside** the sum, so `U = w + x + z` exactly and `N = E + U` holds by construction (§1.2 consequence 6). It moves to the end of the line so the three summed fields sit together. **(TP-2)** The at-most-one record a budget clamp cut short is counted in `x` and named on its own `unevaluated:cost-ceiling issue=#<n>` line (§1.2 consequence 7, §1.4 rule 4). Previously it was counted in `gated`.

**The fourth token is new in revision 5 (AM-31) and it behaves unlike the other three.** `sufficient`, `cost-ceiling` and `list-truncated` describe **why the walk stopped or was short**, so a count is the whole of the information. **`query-failed` describes one named record the walk stepped over while continuing**, so a count alone tells an operator a number without telling them which issue to look at — and their remedy is per-record. **Binding: it is reported with its issue number, on its own line, in the anomaly class.** `sufficient` and `cost-ceiling` are **routine and emit no `WARN`** (AM-30 point 3); `list-truncated` and `query-failed` are **anomalies and keep the loudest class**.

**And, when `eligible == 0 and gated > 0`, the distinct, unmissable held-everything block** (AM-13 point 2). This is not the same fact as the summary and must not be left for an operator to infer by comparing two numbers at 3am:

```
select_work_candidates: ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED <G> of <N> in-milestone issues are held for human authorization
select_work_candidates: ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED reasons: trusted-app-no-machine-filing-marker=<a> not-in-trusted-set=<b> no-codeowner-actor=<c> …
select_work_candidates: ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED issues: #1539 #1340 #1356 #1540 (+<k> more)
select_work_candidates: ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED INCOMPLETE — <U> further records were not evaluated (see UNEVALUATED above); this count is a FLOOR
```

*(Revision 7: when `unevaluated:list-truncated=yes`, the `INCOMPLETE` line MUST also say that records beyond the truncated list were not seen at all. `<U>` does not count them, so the floor is lower than `<U>` alone suggests.)*

**The fourth line is new in revision 4 and is emitted only when `complete=no`** (AM-20 point 4). Without it, a line reading *"<G> of <N> in-milestone issues are held for human authorization"* when *<U>* of them were never looked at is **a false statement in the one place the design put its trust.** AM-13 exists because a gate that holds everything and whispers is indistinguishable from an empty backlog; P-1 added a **third** state the design had no vocabulary for — *a gate that did not finish looking* — and three states, two of which are silent, is worse than the two-state problem AM-13 was pulled forward from S6 to fix.

**One interaction that falls out of the rulings and is worth stating because it removes a whole class of worry.** The **early exit cannot coexist with the `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` condition**: the stop requires `eligible ≥ --max-candidates ≥ 1`, and the block requires `eligible == 0`. So **the one place AM-13's counts are load-bearing is always a budget-or-truncated-or-quarantined walk, never an early-exited one.** The routine, expected, every-cycle stop can never be the thing that makes the held-everything count wrong. **This property is what makes the human's directive safe to adopt without touching AM-13**, and §6.2 pins it as a mutual exclusion rather than leaving it as an observation.

**The exit-3 condition, restated at the point it is computed (AM-32).** After Step F has the counts: **if `complete == no` and `eligible == 0`, exit 3.** Otherwise exit 0. **This includes the held-everything case (revision 7, RUN4 RP4-2).** When the `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` block fires, the exit is still decided by this line alone: 0 with `complete=yes`, 3 with `complete=no`. The block does not change the exit, and F30 no longer claims it does. **The gate must not derive this from the summary string it just printed** — the caller greps stderr for diagnostics, the *exit status* is the machine-readable channel, and computing one from the other would make a format change into a control-flow change.

**Per-candidate authorization lines (AM-24, binding).** For each candidate eligible **via D5**, the gate MUST emit one stderr line naming the issue number and the authorizing actor:

```
select_work_candidates: AUTHORIZED issue=#<n> actor=<login> via=labeled
```

> **`via=` has exactly ONE legal value in revision 5, and the field is retained anyway (AR-7 / AM-35).** Revision 4 emitted `via=<labeled|milestoned>`. **Under AR-7 the `milestoned` arm is deleted, so `via=milestoned` can no longer be produced by any input.** I keep the field rather than dropping it for two reasons, and the second is the operative one: it makes the S6 audit consumer's parser stable across the S3 era, when a second channel returns; and **a log line that never says which channel authorized is a log line that cannot demonstrate the channel was removed.** §6.2 asserts that **no fixture produces `via=milestoned`** — an absence test on the output, which is the cheapest possible detector for a deletion that missed a call site.

**State the limit before the feature, because this does NOT satisfy FR17 and must not be read as doing so.** `pm-agent` asked the architect whether any mechanism satisfies FR17 — *"approving without an assessment MUST NOT be silently possible"* — in the S2 era. **AM-24's answer is no, and the reason is structural rather than an absence of effort:** (i) the condition FR17 wants a signal about is **universal** in S2, because no assessor exists until S5, so every S2 authorization is an approval without an assessment — and a warning that fires on 100% of acts is not a warning, it is a banner, invisible by the second week; (ii) **S2 has no interception point** — the authorizing act is a human applying a label in the GitHub UI, and nothing in S2 sits between the act and its effect; the gate reads the resulting event minutes or days later; (iii) the one adjacent channel that could refuse at the moment of the act is `/approve`, which AF-5 establishes cannot authorize at all and which AM-25 now makes inert.

> **REVISION 5 CHANGES FR17's DISPOSITION, and the change is a downgrade in expectation rather than in mechanism (the human's H1 item 3, #1540 2026-09-16T07:14:38Z).** Through revision 4 this document said *"FR17 is deliberately deferred to S5"*, with **Q4 to be answered before S5 binds it**. **The human has now deferred the assessor agent itself, indefinitely:** *"**The assessor agent (Layer 3) itself is deferred to a future release, not just its per-cycle cap.** HOS doesn't currently see enough public/external request volume to justify building it now. When a request genuinely needs deeper analysis, the human will invoke an interactive/human-facing agent session directly rather than relying on an automated pipeline stage. **No cap value is needed since nothing is being built. This is the standing state, not a temporary bridge to a near-term S5.**"*
>
> **So FR17 is PERMANENTLY DEFERRED, not deferred-to-S5**, and the difference is not semantic. A deferral to a named future slice is a **schedule**; a deferral with no slice behind it is an **accepted permanent gap**. **Every S2-era authorization is, and will remain, an approval made without a written assessment** — H1 item 5 puts that to the human in those terms and they accepted it *"given item 3's deferral reframes this as permanent, not temporary."* **AM-24's reasoning is unaffected** — no mechanism satisfies FR17 in S2 by construction — but its conclusion now has no expiry date, and **Q4 is moot for as long as the assessor track is deferred.** A future reader who finds "deferred to S5" anywhere in this chain should read it against this paragraph.

What these lines *are*: in the S2 era they are **the entire machine-readable record that a human's unaided judgement was the only judgement in the loop** — **and, with the assessor deferred indefinitely, that is now the standing state rather than a bridge.** AD-12's durable per-decision audit is S6, and S6 is a long way from a repo that is building work today. This is deliberately small — one field and one line per authorized candidate, on a stream DEV-2 already un-silences — and it is the S2-era half of what AD-12 will later do properly. **Its significance rises with the deferral: it is the only durable record of the one judgement in the loop, and it now has to carry that weight without a successor scheduled.**

*Traceability (AR-5):* Step F implements **FR27** (AR-6: *Partly* — this is the S2 half; AD-11's full held-request listing is S6) and is the operator-surface half of **FR29**'s legibility requirement. It does **not** implement FR17, per the paragraph above.

Four binding properties of the held-everything block, unchanged in substance from revision 3:

1. **It must be distinguishable at a glance from "the milestone is empty."** `eligible=0 gated=0` and `eligible=0 gated=12` are opposite operational situations — nothing to do versus everything blocked on one person — and the summary line alone renders them as near-identical strings. The `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` token exists to be greppable and to be *visually* different, not to be parsed.
2. **It names the reason tokens and their counts** (AM-13's requirement), drawn from §1.2's closed reason-token set. `no-codeowner-actor` is the **D5.3** outcome (renumbered in revision 5 — RP-17) and is a gate-local token, not a `RequesterVerdict.reason`. **Binding (AM-20 point 3): the FOUR `unevaluated:*` tokens MUST NOT appear in this breakdown** — `query-failed` least of all, because a record whose query failed is the one an operator is most likely to mistake for a record a human must act on. It enumerates **determinations** only. They are reported on the separate `UNEVALUATED` line above, and mixing them here would restore exactly the conflation AM-20 exists to remove — a starved or quarantined record counted as *"held for human authorization"*, which is a false claim about a person's queue.
3. **It names the issue numbers**, bounded — first 20, then `(+k more)`. AM-13 requires only the count and the reasons; I am adding the numbers because §2.7.3's cutover is not performable without them, and because the gate has already scanned at most 100 records so the information is free. This is deliberately **not** AD-11's full held-request listing mode, which stays in S6: no bodies, no titles, no per-issue explanation, one bounded line.
4. **It is never routed into an issue** (AM-13 point 3). A per-cycle issue-filing loop on this condition would be its own denial-of-wallet, and bounded-retry/escalation is AD-10's design, which belongs with the assessor slice. stderr and the cycle log, nothing more.

Durable per-decision audit events are AD-12/S6 and are **not** built here. These lines are the whole of the S2-era operator signal, which is why §2.4's removal of `2>/dev/null` is **required, not optional** (AM-13 point 1).

### 2.4 Call site 1 — `bin/hos-cron` (the pre-computed candidates block)

> **REVISION 7 — FOUND WHILE VERIFYING, NOT APPLIED (out of revision 7's scope; recorded so it is a visible omission).** **#1805 (commit `5cd369130`, merged 2026-09-22, after revision 6) rewrote this block.** Read this session at `bin/hos-cron:1130-1162`, it now runs `gh api --paginate … 2>/dev/null | jq -sr "add | $(cat …next_candidates.jq)"` inside `if _GATE_CANDIDATES=$(…)`, with a long #1805 comment above it. **Every `bin/hos-cron` line citation in this section and in §0.0.4's table is therefore pre-#1805 and stale** (for example the `head -5` is now at `:1912-1915`, not `:1892-1895`). The design is unaffected, because S2 still replaces the whole block with the §2.4 "After" form. But `coder` must locate the block by content, not by line. #1805 also added `TestPaginationGlobalSort` and `TestBothPathsPaginate` to `tests/automation/test_next_candidates.py` (read this session at `:192` and `:242`). **§4.2's port table has no disposition for either class.** Both need one before that file is deleted. **Handed to run 5 and to the next revision; not dispositioned here.**

**Today** (`:1131-1146`, *pre-#1805; see the note above*): the actionable-work gate runs `gh api …` piped through `--jq "$(cat "$REPO_ROOT/scripts/automation/lib/next_candidates.jq")"` with `2>/dev/null`, capturing into `_GATE_CANDIDATES` and setting `_gate_candidates_ok`. `_build_context` (`:1886-1897`) reuses `_GATE_CANDIDATES` and `head -5`s it into the `### Next work candidates` section.

**After:**

```
  _GATE_CANDIDATES=""
  _gate_candidates_ok=1
  _gate_err="$(mktemp)"
  if [[ -n "${HOS_TARGET_MILESTONE_NUMBER:-}" ]]; then
    _GATE_CANDIDATES=$( (cd "$REPO_ROOT" && python3 -m scripts.framework.select_work_candidates \
        --repo "$_REPO_SLUG" --milestone "$HOS_TARGET_MILESTONE_NUMBER") 2>"$_gate_err" )
    _gate_rc=$?
    case "$_gate_rc" in
      0) _gate_candidates_ok=1 ;;                           # a determination the gate can state
      3) _gate_candidates_ok=0 ; _GATE_CANDIDATES="" ;;     # AM-32: DEGRADED — "unknown", not "none"
      *) _gate_candidates_ok=0 ; _GATE_CANDIDATES="" ;;     # exit 2 and anything unexpected
    esac
    cat "$_gate_err" >&2                                    # DEV-2: nothing is swallowed
    if [[ "$_gate_rc" -eq 3 ]]; then                        # AM-32: say it in the cycle log
      echo "$LOG_PREFIX WORK SELECTION INCOMPLETE — the gate could not determine the candidate set and emitted none; this is NOT 'no actionable work' (see the select_work_candidates lines above)"
    fi
    if grep -q 'ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED' "$_gate_err"; then    # AM-13 point 3
      echo "$LOG_PREFIX ALL WORK CANDIDATES GATED — every in-milestone issue is held for human authorization; this is NOT an empty backlog (#1539/#1540, see the select_work_candidates lines above)"
    fi
  fi
  rm -f "$_gate_err"
```

Unchanged around it: `_gate_all_ok` composition (`:1152-1156`), the `no actionable work` skip (`:1158-1163`), `_build_context`'s consumption and `head -5` (`:1894-1895`). The `(cd "$REPO_ROOT" && …)` subshell mirrors the established `_audit` invocation at `:364`.

> **AM-32 — the caller contract, and why the `if _GATE_CANDIDATES=$(…)` form is REPLACED by an explicit `case` (binding).** Revision 4 captured with `if _GATE_CANDIDATES=$( … ); then`, which collapses **every** non-zero status into one branch. Under AM-31 that is no longer safe to do, and the reason is the premise I read at the line this revision (§0.0.4, `bin/hos-cron:1134-1164`):
>
> - **exit 2** leaves `_gate_candidates_ok=0` → `_gate_all_ok=0` (`:1156`) → the cycle **proceeds** on "unknown", and the `#1395` skip at `:1158-1163` is **not** taken.
> - **exit 0 with empty output** leaves `_gate_candidates_ok=1`, and `:1158-1163` then **skips the entire cycle as "no actionable work"** if the other gates are also empty.
>
> **AM-31 converts a total events failure from exit 2 into a determination the gate can only partly state. Unamended, that would turn "the gate could not determine anything" into "there is nothing to do" — a silent skip**, which `CLAUDE.md`'s sandbox section names as the precise failure class to guard against, one layer up. **Exit 3 is the mechanism** (AM-32 offers it explicitly: *"2 is taken by fail-closed and by `argparse`; 3 is free"*), and §8/E-9 records why I read AM-31's *"MUST exit 0"* as *"MUST NOT stop the world"* rather than as a literal integer.
>
> **The binding property is not the number 3 — it is that ONLY `eligible=0 AND complete=yes` may contribute to the skip condition.** The `case` above satisfies it by mapping 3 onto the same `_gate_candidates_ok=0` path exit 2 already used, which is **the existing "unknown → proceed" semantics the launcher has had since `:1147-1151`** — so no new control flow is introduced, only a status that reaches it. **A call-site test in `tests/automation/test_hos_cron.py` is binding** (AM-32, §6.3): assert that a gate exiting 3 with empty stdout does **not** produce the `#1395` early skip.
>
> **Why the exit-3 log line is separate from the `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` grep and must stay separate.** They describe opposite situations: `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` means *the gate looked at everything and everything is held* (a human has work to do); exit 3 means *the gate could not finish looking* (nobody knows what is held). **Folding them into one message would recreate, in the cycle log, exactly the two-states-rendered-as-one defect AM-20 removed from the summary line.** Both may fire on the same cycle, and when they do, each says a true thing the other does not.
>
> **Why `--max-candidates` is still not passed at this call site.** The default is now **5**, which is what `_build_context` consumes at `:1894-1895`. Passing it explicitly would put a second copy of that number in a second file — the duplicate-literal defect this design exists to remove — and the flag may only lower, so a consumer who wants fewer can lower it without an edit here.

**Deliberate change, DEV-2 — required, not optional (AM-13 point 1):** the `2>/dev/null` at `:1139` is **removed**. Nothing about control flow changes; a silent skip is precisely the failure mode `CLAUDE.md` names as the one to guard against, and a fail-closed gate that says nothing is indistinguishable from an empty backlog.

**Why DEV-2 alone does not satisfy AM-13 point 3, and why the temp file is there.** I re-read `bin/hos-cron:113-114` this revision: the documented crontab is `>> /tmp/hos-worker-hos.log 2>&1`, so with `2>/dev/null` gone the gate's stderr does reach the cycle log in the shipped configuration. That is necessary and not sufficient, for two reasons the amendment's point 3 exists to cover. First, it is **configuration-dependent** — a consumer who redirects only stdout loses the entire signal, and AM-13's whole point is that this condition must not be losable. Second, raw subprocess stderr arrives **without `$LOG_PREFIX`**, so it is invisible to every existing habit and tool that greps the log by `[hos-worker-cron:<project>]`; the one line an operator most needs would be the one line that does not match their filter. Capturing stderr, re-emitting it verbatim, and then adding **one** prefixed line on the `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` token fixes both without parsing the gate's output for anything load-bearing — the token is a presence check, never a value the control flow depends on.

**What `bin/hos-cron` must NOT do with it:** not suppress the raw stderr in favour of the prefixed line (the reason tokens and issue numbers live in the raw lines); not branch on it (`_gate_candidates_ok` and `_GATE_CANDIDATES` remain the only control-flow inputs — a gated-everything cycle is *exit 0 with an empty list*, behaviourally identical to an empty milestone, and deliberately so, **when `complete=yes`**. *Revision 7, RP4-2: a gated-everything cycle with `complete=no` exits **3** and takes row 3 of the table below. It gets no early skip, and both the `WORK SELECTION INCOMPLETE` and the `ALL WORK CANDIDATES GATED` lines fire, because the `grep` runs whatever the exit status. At RUN4 M-1's population that is the ordinary cutover state, because a fully-gated walk cannot finish inside the ceiling*); and **not file an issue about it** (AM-13 point 3).

**Semantics after the change — trace it through, because this is where AD-3's "the fallback is the same gated script" pays off:**

| Gate result | `_gate_candidates_ok` | Context section | Worker behaviour |
|---|---|---|---|
| exit 0, candidates (**the early-exit case — now the normal one**) | 1 | the list (≤5) | picks the first non-blocked candidate |
| exit 0, empty, `complete=yes` | 1 | `(none available or fetch failed)` | Step 2 fallback → **same gated command** → same empty result → no work. **This is the only row that may contribute to the `#1395` skip, and that is AM-32's whole requirement** |
| **exit 3, empty, `complete=no` (NEW)** | 0 | `(none available or fetch failed)` | Step 2 fallback → **same gated command** → exit 3 → no work, **and no early skip.** The cycle proceeds to its other stages |
| exit 2 (any cause) | 0 | `(none available or fetch failed)` | Step 2 fallback → **same gated command** → exit 2 → no work |

In the third and fourth rows `_gate_all_ok=0` means the cycle does **not** take the `#1395` early skip — Step 0 triage, Step 0.5 release gating and Step 1 PR handling all still run. Only *new work selection* is closed. That is the intended split: the gate closes work selection, not the cycle. **Note that rows 3 and 4 are behaviourally identical at the launcher and deliberately so** — the launcher needs one thing from both (*do not conclude there is nothing to do*), and the distinction between "could not determine" and "fail closed" lives on stderr and in the audit trail, where an operator reads it, rather than in control flow, where an extra branch would be one more thing to get wrong.

**One property worth stating because a reviewer will ask:** this block runs *before* the cycle's git sync (`bin/hos-cron:1004-1017` explains why), so the gate code executed is the local clone's, one sync behind `origin/main`. That is unchanged from today, where `cat next_candidates.jq` read the same local tree.

### 2.5 Call site 2 — `bootstrap/worker-cron-prompt.md` Step 2

**Today** (`:97-101`): a `gh api … --jq "$(cat scripts/automation/lib/next_candidates.jq)"` fallback. That command is **unallowlistable under `CLAUDE.md`** (command substitution). **What happens to it on an unattended cycle is UNRESOLVED, and revision 4 stops asserting an answer (AM-28(g), binding).**

> **The discrepancy, stated rather than resolved.** Revision 3 asserted that on an unattended cycle the command *"is denied outright and silently skipped"*, citing `CLAUDE.md`'s sandbox section. **`bin/hos-cron:1764` launches `("$CLAUDE_BIN" --print --permission-mode bypassPermissions)`** — read by me this session — with `:1758-1762` stating in terms that this *"clears the approval gate so the agent can run gh/git/bash unattended"* and that the safety net is the App's scoped permissions, **"NOT the CLI permission gate."** The two repo sources describe opposite outcomes for the same command in the same situation. **I adopt neither side.** I have not run the experiment that would settle it, and an argument that rests on an unreconciled premise is an argument that changes its mind when someone reads the other file.
>
> **No design consequence either way** — exactly as the panel says. AD-3's collapse of the two call sites does not depend on which reading is right: under the `CLAUDE.md` reading the current fallback is silently skipped and the gate is never reached; under the `bypassPermissions` reading the current fallback *runs*, un-gated, and reopens the bypass S2 exists to close. **Both readings are reasons to replace it, and they are the same reason at different severities.** The replacement is required either way.
>
> **The contradiction itself is load-bearing well outside this design, and is recommended as a follow-up issue rather than carried here** (Amendment 3 §4 item 4). *"The agent cannot run that unattended"* is a premise this repo uses in several places; if `bypassPermissions` means it can, those arguments need re-reading. **It is not S2's to carry**, and S2 must not acquire a seventh thing to carry.

**After** — the fallback becomes one static command with no substitution, no inline `jq`, and no variable expansion (`@@MILESTONE_NUMBER@@` is substituted by `_build_prompt` before the LLM ever sees it, so the rendered text is fully literal):

```
python3 -m scripts.framework.select_work_candidates --repo thurlow-research/HumanOversightSystem --milestone @@MILESTONE_NUMBER@@
```

**The prose that must accompany it is part of the contract, not decoration:**

> This command is the **only** sanctioned way to produce work candidates. **If it exits non-zero — 2 or 3 — there are no candidates for you this cycle.** Exit 2 means it failed closed. Exit 3 means it could not determine the full candidate set and is telling you so rather than pretending the queue is empty; **in neither case does "no output" mean "there is no work"**, and in neither case are you to go looking for some. Do NOT construct your own query, do NOT fall back to `gh api`, do NOT read the issue list by any other means, and do NOT pick an issue from the Step 0 triage list. STOP after Steps 0/0.5/1.

Without that paragraph a capable model will helpfully improvise a `gh api` query and reopen the bypass in the most sympathetic possible way. A conformance test (§6.3) asserts the prompt contains this instruction and contains **no** `gh api …labels=needs-ai…` query and **no** `--jq`.

> **Why exit 3 is named in the prompt rather than left to "non-zero" — the LLM is the one caller for whom the distinction is a temptation (AM-32, applied to §2.5).** `bin/hos-cron` needs one behaviour from 2 and 3 alike (§2.4), so the shell branch is shared. **A model is different: told only that the command "failed", it is exactly the reader that will reason "perhaps the tool is broken; let me check the issue list directly" — which is the improvisation this paragraph exists to forbid, arriving through the most sympathetic possible door.** Naming exit 3 and saying what it means removes the inference the model would otherwise make. **The prompt's rendered text still contains no substitution, no expansion, no inline `jq` and no continuation**, so G11's four conditions are unaffected by the addition — the extra sentences are prose, not command text.

**AM-11 — the binding acceptance condition, because G11 is superseded rather than merely overlapped.** `ADR-1542` §4.9 (G11) planned a sandbox-allowlistable wrapper around `next_candidates.jq` for exactly this call site. S2's entry point **is** that wrapper, built for a different reason, and building G11 separately would produce the duplicate-gate defect (#1135) in its purest form: two wrappers over the same selection decision, one of them trust-gated. The architect has therefore marked G11 superseded and orphaned its sign-offs **for that item only** — with a condition attached that this document must meet, not merely intend:

> The rendered fallback invocation MUST contain **no command substitution, no variable expansion, no inline `jq`, and no backslash continuation.**

That is G11's actual requirement. **The justification for accepting it is restated in revision 4 on a ground that holds either way (AM-28(g), binding), because revision 3's rested on the premise §2.5's opening now records as unresolved.**

> **The fallback MUST be sandbox-allowlistable because `CLAUDE.md` requires it of every command in this repo** — a standing, documented rule, independent of what any particular runtime does with a non-conforming command. That is the whole justification, and it is the same justification whether an unallowlistable command is silently skipped or silently *executed with the permission gate bypassed*. Revision 3 instead argued that a non-conforming command *"would fail to execute"*, which is one of the two readings and is the weaker argument in the worse way: **if the `bypassPermissions` reading is the right one, revision 3's argument is not merely unproven — it is backwards**, because the failure it invokes as the reason to fix the line is the failure that would *not* occur, and the command would run un-gated instead.

The command above satisfies all four conditions by construction, because `@@MILESTONE_NUMBER@@` is substituted by `_build_prompt` *before* the LLM ever sees the text, so the rendered string is wholly literal. **§6.3's conformance test is the mechanism that keeps it true, and AM-11 makes it required, not optional** — an intention about a prompt string, unpinned by a test, survives exactly until the next person needs a second milestone. **§6.3 also now specifies *how* that test obtains the rendered string** (AM-28(a)), which revision 3 left unstated: an unspecified mechanism in a test that pins a security-relevant string is a test that gets written the easy way.

**Note on AD-7, binding:** this design assumes Step 0 does the wrong thing. Nothing above depends on Step 0's routing behaviour. S4 may improve Step 0; the gate's correctness does not move when it does.

### 2.6 Worked trace of the five live candidates

Applying §2.3 to the five candidates the dispatching worker verified (author, then `needs-ai`/milestone actors). Revision 1 labelled this "before §8/E-1 is ruled"; **E-1 is now ruled (AM-1) and the trace is unchanged** — option C keeps AD-2 exactly as written, so every verdict below stands. What changed is the *interpretation*: this is no longer a provisional picture pending a widening, it is the intended steady state.

| Issue | Author | `needs-ai` / milestone actor | D3 verdict | D5 result | Selectable? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| #1539 | `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` | `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` | untrusted — `trusted-app-no-machine-filing-marker` | actor is a bot → `is_bot_reviewer` → `continue` → `None` | **No** |
| #1340 | `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` | `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` | same | same | **No** |
| #1356 | `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` | `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` | same | same | **No** |
| #1540 | `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` | `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` | same | same | **No** |
| #1643 | `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` | `ScottThurlow` (needs-ai), `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` (milestone) | untrusted | `labeled` event actor `ScottThurlow` — not a bot, in CODEOWNERS → **authorized** | **Yes** |
| #1678 | `hos-worker-hos[bot]` | `hos-worker-hos[bot]` (both) | untrusted — title has no marker | bot actor → `continue` → `None` | **No** — this is the self-authorization shape the gate exists to reject |

> **Three revision-5 corrections to how this table must be read. None changes a verdict, and saying so is the point of listing them.**
> 1. **The "milestone actor" half of column 3 is now INFORMATIONAL ONLY.** Under AR-7 / AM-35 a `milestoned` event authorizes nothing, so the milestone actor cannot change any row's outcome. It is retained because it is what was measured and because striking it would make the trace incomparable with the three prior revisions that carry it. **A reader must not infer from its presence that the gate reads it.** #1643 — the one selectable row — passes on its `needs-ai` **`labeled`** event by `ScottThurlow`, which is the only channel there is.
> 2. **The population these five were drawn from is superseded.** They were the live candidates at revision 1; the queue is now **101 records** (§0.0.4). The trace is a worked example of the *rules*, not a census, and the census is §0.0.4's.
> 3. **Under the early exit, a walk over this set would stop after emitting #1643 alone** — one eligible candidate, `--max-candidates 5` not reached, so the walk runs to the end of the ranked set and reports `complete=yes` with `eligible=1`. **The early exit changes nothing here precisely because so little is eligible**, which is the shape of the post-merge drain state and the reason the request ceiling remains the binding constraint in that state rather than the exit.
> 4. **NEW IN REVISION 6 — the walk order over these six records is now FIXED and computable from the table, which it was not under either prior key.** Under the binding walk key **`(rank, number DESC)`**, the three `priority:critical` records (§2.3's measurement: #1678, #1643, #1539) walk **`#1678` → `#1643` → `#1539`**, and the remaining three follow in their own bands, highest number first. **Three things a reader should check against this.** (a) **The verdicts in the table are order-independent** — every one is a function of the record's author and its events, and nothing in D3/D4/D5 reads walk position — **so no row changes and the trace stands exactly.** (b) **#1539 is walked third, not first**, which is worth stating plainly because it is the issue the whole mechanism exists to close: it is still inside the first three records evaluated, inside any ceiling ≥ 3 and inside the early exit at 5, so the safety property holds — **but "the fix's own issue is evaluated first" is no longer true and must not be repeated.** (c) **The emission order is unchanged** — Step E re-sorts to `(rank, number ASC)`, so #1643, the one selectable row, is emitted the same way it always was. **This table is the smallest worked demonstration in the document that the walk order and the emission order are independent of the verdicts, which is why it did not need re-deriving when the walk key changed.**

This is the correct, intended behaviour of every line of §1–§2 as written. It is also a near-dead work loop, which is why E-1 was an escalation and not a footnote. The mechanism has a *working* release valve — #1643 passes purely because a human clicked `needs-ai` from their own account, which is precisely AM-1's option C in action, observed rather than theorised.

**Correction, AM-6 / AF-5 — revision 1 got the valve's shape wrong in one clause.** Revision 1's §8/E-1 option C said an issue becomes selectable when `ScottThurlow` personally applies `needs-ai` or the milestone from their own account **"or comments `/approve`."** *That last clause is false.* `.github/workflows/label-swap.yml` performs the `/approve` label edit with `GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}` (`:36`), so the resulting `labeled` event's actor is `github-actions[bot]` — `type == "Bot"`, login ending `[bot]` — which `is_bot_reviewer` excludes unconditionally at layers 1 and 2, whether or not it appears in `BOT_ACCOUNTS`. `verify_codeowner_actor` walks events only. **A CODEOWNER commenting `/approve` therefore produces no authorization at all in S2.** The issue flips labels, the workflow posts `"✅ /approve — needs-human → needs-ai (authorized by @ScottThurlow)."`, and the gate still refuses it: fail-closed and therefore safe, and **a silent no-op that looks exactly like success** — the same failure mode AD-6 already condemns in that workflow's own `2>/dev/null || true`.

The first two clauses of option C are correct, and #1643 is the live proof of the first. Everything that follows from the correction — what the human must actually do, what the repo must stop telling them, and the deadlock this creates for the cutover set — is §2.7.

**Reading the `needs-ai` column above with AF-5 in mind:** the trace's actors are the *recorded event actors*, so an issue whose `needs-ai` arrived via `/approve` would show `github-actions[bot]` there, not the commenter. None of the six does, so no row changes. A reviewer checking this against live GitHub should expect `github-actions[bot]` on any issue whose label came from a slash command, and must not read the workflow's confirmation comment as evidence of who applied the label.

---

## 2.7 The S2-era authorization act (AM-1, AM-3, AM-6) — what the human does, what the repo must stop saying, and the one thing that does not work

This section is new in revision 2. It exists because AM-1 makes one demand of this document above all others: *a gate whose documented approval path silently does nothing is worse than no gate.* Everything here is about making the documented path the one that actually works.

### 2.7.1 The authorizing act — stated verbatim, as the amendment requires

> **The authorizing act must be performed by the human's own GitHub account.** `bootstrap/edit_issue.sh --app human` writes as `scottthurlow-claude[bot]`, which `is_bot_reviewer` excludes, so any tooling or documentation offering `edit_issue.sh --app human` as the approval path is wrong and must not be written. The valid S2-era acts are: **the GitHub web UI, the GitHub mobile app, or a personal-access-token `gh` invocation under `ScottThurlow`.**

Three binding consequences for the S2 change set:

1. **No approval helper may be written.** Not a script, not a `Makefile` target, not a documented one-liner, not a convenience wrapper "that just calls `edit_issue.sh` for you." Any such tool would run under an App identity and would produce a `labeled` event the gate excludes — a tool that appears to authorize and does not is strictly worse than no tool, because it converts a visible gap into an invisible one. This is the same reasoning as D-5: the failure is silent and looks like success.
2. **The sentence above must appear in whatever the human is asked to do at cutover** (§2.7.3), not only here.
3. **Nothing in S1/S2 may special-case the human-proxy App**, in either direction. It is one of the three `machine-accounts.env` App identities in AD-1(b), narrowed by AD-2 exactly like the other two. A carve-out written to make the queue move would be option A wearing a configuration costume.

Option **D** — the human-proxy session stops self-applying the dispatch label to its own filings, and ends every filing by telling the human the exact act required — is adopted as the ergonomic wrapper and is *also* the correct hygiene fix independent of ergonomics: a human-proxy session applying `needs-ai` to its own filing is the same self-authorization shape as the worker's Step 0 (VF-3), one identity removed. It should stop regardless. The `CLAUDE.md` human-proxy-section edit that needs is a protected-surface change, human-gated at merge, and is **not pre-authorized here** (AD-14). It is also **not** in S2's file list (§2.1) — it is a behaviour change to an interactive session, separable from the gate, and folding it into the critical-path fix would add a sixth protected surface for an ergonomic gain. **AM-17 confirms this reasoning and rules that the count is not six**, while requiring that the human be *told the surface exists* as a deliberately-excluded planned follow-up (Amendment 2 §3(b) item 4's closing paragraph) — recording an excluded surface is the difference between a scope boundary and an omission. If the human wants option D inside S2, the count is six and that is their call to make, not this document's.

### 2.7.2 `/approve` in the S2 era: it is made INERT (revision 4 — AM-25 supersedes revision 3's disposition)

> **Section heading changed in revision 4.** Through revision 3 this section was titled *"…and why it is not made to refuse"*, and §9/R5 handed that judgement call to the panel with its three stated reasons attached. **The panel falsified one reason; the architect checked the other two and falsified a second. AM-25 OVERRULES R5 and the disposition goes with its basis.** The reasoning is preserved below rather than deleted, because *how* a stated justification came to be wrong is the useful part.

**What `/approve` does in S2: NOTHING to any label.** Binding, and it is small in code and large in consequence:

> **In S2, `/approve`'s branch in `.github/workflows/label-swap.yml` performs NO label write.** It posts one comment stating that `/approve` does not authorize autonomous work and naming the act that does (§2.7.4's verbatim wording, from the human's own account), and it **changes no labels**. The `gh issue edit --remove-label needs-human --add-label needs-ai` at `:74` is **removed**, and the `2>/dev/null || true` that guards it goes with it. **`/decline` is untouched** (`:79`-`:82`, re-read by me this session — revision 3 cited it at `:83`, which was wrong).

**Three harms disappear at once, none of which wording could have reached:**

1. **The false *"authorized by @you"* claim** — AM-6 point 3's shipping condition, now satisfied by the command no longer doing anything to claim.
2. **The consumption of the human's one additive act** on an issue not yet carrying the dispatch label — AM-15's "actively harmful" finding, which revision 3 could only document, not fix.
3. **P-5's double invisibility.** The panel found that post-S2 `/approve` removes `needs-human` **as well as** adding `needs-ai`, so the issue leaves the human's queue while remaining unactionable by the worker — **invisible to both.** With no label write, `needs-human` is no longer stripped and the issue stays in the queue the human watches. **P-5 dissolves at the root rather than being mitigated by wording.**

**Why R5's basis is gone — the three stated reasons, checked (AM-25).** Revision 3 declined to make `/approve` refuse on three reasons, and recorded them explicitly. That record is the only reason this was checkable at all, and it is worth saying so before the table: a judgement call that states its reasons can be falsified; one that says "on balance" cannot.

| R5's stated reason | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| *"the runbook's intervention procedure"* references `/approve` | **FALSE** | `grep -n '/approve' docs/OVERSIGHT-RUNBOOK.md` → **no matches.** Found by the panel (P-3); **re-derived by me this session** (§0.0.3) |
| *"the `merge_authority.py` `_HUMAN_GATE_LABELS` merge-block removal"* | **FALSE** | `_HUMAN_GATE_LABELS` blocks on **PR** labels (`scripts/automation/lib/merge_authority.py:494`, `:519-521`, `:557-566`); `/approve` fires **only on issues** (`label-swap.yml:29` excludes `github.event.issue.pull_request`). **A command that cannot touch a PR's labels cannot be the thing that clears a PR's merge block.** The architect's finding; **re-derived independently by me** (§0.0.3) |
| *"the documented triage transition"* | **True, but not a dependency** | `docs/LABELS.md:29` documents `/approve` as a `needs-ai` writer, and `worker.md`/`worker-cron-prompt.md` cite its swap *pattern* as the model for the PR-side clear. Those are citations of a **pattern**, not callers. **No code invokes `/approve`** — and S2 invalidates the transition anyway |

**I stated three reasons to leave a live workflow alone. Two do not exist, and the third is a documentation reference to a transition S2 invalidates.** Both false ones were checkable in one `grep` each. The lesson I am recording against myself, because it generalises past this instance: **the two false reasons were the two I did not verify, and they were false in the direction that supported the conclusion I had already reached.** The third — the one I *had* read — is the one that survived, narrowed. A stated reason is not evidence; it is a claim with a citation attached, and this document's own §0 discipline applies to its dispositions and not only to its facts.

**No new protected surface.** `.github/workflows/**` is already in S2's five (AM-17) and `label-swap.yml` was already being edited for AM-6 point 3. **This changes *what* that edit is, not how many surfaces S2 touches. The count remains five.**

**Disclosed, not absorbed.** Making a command a CODEOWNER types behave differently is **user-visible behaviour** for the one human this system has, so per the CORE product-boundary checkpoint it is **named to the human at H1** (Amendment 3 §3(b) item 2's fourth bullet and item 4's fifth surface) rather than bound silently. **If they want `/approve` to keep swapping labels, they say so at H1 and the wording-only mitigation is taken instead** — the security properties are identical either way, because `/approve` cannot authorize in S2 under any version. But they should decide it knowing that leaving it live currently takes away the cheap path to authorization.

**What is unchanged: S2 remains event-only.** AM-6 point 2 is explicit that comment-reading must not be bolted onto the critical-path fix, and I am not proposing it. Making `/approve` inert is the *opposite* of pulling its channel forward: it removes a write, it adds no read, and it leaves the gate's input set exactly as AM-6 point 2 specifies.

**Ruled in revision 3: comment-reading is NOT pulled forward (AM-16 — E-5 question 3).** I raised it because E-5 is the strongest argument for AM-6 point 4 that exists — `label-swap.yml:29` has no label-state precondition, so a `/approve` comment is a **single** act available on an already-labelled issue, which is exactly the act the cutover set lacks. The architect verified that and held the line anyway, for three reasons in ascending weight, and they are recorded here because a future reader will re-discover the same argument:

1. **The cost that motivated the question is gone.** AM-14 replaces a ≥186-act pre-merge pass with a single-digit primed head and a lazy drain (§2.7.3). Two acts at the moment the human decides an issue is next — a decision they already make — is not a burden that justifies advancing a new authorization channel onto the critical path.
2. **The comment channel is irrevocable by construction, and its bounds are S3's content.** Every event-derived authorization has a natural clearing act (`unlabeled`, `demilestoned` — which is what AM-8's S3 rule formalises). **A comment has no inverse:** there is no "uncomment" event, and a deleted comment leaves no timeline record the gate can read. A `/approve` typed months ago on an issue since rewritten would authorize it indefinitely, and the two mechanisms that bound that — AD-5's digest binding and its ordering invariant (approval newer than assessment) — are precisely what S3 introduces. *(Revision 7 annotation (TP-8): since the human's H1 item 3, **no digest-binding fix is being built** and the assessor is deferred indefinitely. So neither mechanism is scheduled, and the ordering invariant has no assessment to be newer than (§7.2 R-1, R-2). AM-16's conclusion, not pulling the comment channel forward, is strengthened rather than weakened: its bounds now have no date at all.)* Shipping the channel in S2 and its bounds in S3 means S3 retrofits invariants onto live authorization semantics instead of adding a channel already inside them. **The channel and its bounds are one design; the ordering is not an accident of the build plan.**
3. **Decisive: it would put a newly-specified authorization channel on the critical-path fix, before the panel has examined the channels that already exist.** The comment channel's whole correctness rests on anchoring to the GitHub-reported comment author, and the failure mode of getting that wrong is an issue's own body claiming its approval — the **same class of fact as AF-5**, a four-line `env:` block that silently decided whether an authorization path worked, unnoticed through an ADR, a design and five days. A second instance of that class does not belong in the commit that closes the first one.

**Consequence for S3, recorded now so the reason is on the record when it is built rather than reconstructed: E-5 is added to the S3 work item as the ergonomic justification for AM-6 point 4.** AM-6 point 4 is unchanged and remains binding on S3, with the anchoring requirement, the untrusted-body rule, and the one-extra-API-call cost exactly as stated.

**AM-6 point 3 remains a shipping condition:** *S2 MUST NOT ship while the repo tells a human that `/approve` authorizes work.* **Revision 4 satisfies it by removing the act rather than by correcting the claim.** Three required elements of the S2 change set, all listed in §2.1 — the third is new:

| Where | Required change |
|---|---|
| `docs/LABELS.md` | The `needs-ai` row's writer column already names `label-swap.yml` (`/approve`, `/handoff`) — verified in revision 2, re-read in revision 3. **Updated in revision 4: `/approve` is no longer a writer of `needs-ai` at all**, and the row must say so rather than describing a swap that no longer happens. It gains: that in the S2 era `/approve` **changes no labels** and authorizes nothing; the acts that **do** authorize (§2.7.1, §2.7.4 verbatim); AM-15's authorization-record sentence; and — **new in revision 4, AM-27 point 2** — **the milestone-route prohibition**, with the Step 0 race as its stated reason. §4.3 carries the full wording requirement. **This file is the durable reference home for the authorizing act.** |
| `.github/workflows/label-swap.yml:74-75` | **Changed in revision 4.** `:74`'s `gh issue edit --remove-label needs-human --add-label needs-ai … 2>/dev/null \|\| true` is **removed**. `:75`'s body — currently `"✅ /approve — needs-human → needs-ai (authorized by @${COMMENTER})."` — is replaced by a statement that **`/approve` does not authorize autonomous work and has changed nothing**, naming the act that does. The **three facts** it must carry are now (a) this authorized nothing, (b) **no label was changed** — which replaces revision 3's "it may have consumed your additive act", since it no longer can, and (c) here is what does authorize, from your own account. **Exact wording is the coder's** (the architect declined to fix it, and declines again in Amendment 3 §4). §6.3 pins the **absence** of `authorized by` **and the presence** of a "does not authorize" statement — R4's hole, deliberately left open in one direction only. |
| `docs/OVERSIGHT-RUNBOOK.md:70-75` | **NEW in revision 4 — AM-25 / panel P-3.** The **operator's procedure** home. §4.3 carries the three facts. |

**Correction to the panel's P-3, which sharpens it rather than weakening it — and it is why the runbook fix must carry three things, not one.** The panel said the runbook's act is a no-op because the issue *"already carries `needs-ai` — which is every issue in the cutover set."* That is right for the queued set, but **the runbook passage is about a different case**: the overseer's escalation handoff, where the issue may carry only `needs-human`. On *that* issue, *"remove `needs-human`, add `needs-ai`"* performed **from the human's own account** is a perfectly valid authorization — it is §2.7.1's act, described without its load-bearing qualifier. **So the runbook's defect is not only "sometimes a no-op": it is that it never says the act must be performed from the human's own account**, in a repo whose documented tooling for label edits is `bootstrap/edit_issue.sh --app human`, which writes as `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` and which AM-1 consequence 1 forbids as an approval path. **The failure is therefore available on *every* issue, not only on those already carrying the label.**

**There is a good precedent sitting in the same file to align with, and the fix should read as its sibling rather than as a new concept.** The release-request exception (NG3b) at `:77` onward — read by me this session — **already teaches a human act whose *actor* is verified from the GitHub Events API, in those words**: *"your removal of it is one of the three authorization signals the worker verifies from the GitHub Events API"*, performed *"as the same CODEOWNER account, directly in GitHub."* That paragraph is the shape the corrected control 1 should take. A reader who has absorbed NG3b already understands why the account matters; the fix is to stop making control 1 the one place in the file that omits it.

**`/approve` in the S2 era is INERT, which supersedes revision 3's "actively harmful" characterisation — but the harm it describes is why the change was made, so the reasoning is retained.** Revision 2 documented `/approve` as a no-op for authorization; **AM-15 hardened that to actively harmful**, on the mechanical finding that `label-swap.yml:29` has **no label-state precondition** — the command fires on any issue — so on an issue not yet carrying the dispatch label, `/approve` applied that label as `github-actions[bot]`, **consuming the one genuinely additive act the human still had** and converting a one-act issue into a two-act issue (§2.7.4). Revision 3 could only document that. **Revision 4 removes it: with no label write, there is no act to consume.** A reader encountering AM-15's "actively harmful" language in this chain should read it as the *diagnosis that motivated AM-25*, not as a live property of the shipped command.

**What S3 does about it (AM-6 point 4, stated now so it is not redesigned from scratch):** S3's gate accepts, in addition to the event sources, **a comment whose GitHub-reported author is a verified human CODEOWNER and whose first line begins with the approval command**, anchored the same way `label-swap.yml` already anchors it (first word of the first line). The comment *body* stays untrusted content — only the anchored command token and the API-reported author are read, and nothing in the body may influence the outcome. This costs one additional API call per untrusted candidate, on the path the ADR's cost note already accepts as non-free. The attack to watch, and the one the panel should press on: an implementation that reads "the gate reads comments" and forgets the anchoring on the *GitHub-reported author*, which would let an issue's own body claim its approval.

### 2.7.3 The cutover set: bounded by the gate's own query, no grandfathering (AM-3)

**A grandfathering list is forbidden.** It is two violations at once: a static list of pre-approved issues is a trusted-set widening (AD-13) *and* a cached eligibility determination (AD-4/FR7 — an authorization verified at time T is re-verified at T+n; an approval recorded earlier is evidence to re-check, not a stored grant). Revision 1 argued against it; AM-3 makes it a rule.

**The cutover set is bounded by the gate's own query — and it is 101 issues (updated in revision 5; it was ≥ 97 in revision 4).** The boundary rule is unchanged and still right: an issue that is closed, milestone-less, in a non-target milestone, or without the dispatch label costs nothing at cutover — it costs one human act at the moment it would otherwise have been selected, which is the same act the human already performs implicitly when they decide it is next. The set is therefore **the open, dispatch-labelled, non-PR issues in the active milestone**, exactly `--milestone <n>&labels=needs-ai&state=open` minus `EXCLUDED_LABELS` minus PR records.

**What revision 2 got wrong here, corrected by AF-6 and cited rather than re-derived.** Revision 2 wrote "the cutover set is not 46 issues" and AM-3 wrote "far smaller than 46". Both inferred *smallness* from a boundary rule, and bounding a set by a query says nothing about its cardinality. Measured live by the architect with the exact query AM-3 names as the bound:

```
bash bootstrap/query_issues.sh --app worker --list --milestone "v0.7.0" --label needs-ai --state open
  → 2026-09-14 (panel):     96 records, #1539 at position 86
  → 2026-09-15 (architect): 97 records, #1539 at position 88
  → 2026-09-16 (me, §0.0.3): 97 records, #1539 at position 88
  → 2026-09-16 (pm-agent/panel): 98 records
  → 2026-09-16 (worker, §0.0.4, VIA THE GATE'S OWN STEP C QUERY, PAGINATED): 101 records
```

> **UPDATED IN REVISION 5, and the last row is different in kind from the four above it.** Rows 1–4 come from `bootstrap/query_issues.sh --list`, which issues **a single unpaginated page** (`:189-201`) — so each is a **floor**, capped at 100 by construction. **The 101 figure comes from the gate's own paginated Step C query** (`issues?state=open&milestone=2&labels=needs-ai&per_page=100`, PR records excluded, milestone 2 confirmed as `v0.7.0 — Quality`), **so it is the first row in this table that is not page-limited — and it is the first that could exceed 100.** **Provenance: the dispatching worker, measured 2026-09-16T~15:55Z. NOT re-derived by me**, because `query_issues.sh` emits no `.user` field and `CLAUDE.md` forbids hand-rolling a `gh api` read, so **no sanctioned path available to me can reproduce it** (§0.0.4).
>
> **Two consequences, and the second is the one the human's clearance rests on.** **(i) The floor property is discharged for the last row only:** *"≥97"* was correct and is superseded by a figure that is not a floor. **(ii) The population has crossed the single-page ceiling**, so **`query_issues.sh --list` can no longer enumerate this queue at all** — which sharpens rather than removes the irony §2.7.3 already recorded: **the tool the human is told to enumerate their own queue with now has, in live effect, the exact defect AM-22 removed from the gate.** §4.3 requires the command to be presented with that caveat; **revision 5 upgrades the caveat from "page-limited" to "page-limited AND currently truncating."**

**The figure is 101, and the five measurements above are the whole of its provenance** — the 2026-09-16 row is mine, re-derived this session through the sanctioned read path; the other two are cited from the panel and Amendment 3 §0 respectively and are **not** re-derived by me. Revision 3's **≥93** was AF-6's measurement and was correct when made.

**The movement between those rows is AF-8's finding and matters more than the figure.** A `created desc` list moves old issues toward the truncation edge **monotonically as new issues are filed**. In two days the page gained a record and #1539 moved two places further from the head. **It is roughly a dozen filings from falling off a 100-record page entirely** — which is why §2.2.1 paginates Step C and why R-6 is closed rather than carried (§7.2). A residual recorded without its distance to firing is a residual nobody can prioritise.

**Read the number with both of its error bars, because they point in opposite directions and both matter:**

- **It is a floor.** `query_issues.sh --list` issues a single `per_page=100` page with **no** `--paginate` (`:189-201`, read by me this session), then filters PR records. **This error bar is now DISCHARGED for the current figure and retained for the historical ones:** 97 non-PR records could have been a full 100-record page containing 3 PRs — and PRs in this repo do carry `needs-ai`. **I can bound page occupancy at ≥97/100 but cannot state it exactly**, because `--list` filters PRs before printing. So **≥97, possibly more**, and any document quoting "97" without "at least" is quoting it wrong. **Note the irony and do not let it pass unremarked: the tool the human is told to enumerate their queue with has exactly the defect AM-22 just removed from the gate.** It is not fixed here — S2 must not acquire another thing to carry — but the human's own enumeration remains page-limited after S2 ships, and §4.3 requires the command to be presented with that caveat attached.
- **Not every one of the 101 needs a human act, and revision 5 can now say something about how many.** §0.0.4's author distribution — `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` 49, `hos-worker-hos[bot]` 32, `hos-overseer-hos[bot]` 16, `ScottThurlow` 4 — **bounds the trusted-authored subset at 4** (`ScottThurlow`'s own filings, AD-1(a)), because the roster ships empty and none of the 97 bot-authored records carries a machine-filing marker. **What is still not measured is how many of the remaining 97 already carry a human CODEOWNER's `labeled` event**, and measuring that costs one events call per candidate — **which is exactly what the ≈100-request ceiling prices, and the reason the gate's worst case is its normal case while the queue drains.** A `ScottThurlow`-authored issue is trusted by AD-1(a); a bot-authored issue matching a machine-filing marker is trusted by AD-2; and an issue already carrying a `labeled`/`milestoned` event by a human CODEOWNER (#1643 is the live example) is already authorized. The subset requiring an act is the untrusted-authored remainder lacking such an event. It is **not measured**, and measuring it costs one events call per candidate — **which is exactly the ≈100 paid checks §2.2.1 prices, and the reason the gate's worst case is its normal case while the queue drains.**

**The consequence that decides the cutover, and it is architectural rather than operational: the authoritative count cannot be obtained before S2 merges.** Deriving it requires running the gate's own authorization test against every candidate — and the gate is what S2 ships. §2.3 Step F's `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` output is "the only listing that cannot drift from the gate, because it *is* the gate", and that output exists only post-merge. **A pre-merge pass therefore cannot be sized before it is performed.**

Two ways to produce a number, and they do **not** agree in strength — the first is an estimate, the second is the answer:

| Purpose | Command | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| The human's own enumeration, before S2 merges — **an estimate, and a page-limited one** | `bash bootstrap/query_issues.sh --app human --list --milestone "v0.7.0" --label needs-ai --state open` | Existing canonical entry point; filters `.pull_request == null` already; **prefix-matches the milestone title** (`:55-57`, `:160-169`) — fine *here*, since it is an enumeration tool for a human that resolves to one milestone and fails closed on ambiguity, and exactly what the gate must not do (§1.4). **Two limits, both new in revision 3 (AF-6):** it returns a **single unpaginated page**, so its count is a floor; and it cannot distinguish the issues that need an act from those already authorized or trusted-authored, because that determination is the gate's. |
| The authoritative answer, after S2 merges | The gate's own `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` lines (§2.3 Step F) | The only listing that cannot drift from the gate, because it *is* the gate. The minimal form of AD-11's held-request listing, pulled forward by AM-13 because the cutover is when it matters most. |

### The cutover default: lazy drain plus a primed head (AM-14, binding — the single-pass recommendation is WITHDRAWN)

Revision 2 recorded, correctly at the time, that *"the architect's recommendation is the single pass"*. **That recommendation is withdrawn and that sentence is removed from this document.** Three things changed, each sufficient on its own: the volume is ≥93 rather than "far smaller than 46" (AF-6, and now **≥97** — §2.7.3); the per-issue act is **two acts, not one** (§2.7.4, E-5 accepted in full); and the pass **cannot be sized before it is performed**. At two acts per issue a full pass is **≥194 GitHub UI acts** of undetermined necessary size — which is not a recommendation but an open-ended request. **And the figure grows with the backlog**: the arithmetic that withdrew the recommendation gets worse, never better, with every issue filed.

**One sentence in Amendment 2's H1 became true only in revision 4, and it is worth saying so rather than letting its survival read as evidence it was always fine.** H1 item 2 assures the human that the number of issues actually needing an act *"is knowable only by running the gate, which is what S2 ships."* **That was not true as designed**: a gate that evaluated 25 of 97 records in API order could not report the queue's size at all. With AM-19's ranked walk, AM-22's pagination and AM-20's `scanned`/`evaluated`/`gated`/`unevaluated`/`complete` fields, **it is now true** — the gate evaluates the ranked set to the ceiling and reports exactly what it did and did not look at. Had P-1 been ruled differently the sentence would have had to be struck from H1.

> **The default cutover is the lazy drain.** AM-3 already ruled it safe and nothing changes that: an issue nobody is about to build costs nothing, and the authorizing act is performed at the moment the human decides the issue is next — an act they already perform implicitly by deciding it.
>
> **Plus a primed head, bounded by intent rather than by the backlog.** Immediately before S2 merges, the human authorizes only the issues they actually want built in the first days after merge — the working expectation is **single digits**, and **#1539 and #1540 themselves are the obvious first entries**. This is not a subset of a pass; it is a different act with a different justification. A pass drains a queue; a primed head keeps the worker fed while the drain happens lazily. It costs a handful of acts and removes the one failure the pass existed to prevent.
>
> **AM-13 remains the backstop and is unchanged — but it is now load-bearing, not precautionary.** The gate's `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` line is what makes the lazy drain safe to choose: a fully-gated queue announces itself instead of looking like an empty backlog. With the pass withdrawn, the line is the only remaining brace. **A reviewer who proposes dropping it — as logging polish, as noise, or as "the summary line already says `gated=N`" — is proposing to make this ruling unsafe**, and should be answered with that sentence rather than with a discussion of log volume. §7.1's DEV-2 (removing `2>/dev/null`) carries the same status for the same reason.

**How large the primed head should be, and which issues are in it, is the human's** — the architect set the working expectation at single digits and named the two obvious entries, and declined to go further. What the human is being asked to clear in ESC-6 item 2 is **the standing per-issue obligation at its true price (two acts), not a cutover plan** (Amendment 2 §3(b) item 2).

**One interaction the coder and the panel should both have in front of them, recorded rather than escalated.** The primed head *suppresses* the `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` block for as long as it has work: the block fires only when `eligible == 0 and gated > 0` (§2.3 Step F), so with five primed issues and ninety gated ones the loud line stays silent and only the summary's `gated=<G>` field carries the fact. That is **not** the failure AM-13 guards against — during that window the worker is doing real work, so the repo cannot be mistaken for dead — and the count is never actually hidden, because the summary line always carries `gated=<G>`. It is nonetheless the window in which the mechanism is least visible, and it ends exactly when the head is exhausted. I considered raising it as a new escalation and concluded it does not meet the bar: nothing in it contradicts a ruling and no contract text has to change. It was handed to the panel as **§9/R8** in revision 3; **in revision 4 R8 is folded into §9.2's R7**, with the question I put to the panel carried across unchanged and unanswered (§9.3).

> **Revision 4 improves this window without closing it, and the improvement is worth stating because it changes what an operator can see rather than what the gate does.** Revision 3's version of this note said that during the primed-head window *"only the summary's `gated=<G>` field carries the fact."* **That is no longer the whole of it: AM-20 adds `evaluated`, `unevaluated` and `complete=<yes|no>` to the same line** (§2.3 Step F), so the continuous signal during the window is materially richer — an operator reading the summary now sees not only how many records are held but **whether the gate finished looking**, which is the fact revision 3 had no field for at all. **The structural concern survives untouched**, and I am not claiming otherwise: the loud `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` block is still conditional on `eligible == 0`, so the design's most visible control is still the one that switches off exactly when there is work. **A design whose visibility control is conditional on having no work is still the shape the panel should decide on.**

**§2.7.4 states what each of these acts actually is** — including the route that is now forbidden.

### 2.7.4 The already-applied-signal problem — raised as §8/E-5 in revision 2, **RULED by AM-14/AM-15/AM-16** in revision 3

> **REVISION 5: AR-7 REMOVES ONE HALF OF THE VALVE THIS SECTION WAS WRITTEN AROUND, and the removal simplifies the human's instructions rather than complicating them.** Through revision 4 the valve was *"the dispatch label **or** the target milestone"*. **`pm-agent`'s AR-7 narrows it to one:** *"A `milestoned` event MUST NOT authorize anything. An authorization is established by exactly one signal: a `labeled` event, for the dispatch label being queried, whose GitHub-reported actor is a verified individual human CODEOWNER."* **AM-15's own sentence at `:133` — *"a human placing a new issue into the target milestone still authorizes it"* — is SUPERSEDED, and the architect records it as theirs and withdraws it** (AM-35). **Measured cost of the narrowing: zero of 98 candidates de-authorized** (`pm-agent` §0.2). **Held for Q11**, which the human has not answered; AR-7 **fails closed** — a "no" *restores* a channel rather than un-building one — so revision 5 is built on it exactly as `pm-agent` permits.
>
> **What this does to the section below: the two-act problem is UNCHANGED and the route table gets SHORTER.** The already-applied-signal deadlock was never about which channel; it is about idempotency, and it applies identically to a single channel. What changes is that the forbidden milestone route is no longer *forbidden-but-mechanically-valid* — **it is inert**, which is a strictly stronger reason not to document it, and one that survives a reader who disagrees with the Step 0 race argument.

AM-1's valve is that the human applies **the dispatch label** from their own account, producing a fresh `labeled` event whose actor is a verified human CODEOWNER. On a *new* issue that works, and #1643 is the proof.

**On every issue in the cutover set it does not, and the reason is structural — and AR-7 makes it sharper rather than smaller.** The cutover set is *by definition* the set of issues that **already carry the dispatch label** — that is what the gate queries. GitHub's label writes are idempotent: applying a label an issue already carries produces no new `labeled` event. So for every issue in the cutover set, **the one qualifying signal is already present and cannot be freshly applied.** There is no additive act available. The web UI will not even offer the label as addable — it offers removal. **Revision 4 could at least observe that a second channel existed and was equally blocked; under AR-7 there is no second channel to observe, so the remove-then-re-add is not merely the best route — it is the ONLY route, and a design that made it awkward would be a design with no authorization path for the existing queue at all.**

The human's actual options — **ruled by AM-15, which forbids one of them outright:**

| Route | Acts | Permitted as an authorization route? | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Remove `needs-ai`, then re-add it, from their own account** | 2 | **YES — and it is the only route.** | Produces `unlabeled` (human) then `labeled` (human). S2 ignores reversal events, so the fresh `labeled` authorizes. It also survives AM-8's S3 clearing rule, which re-establishes on "a fresh qualifying event by a verified human CODEOWNER" — which this is. **Its window is uncontested by any automation in this repo as it stands today**, and that is a verified mechanical finding rather than an assumption: the issue keeps its milestone throughout, so Step 0 — the only path that applies the label unprompted — never sees it; and the only *other* site in the entire repo that adds `needs-ai` is `label-swap.yml:74` (`/approve`), which fires only on a human comment. Both facts are needed; neither alone is sufficient. (Architect-verified; §9/R9 attacks the "as it stands today" qualifier.) |
| ~~Remove the milestone, then re-apply it~~ | 2 | **NO — FORBIDDEN as an authorization route (AM-15, binding).** | Revision 2 called this "riskier" and said "prefer the label route." **That understates it, and the reason is mechanical:** `bin/hos-cron:1018-1024` fetches `issues?milestone=none&state=open` every worker cycle, and `worker-cron-prompt.md:33-43` Step 0 triages **every** milestone-less issue — assigning a milestone *and* applying `needs-ai`, **both as `hos-worker-hos[bot]`**. The instant the human removes the milestone the issue becomes eligible for that sweep, so the next cycle may consume the human's remaining additive act on **both** channels, leaving the issue un-authorizable through either until a further remove-then-re-add. **New in revision 4 (AM-27 point 2): this prohibition's durable home is `docs/LABELS.md`, with the Step 0 race as the stated reason, and §6.3's `test_labels_doc_*` family gains a test pinning it.** Revision 3 left it with no documentation home and no test — and §4.3's four required `docs/LABELS.md` statements did not include it. **A prohibition whose only home is one-shot clearance text (H1) is a prohibition that expires the moment the human answers**, and this is the one the design's own failure-mode (ix) is about. **REVISION 5 — the row's status changes from FORBIDDEN-BUT-VALID to INERT (AR-7 / AM-35), and the change strengthens it.** Revision 4 had to say *"the milestone channel remains a valid authorization source in the gate: a human placing a new issue into the target milestone still authorizes it"* — a sentence that made the prohibition a matter of **operational advice**, defeasible by anyone who judged the Step 0 race unlikely. **That sentence is withdrawn: AM-15 `:133` is superseded by AR-7 and the architect records it as their own error.** A milestone assignment now carries **no authorization weight at all, on a new issue or an old one**, so the route is not merely risky — **it does nothing**, which is the one form of instruction AM-1 says is worse than no instruction. **The `docs/LABELS.md` wording must therefore say the milestone does not authorize, not merely that the route must not be used** (§4.3), and the Step 0 race is retained as the *historical* reason the route was attractive rather than as the reason it is refused. **The human is being asked to ratify exactly this narrowing at Q11 and has not answered; AR-7 fails closed, so the design is built on it.** |
| Comment `/approve` | 0 | **NO — it does nothing at all** (§2.7.2) | **Changed in revision 4 (AM-25).** Through revision 3 this row read *"1 act — NO, and it is actively harmful"*: `label-swap.yml:29` has no label-state precondition, so on an issue not yet carrying the dispatch label `/approve` applied it as `github-actions[bot]`, **consuming the human's one genuinely additive act.** **S2 now makes the command inert** — it writes no label, so it consumes nothing and it strips no `needs-human` (which is P-5's fix). It posts a comment naming the act that does authorize. The act count is **0**: it neither authorizes nor costs. |
| `edit_issue.sh --app human` | 1 | **NO** — §2.7.1 | Writes as `scottthurlow-claude[bot]`, which `is_bot_reviewer` excludes. It does nothing, visibly succeeds, and no helper script can ever fix this — any such tool would run under an App identity. |

**The instruction, written verbatim as AM-15 binds it.** This wording — not a paraphrase of it — is what goes into **each of the three artefacts named below**, and into anything else that tells a human how to authorize an issue:

> **"Cutover material" is RETIRED as a term (AM-27 point 1, binding — new in revision 4).** The panel found the phrase used eleven times across this design and both prior amendments and **never defined once**. **A binding instruction with no named landing site is a binding instruction that lands nowhere.** It resolves to **exactly two durable files plus one transient**, and every occurrence in this document is replaced by the specific file it means:
> 1. **`docs/LABELS.md`** — the dispatch label's meaning, its writers, and the authorizing act. **The reference home.** This is where a reader who wants to know what the label *is* will look.
> 2. **`docs/OVERSIGHT-RUNBOOK.md`** — the operator's procedure. **The procedural home** (AM-25). This is where a reader who wants to know what to *do* at 3am will look.
> 3. **The S2 PR body** — the one-time cutover note, including AM-26's `machine-accounts.env` precondition for consumers. **Transient by design**: it is read once, by the reviewer and by whoever performs the primed head, and it is not a place a durable rule may live alone.
>
> Where an amendment of the architect's used the phrase, it means these three.
>
> **THE ABSENCE TEST IS RE-SCOPED IN REVISION 5, and the re-scoping is a ruling about this repository's audit trail rather than about a test (AM-38, binding).** Revision 4 specified `test_no_document_uses_the_phrase_cutover_material` as *"a repo-wide absence assertion over `docs/v0.7.0/**`"*. **The phrase survives 11 times across 6 files**, including the **immutable run-1 panel artifact** and `REQUIREMENTS-1540-AMENDMENT-1`'s FR29 text. **As specified, the test cannot pass without editing other authors' accepted rulings and a historical artifact.**
>
> > **The general rule AM-38 issues, which is larger than this test and is quoted because it now governs this whole chain:** *"no ACCEPTED document in this chain — ADR, amendment, requirements document, technical-design revision or panel artifact — may be edited after acceptance except to correct a factual error in it, and any such correction must be recorded in the document that makes it. **Amendments supersede; they do not overwrite.**"*
>
> **Binding: the assertion's scope is the files S2 actually edits, and nothing else.** The occurrences in accepted historical documents stay exactly where they are. **AM-27 point 1's purpose — that the phrase be *defined* and not used *loosely in new text* — is met by that scope; a repo-wide sweep was never what it needed**, and a test that can only pass by rewriting history is a test that will be satisfied by rewriting history. §6.3 carries the re-scoped assertion.
>
> **One consequence for this document, applied rather than noted.** Revisions 1–4 of *this* file are superseded by revision 5 **in place**, because a technical design is a living contract and not an accepted historical artifact — but **the revision history in §0.0 is append-only under the same rule**, which is why §0.0's revision-4 coverage map keeps its four defective rows with a correction paragraph beside them (RP-22) rather than being quietly fixed.

> **To authorize an issue, apply the dispatch label to it from your own GitHub account** (web UI, mobile app, or a personal-access-token `gh` invocation under your own login — **never** `edit_issue.sh --app human`, which writes as `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` and does nothing).
