> **If the issue already carries the label** — which every issue already in the queue does, and which you will notice because the UI offers you *removal* rather than addition — **remove it first, then re-add it.** The remove-then-re-add is not undoing anything: the pair of events it writes, both under your own account, *is* the authorization record the gate reads.

**Why it is worded that way, and why it must not be "simplified".** It requires no prior knowledge of GitHub's idempotency behaviour and is **correct whichever way §0.6 gap 6 resolves**, because it routes on what the UI actually offers rather than on a fact this repo has not verified (AF-7). That is deliberate, and it is the property that makes an open verification gap non-blocking. A later editor who shortens it back to "apply the `needs-ai` label" — reasonably, on the grounds that the long version is fussy — reinstates precisely the silently-does-nothing approval path AM-1 forbids: the human performs it, the UI accepts it, nothing happens, and the issue stays gated with no signal that the act failed.

**My "it looks like churn" objection is answered, and the answer belongs in the documentation rather than in this document alone.** I raised (E-5 question 2) that a remove-then-re-add reads to a later observer like undoing and redoing nothing, and that the milestone route might be more *legible* despite being riskier. The first half is wrong on its own terms and the second is settled by the route table above. **An `unlabeled` followed by a `labeled`, both by `ScottThurlow`, is not an undo-redo — it is the authorization record.** It is the only durable, GitHub-reported, unforgeable evidence that a human CODEOWNER authorized this specific issue at this specific time, which is exactly what AD-4 says authorization *is*. A reader who sees it as churn has misread the mechanism, and **the fix for that is one sentence in `docs/LABELS.md`, not a quieter act** — which is why §4.3 now makes that sentence a required element of the S2 change set and §6.3 pins it with a test.

**S3's relationship to this, now ruled (AM-16):** a `/approve` comment *is* a single act available on an already-labelled issue, and that is the strongest ergonomic argument for AM-6 point 4's comment-reading that exists — but it is **not pulled forward**, for the three reasons in §2.7.2, and **E-5 is recorded in the S3 work item as that ruling's ergonomic justification**. S2 stays event-only.

---

## 3. Fail-closed matrix

Every row is a test case (§6). **"Gated"** = the gate looked and the request is not a candidate. **"exit 2"** = fail closed, no candidates at all this cycle, and the caller must treat it as "unknown, do not skip the cycle". **"exit 3"** (new in revision 5) = the gate ran, emitted nothing, and **cannot say there is nothing** — see §2.2. **Revision 7 (RP4-2): where a non-exit-2 row states "exit 0" for a per-record or configuration outcome (F6, F14, F30 before its amendment), read it as "not an exit-2 condition".** The process exit for every such row is §2.2's single rule: exit 3 iff `complete=no` AND stdout is empty, otherwise exit 0. No row overrides it.

> **The matrix is read against AM-31's per-harm criterion, which REPLACES AM-21's enumerable-and-ordered one.** *Security-affecting → exit 2. Completeness- or ordering-affecting → report and continue.* **Three rows change class under it and no others: F13 (from exit 2 to quarantine-and-continue), F33 (from an exception-under-protest to the rule), and F32 (out of the `WARN` class).** Every Step B configuration row keeps exit 2 unchanged — **a reader who generalises "quarantine and continue" to Step B has removed the gate's foundation** (AM-31's "What this is NOT").
>
> **Row-count bookkeeping, because a coder counting rows is how a missing test is found.** **Live rows: 34** — F1, F2, F3, F4, F4b, F4c, F5–F14, F14b, F16, F18, F19, F20, F22–F26, F30–F37. **Retired stubs, retained so older cross-references land on a ruling rather than on nothing: F15** (replaced by F31/F32 — *"a subset is not sufficient, a prefix is"*), **F17, F21, F27, F28, F29** (all retired as spent by AR-7 / AM-35). **A stub has no test.**

| # | Failure mode | Component | Outcome | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **F1** | `.github/CODEOWNERS` absent | gate Step B2 | **exit 2** **`codeowners-file-absent`** + remediation line | No actor can ever authorize; running is pointless and silence is dangerous. **Token and remediation line changed in revision 5 (AM-34 point 1)** |
| **F2** | CODEOWNERS present and readable, **names no individual human** (only `org/team` patterns) | gate Step B2 | **exit 2** **`codeowners-no-individual-human`** + remediation line | Teams are not individual humans (AF-2). **This row is why AM-34 exists.** The halt is **correct and stays** — a gate that cannot possibly authorize anything should refuse to run rather than emit `eligible=0` forever while appearing to work — **but for the common enterprise configuration the consumer's file is FINE and the old shared token told them it was broken.** The remediation line must say team handles confer no authorization authority. **NOT team-handle resolution** (AM-34's "What this is NOT"): expanding `@org/core-devs` via the teams API would move the authorizer set from a committed file into un-versioned org settings — the exact objection H3's architect recommendation rested on |
| F3 | CODEOWNERS present, **unreadable** (permission) | `codeowners_humans` raises → gate | **exit 2** **`codeowners-unreadable`** | Not `FileNotFoundError`; never degrade to "empty and carry on". **Distinct from F1 and F2 in revision 5:** absence, unsupported-but-valid, and unreadable are three different facts and must not share a token (§1.3.1's governing rule) |
| **F4** | `machine-accounts.env` absent | gate Step B1/B3 | **exit 2** **`machine-accounts-file-absent`** + remediation line | **AM-34 point 2.** The file is **not shipped** (absent from `framework_consumer_files.txt`), **not produced by any installer**, and `provision_agent_account.sh:56` dies without it — **so after S2 its absence stops work selection entirely in a deployment that ran fine without it before.** The remediation line names the four variables. **Verified present in this repo** (§0.0.4), so the precondition is satisfied here today; the row is about **consumer** deployments |
| F4b | present, but the bot set is empty after the env union | gate Step B1 | **exit 2** `bot-accounts-empty` | House precedent: `require_human_approval.py` exits 2 on empty `BOT_ACCOUNTS`. **An empty denylist promotes every bot to "human"** — §1.3.1 names this the most dangerous loader row |
| F4c | present, but naming none of the three HOS App roles | gate Step B3 | **exit 2** `trusted-apps-empty` | AD-1(b) has no members; the marker table can admit nothing |
| F5 | `BOT_ACCOUNTS` env set to `""` | `load_bot_accounts` | file baseline used; **not** empty | Env may only *add* to the denylist (AD-13) |
| F6 | `trusted-requesters.txt` absent | `load_trusted_requesters` | empty roster; **exit 0**, gate runs | FR3: mechanism functions with CODEOWNERS + apps |
| F7 | roster present but unreadable | `load_trusted_requesters` raises | **exit 2** `roster-unreadable` | Only `FileNotFoundError` is benign |
| F8 | roster line malformed (missing who/when/why) | parser | line **skipped** + stderr diagnostic | A malformed roster grants nothing |
| F9 | roster lists a `[bot]` login | parser | entry **rejected** + diagnostic | Otherwise the roster is a route around AD-2 |
| F10 | `--milestone` non-numeric (incl. an unsubstituted `@@MILESTONE_NUMBER@@`) | Step A | **exit 2** | Never coerce a placeholder |
| F11 | `--repo` malformed | Step A | **exit 2** | — |
| F12 | list query: `gh` non-zero / non-JSON / not a list | Step C | **exit 2** `list-query-failed` | A failed query is "unknown", and unknown must not read as "empty and go" at the *gate* (`hos-cron`'s own gate still treats it as unknown for the cycle-skip decision) |
| **F13** | events query: `gh` non-zero / unparseable, for one record | **Step D5.3** | **REPLACED IN REVISION 5 — the record is QUARANTINED and the walk CONTINUES.** `unevaluated:query-failed`, **reported with the issue number**, **never counted as `gated`**, `complete=no`. Exit **0**, or **3** if the walk ends with nothing to emit | **AM-31 REPLACES AM-21, and this is the single row that changes.** The determination is **per-record**: one query per record, no shared state, failure direction *not authorized*. *"A failed events query on record X cannot corrupt record Y's determination, and it cannot authorize X. So exit 2 there buys **zero** security and costs **all** new issue work."* **`probe.py:298-303` already ships the quarantine; the gate's exit 2 was the novel behaviour.** What AM-21's exit 2 protected — *"a partial list can silently demote a `priority:critical` item"* — is an **ordering** concern, and under AM-20's vocabulary it is no longer silent. **A reader arriving from an older cross-reference to "F13 stands exactly as written" has arrived at the row that was replaced** |
| F14 | events pagination bound (10 pages) exhausted, no match | Step D5.2 → D5.3 | candidate **gated**, exit 0, **WARN `events-page-bound-reached issue=#<n>`** | Not an error; fail-closed for that issue only. **This is a determination** about one record and counts toward `gated`, not `unevaluated`; it truncates nothing in the walk's order. **Distinguish it from F13 carefully: the bound exhausted means the gate LOOKED and found nothing (gated); a transport failure means the gate could not look (unevaluated).** Collapsing them is the conflation AM-20 exists to remove |
| **F14b** | the **request budget clamp** cuts a record's events fetch short **before a match** (§1.4 rule 4(a) defines "bites") | Step D5.2 → D5.1 | ~~candidate **gated**, exit 0, **WARN `events-page-bound-reached issue=#<n>` + `WARN budget-clamped-events-fetch issue=#<n>`**~~ **REPLACED IN REVISION 7 (TP-2 / RP4-1(i)):** the record is **`unevaluated:cost-ceiling`, NEVER gated**, and is named on `unevaluated:cost-ceiling issue=#<n>`. `WARN budget-clamped-events-fetch issue=#<n>` fires. **`WARN events-page-bound-reached` does NOT fire.** **The walk STOPS: this is F32's stop, and no further record is admitted, trusted or otherwise.** `complete=no`. Exit 0, or **3** if nothing was emitted | **NEW in revision 5 (AM-30); REPLACED in revision 7.** ~~Identical outcome to F14 for the record~~. Revision 5 called the two outcomes identical for the record. They are not: F14 is a determination (the gate read 1,000 events and found no qualifying actor), and F14b is the absence of one (the gate stopped reading because the cycle's budget ran out). Counting F14b as gated put a record a complete walk may authorize into *"held for human authorization"*, and made its verdict depend on walk position. **Test: §6.2's `test_budget_clamp_that_bites_is_unevaluated_not_gated_and_stops_the_walk`.** A clamped fetch that finds a match is eligible (§1.4 rule 4(d)); one that reaches the end of the history is F14's determination |
| **F15** | **~~authorization-check cap reached~~ — REPLACED in revision 4, and the rule that replaced it was itself REPLACED in revision 5** | — | — | **AM-21 replaced this row; AM-31 has since replaced AM-21's criterion, and AM-30 has since re-denominated the bound F31/F32 express. The stub therefore points forward twice and that is deliberate — a reader arriving here from a revision-3 cross-reference needs both hops.** Its stated rationale — *"the partial answer is always a **subset** of the eligible set"* — was **the wrong property**: a subset is not sufficient, a **prefix** is. See F31/F32 for the two stops that replace it, and §2.3 D5.1 for why the distinction is the whole of P-1. The row is retained as a stub rather than deleted, so that a reader arriving from an older document's cross-reference to F15 lands on the correction instead of on nothing |
| F16 | `issue.user` absent or `login` empty | `requester_verdict` | **gated** (`no-login`) | Never authorize an unidentified author |
| **~~F17~~** | ~~issue record has no `milestone` object~~ | — | — | **RETIRED AS SPENT in revision 5 (AR-7 / AM-35). The row is kept as a stub rather than deleted, so a reader arriving from an older cross-reference lands on the retirement instead of on nothing** — the same treatment F15 received. **AM-18's stated purpose was *"never authorize blind, and never fall back to the labeled channel"*; with no milestone channel, the milestone object is never read for authorization and there is nothing to fall back from.** Retaining the branch would ship a fail-closed guard over a decision that no longer exists, **plus a new stderr surface in a module that writes none.** The invariant itself is still true — Step C queries `&milestone=<n>` server-side — and **an invariant with no consumer needs no guard.** Retired, **not overturned**: AM-18 was correct for as long as AM-4's derive-the-title-from-the-record construction existed, and §8/E-7's withdrawal is **not** reopened by its retirement |
| F18 | Author is a trusted app, title has no marker | `requester_verdict` | **gated** (`trusted-app-no-machine-filing-marker`) | AD-2 |
| F19 | Author is untrusted; `needs-ai` applied by the worker bot | Step D5.3 | **gated** | VF-3 — the self-authorization the gate exists to close. *(Step renumbered in revision 5 — RP-17)* |
| F20 | Author is untrusted; `needs-ai` applied by `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` | Step D5.3 | **gated** | AD-6: a bot approval never satisfies the gate, **including the human-proxy App**. **This is the 49-record block §8/E-10 is about, and it is the block the literal reading of the human's fast-path directive would exempt** |

| **~~F28~~** | ~~milestone title differing only in case, whitespace, or an em dash~~ | — | — | **RETIRED AS SPENT (AM-35), stub retained.** AM-4 was **correct** — the panel's 97/97 `{"title"}` measurement showed byte-for-byte title equality was the only binding available — and it is retired because the channel it bound is deleted. **The measurement is PRESERVED** in §0.6 gap 5's retirement note and travels to S3's `demilestoned` rule (AM-37) |
| **~~F29~~** | ~~milestone renamed after a CODEOWNER authorized under the old title~~ | — | — | **RETIRED AS SPENT (AM-35), with residual **R-7** (§7.2). Milestone renames now de-authorize nothing, because a milestone authorizes nothing.** This is one of five residuals that went with the single channel AR-7 removed (§1.4) |
| **F30** | Every in-milestone candidate is gated (`eligible==0`, `gated>0`) | gate Step F → `bin/hos-cron` §2.4 | ~~**exit 0, empty list**~~ **AMENDED IN REVISION 7 (RP4-2): empty list, and the exit is §2.2's exit-3 rule unmodified. Exit 0 when `complete=yes`; exit 3 when `complete=no`.** In both cases there is a distinct `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` stderr block and one `$LOG_PREFIX` cycle-log line, plus the fourth `INCOMPLETE` line when `complete=no` | **AM-13, amended by AM-20.** With `complete=yes` it is behaviourally identical to an empty milestone, deliberately, so visibility is the *only* thing distinguishing the repo's most likely operational failure from a quiet week. ~~Never an exit code~~ **The held condition is never its OWN exit code: it neither causes an exit nor changes one, because completeness decides the exit (corrected in revision 7).** Revision 6 said *"never an exit code"* and *"exit 0"* while covering `complete=no`, which contradicted §2.2's *"exit 3 IFF complete=no AND stdout empty"*. With the population past what one ceiling can walk (RUN4 M-1: 149 candidates, ≈145 paid), **every fully-gated cycle is `complete=no`**. So F30's exit 0 would have sent `bin/hos-cron` down the *"no actionable work"* path AM-32 exists to block, on the cutover's most common path. Never an issue. **New in revision 4: when `complete=no`, the block MUST carry the fourth `INCOMPLETE` line naming `<U>` and stating that `<G>` is a floor.** A held-everything line that claims a completeness the walk did not have is a false statement in the one place this design put its trust. **Test home: `tests/automation/test_hos_cron.py`** for the `$LOG_PREFIX` half (AM-28(a)), **which must now cover both exits: the prefixed line fires on exit 0 and on exit 3** (revision 7); `test_select_work_candidates.py` for the gate's own block and its exit (`test_all_gated_exit_follows_the_exit_3_rule`, renamed in revision 7) |
| **F31** | **Early exit: `eligible` reaches `--max-candidates` (default 5)** | Step D5.1 | **exit 0**, `unevaluated:sufficient`, `complete=no`, **no `WARN`**. **No further record admitted, trusted or otherwise** | **AM-19, narrowed by the human's 15:46:50Z directive 1.** Completeness-affecting only (AM-31), and the omission is the **ranked tail**, so nothing the stop leaves unreached can outrank anything admitted (*wording tightened in revision 7, RP4-1*). **Routine and expected — it is now the NORMAL termination of a healthy walk, not headroom**, which is why it is quiet. **Cannot coexist with F30** (`eligible ≥ 1` by definition), so it can never be the reason a held-everything count is wrong. **Default lowered 10 → 5** (`bin/hos-cron:1892-1895`'s own `head -5`) |
| **F32** | **Per-cycle request ceiling reached: `api_requests` would exceed 100** | Step D5.1 | **exit 0** (or **3** if nothing was emitted), `unevaluated:cost-ceiling`, `complete=no`, **and NO `WARN` — dropped from the WARN class in revision 5**. **No further record admitted, trusted or otherwise** | **AM-30 replaces AM-19's check-denominated ceiling; AM-31 supplies the exit criterion.** Completeness-affecting and **ordered** — the omission is the ranked tail. **Two changes from revision 4 and they point opposite ways.** (i) **The unit changes from checks to requests**: a "check" is 1–10 requests, so the old bound was 100–1,000 requests and *"a bound whose unit is not the rationed resource cannot bound the rationed resource."* **The value does not move.** (ii) **The `WARN` is removed**: AM-30 withdraws *"must not bind in normal operation"* as unsatisfiable, and **§0.0.4 measures the population at 101 against a ceiling of 100**, so the warning would have fired every cycle from merge onward — a banner, invisible by week two. **`api_requests <= effective_ceiling` is an invariant** (§2.2.1's counter table), which is what subsumes RP-21's off-by-one. **§8/E-8 is CLOSED by this ruling** |
| **F33** | **List-page bound reached: Step C returns 5 full pages** | Step C | **exit 0** (*or **3** if nothing is emitted: §2.2's rule, made explicit in revision 7 per RP4-2*), **`WARN candidate-list-truncated pages=5 — at least 500 records matched; the total is UNKNOWN`** — the **loudest** report class — **`unevaluated:list-truncated=yes`** (*a flag, not a count, and outside `U`: revision 7, TP-3*), `complete=no` | **AM-22 / AF-8, with its justification REPLACED by AM-31 and its report corrected.** Revision 4 granted this row exit 0 *"under protest"* as an exception to AM-21's enumerable-and-ordered criterion. **AM-31 replaces the criterion it was failing:** no truncated record can be wrongly *authorized*, only wrongly *omitted*, so the harm is completeness and exit 0 is **the rule, not a concession**. **The report is corrected in revision 5: the gate MUST NOT state `records=500`** — that is the number it *fetched*, in the position a reader expects the number that *matched*, and GitHub's `/issues` returns no total. The bound stays at **500** and is re-derived by AM-33: the list bound is cheap (≤5 requests), the request ceiling is expensive (≈100), and equalising them re-opens AF-8. This row is why **R-6 is closed and not carried** (§7.2) — **and §2.2.1 records that the 100-record ceiling R-6 preserved has now actually been crossed** |
| **F34** | **NEW (H3) — roster line names an unrecognised tier**, e.g. `tier:owner`, `tier:push` | `load_trusted_requesters` | line **skipped** + stderr diagnostic **naming the line number and listing the five legal values**; the rest of the roster loads | **The human's addendum fixes the legal set to GitHub's own five names — `read`/`triage`/`write`/`maintain`/`admin` — *"no translation layer, no ambiguity."*** An unrecognised name is a **malformed line, not an unknown tier**: it is never silently ignored and **never passed through to the API to see what happens**. Fails closed (it contributes nobody) and is loud (a roster whose behaviour differs from its text, with nothing recording the difference, is §1.3.1's named defect). Note `tier:Write` is **accepted** — tier tokens are matched case-insensitively after the prefix — and a bare `write` on its own line is **a login named `write`**, which is the one place the "no translation layer" instruction needed a design decision (§1.3) |
| **F35** | **NEW (H3) — collaborator-permission resolution fails** (non-zero exit, non-JSON, not a list, rate-limited, timed out) | `fetch_collaborators` → Step B5 | `tier_members = frozenset()`, **`WARN tier-resolution-failed tiers=<n>`**, `complete=no`, **the walk CONTINUES**. Exit **0**, or **3** if the gate ends with nothing to emit | **The only row in §1.3.1's table that does not propagate, and the asymmetry is the ruling rather than an exception to it.** The other configuration rows are *configuration*: a wrong trust, bot or CODEOWNER set changes **every** record's determination and can do so **permissively**, so AM-31 routes them to exit 2. Tier resolution is *membership*: its failure can only ever **shrink** the trusted set, putting **more** records through the authorization check. **Completeness-affecting → report and continue (AM-31); not silent → the determination is degraded (AM-32).** Treating it as a config failure would hand any transient GitHub 5xx a repo-wide stop — the precise RP-7 harm AM-31 removes, re-created in a new place. **§2.2 defines exactly when "degraded" becomes exit 3** |
| **F36** | **NEW (H3) — `COLLABORATOR_PAGE_BOUND` (3 pages / 300 collaborators) exhausted** | `fetch_collaborators` | resolution proceeds **over what was fetched**, **`WARN collaborator-page-bound-reached pages=3`**, `complete=no` | **Not an error.** Completeness-affecting and one-directional: an unfetched collaborator simply does not join `tier_members`, so the effect is to **under**-trust. **Costs zero when the roster lists no tier** — the fetch is not issued at all — which is every deployment today, including this one |
| **F37** | **NEW (H3) — a `[bot]` login is returned by the collaborators endpoint and matches a listed tier** | tier resolution, §1.3.2 | the login is **rejected** and does not join `tier_members` | **A GitHub App installed on a repository can appear in its collaborator list.** Without this guard, `tier:write` is a route around AD-2 — exactly what the roster's own `[bot]` rejection (F9) exists to close, arriving through a door AD-1(c) did not have when that rejection was written. **The guard is applied to what the API RETURNS, which is the only place it can be applied for a tier** |

---

## 4. Migration plan

### 4.1 `scripts/automation/lib/next_candidates.jq` — deleted, not wrapped

AD-3 permits "internal detail of the entry point **or** absorbed into it". **Absorb and delete.** Keeping it as a `jq -f` subprocess would put a `jq` runtime dependency on the live selection path; `tests/automation/test_next_candidates.py` already skips itself when `jq` is absent, so a `jq`-less machine would run the gate untested. Python is already a hard dependency of this path.

### 4.2 `tests/automation/test_next_candidates.py` — split, then deleted

AD-3 predicts the lock-step test "becomes vacuous". Half of it does; half of it becomes *more* load-bearing and must not be lost in the deletion.

| Existing test | Disposition |
|---|---|
| `TestPriorityOrdering::test_high_beats_low_across_number_inversion` | **Port 1:1** to `tests/framework/test_select_work_candidates.py`, same fixture, same assertion |
| `…::test_full_priority_ladder` | Port 1:1 |
| `…::test_tie_break_is_lowest_number_within_band` | Port 1:1 |
| `…::test_no_priority_label_defaults_to_low` | Port 1:1 |
| `…::test_default_low_label_rendered_as_low` | Port 1:1 — **including the exact expected line** `#894 [low] issue 894`, which is the output-format contract |
| `TestEligibilityFilter::test_needs_human_is_excluded` | Port 1:1 |
| `…::test_empty_input_yields_no_lines` | Port 1:1 |
| `…::test_all_blocked_yields_no_lines` | Port 1:1 |
| `…::test_missing_or_null_labels_does_not_crash` | Port 1:1 |
| `TestBothSelectionPathsAgree::test_filter_file_exists` | **Inverted** → `test_next_candidates_jq_is_gone` |
| `…::test_hos_cron_uses_canonical_filter` | **Replaced** → `test_hos_cron_invokes_the_entry_point` + `test_hos_cron_has_no_inline_jq_selection` |
| `…::test_cron_prompt_fallback_uses_canonical_filter` | **Replaced** → `test_cron_prompt_invokes_the_entry_point` + `test_cron_prompt_has_no_gh_api_candidate_query` |
| `…::test_both_paths_share_query_params` | **Retired as genuinely vacuous** — the query parameters now exist in exactly one place (the entry point). This is the one test AD-3's "becomes vacuous" actually describes. |

Every ported case gets a **trusted author** in its fixture, so ordering is tested in isolation from trust. The file `tests/automation/test_next_candidates.py` is then deleted. Porting is line-for-line: a reviewer must be able to diff the two files and see nine identical fixtures.

(b) **`scripts/framework/machine-accounts.env` must exist** — not shipped, not generated, and after S2 its absence stops work selection entirely; (c) **the personal-access-token route**, because *"a CLI-only operator is not stranded"* is only true if the artefact says so — **RP-9 is the requirement, not a defect** (AM-37), and this is its entire residue. **Which artefact carries it is the architect's and mine to choose and is NOT specified by `pm-agent`.** A satisfying route exists inside S2's current scope by construction: the two gate modules are themselves on that list by AM-26 part 1. **`docs/LABELS.md` and `docs/OVERSIGHT-RUNBOOK.md` discharge FR29(b) for the operating deployment and CANNOT discharge it for consumers** — no `docs/` path is on that list (§0.0.4), which is §9's **P4-b**. **REVISION 6 — Q12 IS ANSWERED and it confirms this row's scope exactly as written:** *"S2 needs only the small, pinned three-sentence FR29(b) artifact."* **The broader installer documentation-shipping fix is tracked as #1711 and does NOT block S2** (the human, #1540, 2026-09-16T19:56:31Z). **Two consequences.** (i) **This row's scope stops being a designer's triage that `pm-agent` and the architect happened to uphold, and becomes a ruling** — the scope question is closed and a proposal to widen it is a proposal to reverse a ruling. (ii) **§9.3's P4-b row finally has a landing site**: it was retired as *"real, pre-existing, NOT S2's, and stays a follow-up"* with no issue named, which is the same no-named-landing-site defect this chain has found three times. **#1711 is the name.** |
| `SCRIPTS-INDEX.md` | regenerate via `scripts/framework/regen_all.sh` |

**Protected-surface count for this table plus §2.1: FIVE — `scripts/framework/**`, `bin/**`, `bootstrap/**`, `.claude/agents/**`, `.github/workflows/**`.** Ratified by AM-17, **and confirmed unchanged by Amendment 3**, which adds `framework_consumer_files.txt` to surface 1's *change set* (a new file inside an existing surface, not a new surface) and changes surface 5's edit from a wording fix to an inert-command fix. `docs/LABELS.md`, **`docs/OVERSIGHT-RUNBOOK.md`**, **`tests/automation/test_hos_cron.py`** and `SCRIPTS-INDEX.md` are edited too and are **not** protected surfaces, which is why they do not raise the count. `CLAUDE.md` (option D) would be a sixth and is deliberately **out of S1/S2** (§2.7.1, confirmed by AM-17) — named to the human as a planned follow-up so that its later arrival is expected rather than a surprise. None of the five is pre-authorized by this document (AD-14).

### 4.4 Ordering and revertability

S1 and S2 are separate PRs in that order. **S1 is still not describable as "behaviour-preserving for every existing caller" — and in revision 5 the reason CHANGES from a narrowing to a deletion, which is a larger consumer-visible change, not a smaller one.**

> **Revision 4's statement was that AM-4 narrowed `probe.py`'s milestone channel (title equality, inverted default). Revision 5's is that AR-7 / AM-35 DELETES it.** A consumer running `probe.py`'s milestone strategy today has issues authorized by `milestoned` events; **after S1 those events authorize nothing, for any title.** Revision 4's change would have gated the *subset* whose milestone title did not match; **revision 5's gates all of them.** The direction is the same — strictly stricter, fail-closed — and the magnitude is larger, so the honest statement is that S1's blast radius on the shipped path **grew** while S1's own code shrank (§1.6). **Both facts are true and stating only the second would be the more flattering half.**

**S1's live effects, restated for revision 5:** `probe.py`'s stricter `bot_accounts` default (DEV-3, strictly stricter and identical when the env is set); **the deletion of the `milestoned` authorization arm and its parameter** (AM-35); and AM-5's bounded pagination. **All three are on a path with no production caller in this repo** — `multi_customer.py` is the only caller of `probe.py` and is not on the live intake path — **but with real behaviour change for any consumer running the milestone strategy.** §7.3 routes the affected sign-offs accordingly, and the re-review's *content* changes with the ruling: it is now a review of a function whose milestone arm is **absent**, not one whose milestone arm is title-bound, and **a re-review written against AM-4's criterion would be checking for a behaviour that no longer exists.** S2 remains the larger behaviour change.

If S2 must be reverted, reverting it restores `next_candidates.jq` and both call sites, and leaves S1 in place harmlessly — so the revert is a single-PR revert, not an unwind. **Reverting S2 does not restore `/approve`'s pre-S2 meaning**, and should not: the `label-swap.yml` and `docs/LABELS.md` corrections (§2.7.2) describe a fact about `github-actions[bot]` that was always true and that AF-5 merely discovered. They are correct with or without the gate, and must survive a revert.

### 4.5 Sequencing against `ADR-1604` (AM-9, binding)

**S2 ships first.** It is the live-path fix for an open `priority:critical` (#1539) and the original ADR §3 makes it the lead slice; #1604 is `priority:high` and nothing in it is blocked by waiting — its other components (branch ownership, breaker rungs, the stuck-set query) are untouched by S2.

1. **`ADR-1604` AD-5's mechanism bullet is amended** (by the architect, in AM-9 — not by this document, which had no standing to do it): the queue exclusion is expressed **once**, as an entry in `select_work_candidates.py`'s `EXCLUDED_LABELS` named module constant. **No inlined twin** in `worker-cron-prompt.md` and **no lock-step divergence test**, because after S2 there is no second implementation to diverge from — which is the whole point of AD-3. AD-5's **semantics are unchanged and still binding**: one narrow marker, authoritative for both the count and the exclusion; the generic human-attention label stays advisory and is never counted. Only the *exclusion* half lands in the gate (§2.2).
2. **Orphaned sign-offs, to be re-reviewed before #1604 is built:** `TECHNICAL-DESIGN-1604` §4.6 (Component H), its §4.7 component-table row for Component H, and its test row for `tests/automation/test_next_candidates.py`. All three were approved against a contract in which `next_candidates.jq` exists and is the single source of truth. The rest of `TECHNICAL-DESIGN-1604`'s sign-offs stand — the amendment touches the *location* of one exclusion, not the mechanism it implements.
3. **Whoever picks up #1604 next must carry the amendment into that document as the first step of the work**, not as a cleanup afterwards: re-point §4.6/§4.7/tests and note AD-5's amended bullet.
4. **The other admissible ordering, stated because it must not be improvised at the time.** If #1604 somehow reaches build before S2 merges, it ships against `next_candidates.jq` unchanged and **S2 absorbs the two exclusions during its deletion**. That ordering is permitted, is the only other admissible one, and **must be stated in whichever PR lands second** so a reviewer of the second PR is not left inferring which contract it was written against. What is *not* admissible under either ordering is both artefacts existing at once — that is the #1135 duplicate-implementation defect this design exists to remove.

## 5. Interface summary (the contract a coder implements against)

```
# scripts/framework/requester_trust.py  — protected surface; no imports from
# scripts/automation/** or scripts/oversight/**

codeowners_humans(repo_root: str = ".") -> set[str]
load_trusted_apps(repo_root: str = ".") -> set[str]
load_bot_accounts(repo_root: str = ".") -> set[str]

load_trusted_requesters(repo_root: str = ".") -> RosterPolicy    # CHANGED in rev 5 (H3)
    # RosterPolicy(logins: frozenset[str], tiers: frozenset[str])
    # was: -> set[str].  A caller treating the result as a set of logins now gets
    # a dataclass and fails loudly, which is the intended migration signal.

fetch_collaborators(repo: str) -> Any                            # NEW in rev 5 (H3)
    # THE ONLY NETWORK CALL IN THIS MODULE, and it is a FETCH WRAPPER, not a
    # predicate.  `gh api --paginate repos/<repo>/collaborators?per_page=100`,
    # bounded by COLLABORATOR_PAGE_BOUND.  ONE call per CYCLE for the whole
    # gate, never one per candidate.  Any failure -> the caller passes None.

load_trusted_set(repo_root: str = ".", collaborators: Any = None) -> TrustedSet
    # `collaborators` is an ALREADY-FETCHED payload, exactly as
    # verify_codeowner_actor takes an already-fetched events list.
    # load_trusted_set performs NO network I/O.  collaborators=None means
    # "no tier resolution was performed" -> tier_members = frozenset(),
    # which is also the value on ANY resolution failure, and is why probe.py
    # needs no change to keep working.

is_trusted_requester(login: str, user_type: str, trusted_set: TrustedSet) -> tuple[bool, str]
machine_filing_marker(title: str) -> MachineFilingMarker | None
requester_verdict(record: Mapping[str, Any], trusted_set: TrustedSet) -> RequesterVerdict

verify_codeowner_actor(
    events: Any,
    codeowners_humans: set[str],
    bot_accounts: set[str],
    label_name: str,
) -> str | None
    # FOUR ARGUMENTS.  `expected_milestone_title` is DELETED, not defaulted and
    # not sentinelled (AR-7 / AM-35): "a sentinel leaves a live code path that a
    # single call-site edit re-enables."  There is ONE authorizing signal — a
    # `labeled` event, for `label_name`, whose GitHub-reported actor is a
    # verified individual human CODEOWNER.  THERE IS NO SECOND ARM.
    # `tier_members` is ABSENT from this argument list BY CONSTRUCTION: requester
    # trust never confers authorization authority (§1.4, pinned by §6.1).

MACHINE_FILING_MARKERS: tuple[MachineFilingMarker, ...]
    # closed against sub-issue filing (AM-10 / D-7); entries are removed, never
    # relaxed, when an emitting site becomes LLM-composed (D-6 trigger 2)
PERMISSION_TIERS = ("read", "triage", "write", "maintain", "admin")   # NEW, H3
    # GitHub's OWN permission-level names, used directly.  NO MAPPING TABLE, and
    # there must never be one (the human's 07:16:58Z addendum).  Matched against
    # each collaborator record's `role_name`, case-insensitively, EXACTLY — never
    # hierarchically — and NOT against the `permissions` object, whose vocabulary
    # is pull/triage/push/maintain/admin and which would silently fail every
    # `read` and `write` entry.  §0.6 gap 8: this payload is UNOBSERVED.
COLLABORATOR_PAGE_BOUND = 3                # 300 collaborators.  NOT a flag.
EXCLUDED_LABELS is NOT here — it belongs to the gate (selection policy, not trust),
    and is the single bound landing site for ADR-1604 AD-5's exclusions (AM-9, §4.5)
```

```
# scripts/framework/select_work_candidates.py — protected surface; CLI entry point
#
# exit 0 = a determination whose extent the gate can STATE, with candidates
#          emitted OR with complete=yes. The SET on stdout is exactly the first
#          k records the ranked WALK over THE FETCHED SET DETERMINED eligible,
#          in walk order (rev 7: a quarantined record has no determination,
#          is named on its own line, and forces complete=no) — and the first
#          k of the complete answer IFF complete=yes (AM-33's words; §8/E-12).
#          stdout PRESENTS that set in
#          the EMISSION order, so stdout is NOT itself a prefix of the walk.
#          (AM-19 as scoped by AM-33, RESTATED in rev 6 as a correction under
#           the human's TP-4 CLASSIFICATION, on the panel's recommended limb —
#           attribution corrected in rev 7, RP4-8: the property is ADMISSION,
#           the print order is a re-sort.)
# exit 3 = DEGRADED. complete=no AND stdout EMPTY. The caller MUST NOT read this
#          as "no actionable work". NOT an error, NOT fail-closed. (AM-32)
#          INCLUDING the all-gated case: ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED + complete=no
#          -> exit 3; + complete=yes -> exit 0. The held block never chooses
#          the exit. (rev 7, RP4-2 — F30 amended)
# exit 2 = fail closed. stdout EMPTY. one `select_work_candidates: <token>` line.
#
#   Exit 3 is conditioned on an EMPTY stdout for a mechanical reason, not a
#   stylistic one: bin/hos-cron branches on the status with an explicit
#   `case "$_gate_rc"` (§2.4; rev 7, RP4-6(g) — the `if _GATE_CANDIDATES=$(…)`
#   form is today's caller, which S2 replaces), and every non-zero arm
#   DISCARDS the candidates. A gate exiting 3 while holding
#   usable candidates would throw them away on the most common path in the
#   design, because the routine early exit always sets complete=no. (§2.2)
#
# ── CLI ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
#   --repo <owner/repo>                 required; ^[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+/[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+$
#   --milestone <positive int>          required; an unsubstituted
#                                       @@MILESTONE_NUMBER@@ MUST fail closed
#   --label <name>                      default "needs-ai"   (FR26)
#   --max-candidates <n>                default 5;   min(5,   max(1, provided))
#   --max-api-requests <n>              default 100; min(100, max(0, provided))
#   EXACTLY FIVE FLAGS. No --repo-root, --exclude-label, --max-list-pages,
#   --trusted, --allow, --skip-gate, --dry-run-trust, --trusted-tier,
#   --collaborators, or any equivalent. (AD-13)
#   RENAMED in rev 5: --max-authorization-checks -> --max-api-requests (AM-30).
#   The VALUE does not move; its UNIT does. A "check" is 1-10 requests, so a
#   ceiling of 100 checks was a ceiling of 100-1000 requests.
#
# ── module constants (NOT flags, deliberately) ───────────────────────────────
#   EXCLUDED_LABELS         = ("needs-human",)  # the ADR-1604 AD-5 landing site
#   LIST_PAGE_BOUND         = 5                 # 500 records  (AM-22 / AF-8)
#   EVENTS_PAGE_BOUND       = 10                # 1000 events  (AM-5)
#   COLLABORATOR_PAGE_BOUND = 3                 # in requester_trust.py  (H3)
#
# ── algorithm order — THE ORDER IS THE CONTRACT (AM-19, AM-29) ───────────────
#   A  args → B config (incl. LAZY tier resolution) → C list (PAGINATED)
#   D1 drop PR records          free
#   D2 drop EXCLUDED_LABELS     free     (also the human's needs-human directive)
#   D3 requester_verdict        free     FR1 FR2 FR4(ratified by the human at Q10) FR5
#   D4 trusted → eligible       free     FR1 FR2 FR3, AD-2  <<< the trusted-author
#                                        fast path, NARROW reading (§8/E-10)
#                                        rev 7: the VERDICT is free and may be
#                                        computed up front; a trusted record is
#                                        ADMITTED only when the WALK reaches it,
#                                        and never after a stop (limb (d))
#   D-rank  RANK THE WHOLE SET  free     <<< PRECEDES D5. Never move it below.
#   D5 walk ranked; pay per untrusted    FR7 FR8 FR16 FR28
#        D5.1 the two stops (EARLY EXIT at --max-candidates; request ceiling)
#        D5.2 fetch events, page-bounded AND budget-clamped. rev 7: a clamp
#             that BITES before a match = unevaluated:cost-ceiling issue=#<n>,
#             NEVER gated, and it IS the ceiling stop (§1.4 rule 4)
#        D5.3 quarantine-on-failure / verify_codeowner_actor / no-codeowner-actor
#   E  sort  <<< LOAD-BEARING in rev 5, NOT redundant.  F  report
#
#   TWO ORDERS, AND THEY ARE DIFFERENT DECISIONS (AM-29):
#     WALK  = (rank, number DESC)                   a REACHABILITY decision.
#             CHANGED IN REVISION 6 on the human's TP-1 ruling: AM-29's own
#             stated fallback is adopted because updated_at is advanced by ANY
#             account posting a comment, making the walk order publicly
#             reorderable (PANEL-RUN3 TP-1). Total on its own — issue numbers
#             are unique by construction, so there is NO tie-break key.
#             A coder who finds updated_at in this expression has found a
#             defect in the document, not in the ruling.
#     EMIT  = (rank, number ASC)                    a FAIRNESS decision. It is
#             #901's documented FIFO-within-band (next_candidates.jq:12, :31)
#             and it does not change. Step E is the line that converts one into
#             the other — WITHIN THE ADMITTED SLICE (rev 7, RP4-5: under the
#             early exit, selection across cycles is newest-first in a busy
#             band; FIFO holds only among what one cycle admits). DO NOT "simplify" the walk key into the emit key, and
#             DO NOT delete Step E on revision 4's since-withdrawn claim that it
#             is a no-op.
#
#   PREFIX-CORRECTNESS, WITH ITS SCOPE ATTACHED (AM-33), RESTATED IN REV 6
#   AND RESTATED OVER DETERMINATIONS IN REV 7 (RUN4 RP4-1; §2.3 D5.1 governs):
#     W = the records the ranked WALK over THE SET STEP C RETURNED DETERMINES
#     eligible, in walk order, with no stop, under the same per-record query
#     outcomes.  For any stop: the SET ADMITTED == W[:j], j = number admitted.
#     A record reached WITHOUT a determination (query failed, AM-31) is NOT
#     in W, is named `unevaluated:query-failed issue=#<n>`, and forces
#     complete=no.  A budget-clamped record is the STOP, never stepped over.
#     == first j of the complete answer IFF complete=yes (AM-33's words; the
#     gate ASSERTS it only when complete=yes — limb (e), §8/E-12).
#     stdout == that set re-sorted by Step E into EMIT order.
#     stdout IS NOT A PREFIX OF THE WALK. Taking the top-k under one order and
#     re-sorting by another does not yield a prefix under either (PANEL-RUN3
#     TP-4). The revision-5 wording claimed it did and was arithmetically
#     false; the panel recommended scoping the property to the WALK and the
#     designer chose that limb (rev 7, RP4-8: not a human-ruled remedy).
#     AN UNQUALIFIED CLAIM OF PREFIX-CORRECTNESS IS ONE THE GATE CANNOT MAKE.
#     Two halves, different owners — Step E cannot touch either, because it
#     permutes a set without changing it. But rev 6's "BOTH UNCHANGED" was
#     false for rank dominance under quarantine; restated in rev 7:
#       RANK DOMINANCE   (nothing the walk left UNREACHED outranks anything
#         ADMITTED; a QUARANTINED record MAY, and is named per record) — a
#         SAFETY property of the walk, preserved by ANY (rank, X) order.
#         rev 6's "Unconditional" is WITHDRAWN: unconditional over records
#         determined or unreached; the quarantine hole is observable (z > 0).
#       INPUT COMPLETENESS (the walk saw every record there is) — a property of
#         STEP C, NOT preserved when the list bound binds. Reported as complete=.
#     A SUBSET IS NOT SUFFICIENT. A PREFIX IS — AND A PREFIX OF WHAT.
#   On EITHER stop the walk ends COMPLETELY — no further record is admitted,
#   TRUSTED OR OTHERWISE. Admitting a later trusted record breaks the
#   ADMISSION-prefix property silently, with every order-agnostic test still
#   green — and after Step E's re-sort it is invisible in stdout's ordering
#   too, which is why §6.2 tests admission and not print order alone.
#
# ── stderr contract ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
#   always, exactly one:
#     select_work_candidates: repo=<slug> milestone=<n> scanned=<N> evaluated=<E>
#       eligible=<M> authorized=<K> gated=<G> unevaluated=<U> api_requests=<A>
#       complete=<yes|no>
#     invariants: N == E + U   and   E == M + G   and   K <= M
#                 U == w + x + z   (rev 7, TP-3: list-truncated is NOT in U)
#                 api_requests <= effective_ceiling   ALWAYS  (AM-30; subsumes RP-21)
#                 complete=yes  IFF  U == 0, no bound truncated the set, and no
#                                    tier resolution was degraded
#                 exit 3        IFF  complete=no AND stdout empty  (AM-32)
#                               — no row overrides this, F30 included (rev 7)
#   when U > 0 OR list-truncated=yes:
#                 select_work_candidates: UNEVALUATED <U> of <N> …
#                   unevaluated:sufficient=<w> cost-ceiling=<x> query-failed=<z>
#                   list-truncated=<yes|no>     # rev 7 (TP-3): a FLAG, not a
#                                               # count, outside U — AM-31
#   per clamp-bitten record (at most ONE; routine, no WARN — rev 7, TP-2):
#                 select_work_candidates: unevaluated:cost-ceiling issue=#<n>
#   per D5-authorized candidate:
#                 select_work_candidates: AUTHORIZED issue=#<n> actor=<login>
#                   via=labeled          # ONE legal value. via=milestoned CANNOT
#                                        # be produced (AR-7). §6.2 asserts that.
#   when eligible==0 and gated>0:  the ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED block (4 lines when
#                 complete=no, 3 when complete=yes)
#   WARN lines:   candidate-list-truncated pages=5 — at least 500 records
#                   matched; the total is UNKNOWN   (loudest; F33. NEVER a
#                   records=<N> count the gate cannot derive — AM-31)
#                 unevaluated:query-failed issue=#<n>        (anomaly; F13)
#                 events-page-bound-reached issue=#<n>       (F14 — a DETERMINATION;
#                                                            NOT emitted on a clamp)
#                 budget-clamped-events-fetch issue=#<n>     (F14b — the record is
#                                                            unevaluated, NOT gated)
#                 tier-resolution-failed tiers=<n>           (F35)
#                 collaborator-page-bound-reached pages=3    (F36)
#   NOT a WARN, deliberately (AM-30 point 3): unevaluated:sufficient and
#                 unevaluated:cost-ceiling. Both are ROUTINE. A warning that
#                 fires every cycle is a banner, invisible by week two.
#   RETIRED in rev 5:  milestone-object-absent  (F17, with AM-18 — AM-35)
#                      authorization-cost-ceiling-reached (F32's WARN — AM-30)
#
#   The FOUR unevaluated:* tokens are UNEVALUATED-reasons, never gate-reasons,
#   and MUST NOT appear in the ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED reasons: breakdown, which
#   enumerates DETERMINATIONS only.  (AM-20)
#
#   stdout is candidate data ONLY. stderr is diagnostics ONLY. Never the reverse.
```

**Boundaries each component must honour:**

- `requester_trust.py` reads **no** environment variable except `BOT_ACCOUNTS` (union-only). It never reads an issue body. It never reads a label. **Revision 5 narrows the network claim rather than dropping it, and the narrowing is exact:** the module now contains **exactly one** network call, `fetch_collaborators`, and it is a **fetch wrapper** in the same class as `probe.py`'s events fetch. **No predicate and no loader performs network I/O** — `load_trusted_set` takes an already-fetched payload, `is_trusted_requester` and `requester_verdict` remain pure, and AD-1's no-network-inside-the-predicate rule is unchanged. **A coder who moves the fetch inside `load_trusted_set` or inside the predicate has broken the property that makes the whole trust path testable without a stub**, and §6.1 asserts the call count rather than trusting the arrangement.
- **Tier membership is never stored (FR7/AD-4 applied one layer out).** No file, no cache, no TTL, no snapshot. **A cached tier membership is a cached trust determination wearing a configuration costume**, and it would let a revoked collaborator stay trusted until someone remembered to re-run a script. Every cycle re-resolves, at a cost of **0 requests when no tier is listed** and 1–3 when one is.
- **`tier_members` is an input to `is_trusted_requester` and to nothing else.** It is absent from `verify_codeowner_actor`'s argument list **by construction**, it is not a CODEOWNERS substitute, and a record authored by a tier member is trusted **as a requester** — which under AR-7 is a statement about whether the record needs an authorizing act, not a statement that one occurred. **The human attached this boundary to their own ruling and it is repeated at every layer:** *"this ruling is scoped entirely to the **requester-trust** roster's own entry format, not to who may authorize."*
- `select_work_candidates.py` is the **only** module that may decide a work candidate is eligible. It never writes to GitHub — no labels, no comments, no state. It is a read-only decision.
- **`select_work_candidates.py` MUST rank before it pays, and the ranking MUST NOT be made conditional on anything.** A reviewer who observes that ranking a set the gate may not fully evaluate "looks wasteful" is observing the design working: **ranking is free** (`priority:*` is a label, already in the Step C payload; **and in revision 6 the walk key's second term is `number`, which is on every record by construction — so the key's availability needs no measurement at all**, where revision 5's `updated_at` term did), and the one edit that restores P-1 exactly is moving D-rank below D5. Amendment 3's own risk register names this as failure mode (i). **Every order-agnostic test stays green through that edit**, which is why §6.2 tests the order and not only the membership. **And revision 5 adds the mirror hazard: the one edit that reverses AM-29 is "simplifying" the walk key into the emit key, or deleting Step E on revision 4's withdrawn claim that it is a no-op.** Amendment 3 warned about optimising the ranking; **this is the same hazard with the opposite sign**, and §6.2 gains the test that catches it. **Revision 6 makes that hazard cheaper to commit and no easier to see:** the two keys are now `(rank, number DESC)` and `(rank, number ASC)`, **which differ by one token**, so the "simplification" is a single-character edit that leaves every membership test green and silently reverses `#901`'s FIFO-within-band. **`test_emission_order_is_rank_number_even_when_the_walk_order_differs` is still the only mechanical defence, and its fixture is now trivial to build precisely because the two orders disagree on every same-rank pair.**
- Neither may cache an eligibility determination (FR7/AD-4). There is no state file, no memo, no TTL, **and no resume cursor** — *"the next cycle continues from where this one left off"* is a property of the world, not of the gate (§2.2.1). Every cycle re-derives from live state. **This is the gate's operating cost and the lever on it: ≈100 API requests per cycle in the worst case is FR7's price, not the ceiling's** (§2.2.1). **The gate's cost is O(population) per cycle while the rate limit is fixed, so no choice of bounds is stable against a growing queue** (AM-30 point 5) — bounds relocate the problem. A future contributor optimising the API cost must change **FR7** with `pm-agent` and the human, not lower the bound; Amendment 4 §4 item 1 recommends that be filed now rather than discovered at the limit.
- Neither imports `scripts/automation/lib/codeowners.py` or `scripts/oversight/codeowners.py`. AD-6's ruling stands: requester trust is about **people**, not paths, so no glob matcher is needed and the dangerous over-matching module stays uncalled. **Parser count is unchanged by S1/S2** (the two documented divergent parsers plus `label-swap.yml`'s inline `awk`); S1 adds none, and removing the `awk` is S3's business.

---

## 6. Test plan

New: `tests/framework/test_requester_trust.py`, `tests/framework/test_select_work_candidates.py`, `tests/framework/test_selection_call_sites.py`. Modified: `tests/automation/test_probe.py` — **no longer additive only**, but the obligation **collapses from six existing tests to ONE in revision 5** (AR-7 / AM-35 / AM-37 retire the milestone-fixture work; §6.4 states why, and why the reduction is a finding rather than a saving) — **and `tests/automation/test_hos_cron.py`** (AM-28(a), extended again in revision 5 by AM-32's call-site test). Deleted: `tests/automation/test_next_candidates.py` (after §4.2's port).

> **REVISION 5 — the two structural changes to the test plan, stated before the lists so a reviewer can check the lists against them.**
>
> 1. **AM-38 supersedes AM-27 point 1's test scope**, so `test_no_document_uses_the_phrase_cutover_material` is **re-scoped from repo-wide to the files S2 edits** (§6.3). As revision 4 specified it, the test could not pass without editing an immutable panel artifact and another author's accepted ruling — **a test that can only pass by rewriting history is a test that will be satisfied by rewriting history.**
> 2. **RP-11 is upheld with a replacement, not a deletion** (AM-37). §4.1 deletes `next_candidates.jq`, so the `EXCLUDED_LABELS` divergence test has nothing left to guard — **and the seam it protected becomes a single-site invariant.** **Binding: the divergence test is REPLACED by a repo-wide assertion that the exclusion set is defined in exactly one place** (AM-9's single landing site). **One test in, one test out**, and the protected-file constant stops being unguarded the moment its guard is removed. §6.3 carries it.

#### Test homes, stated explicitly because revision 3 did not (AM-28(a), binding)

The panel found that `tests/automation/test_hos_cron.py` **exists** and that revision 3 never named it as modified — while **three test rows in this plan exercise `bin/hos-cron`, not the CLI**, and therefore cannot live in `test_select_work_candidates.py` at all. A test row with no home is a test row a coder satisfies by writing something else.

| Row | Exercises | Home | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| **F25** — `python3` missing / module import error | `bin/hos-cron`'s `_gate_candidates_ok=0` path | **`tests/automation/test_hos_cron.py`** | The subject is the launcher's handling of a failed gate, not the gate |
| **F30** — the `$LOG_PREFIX` cycle-log line | `bin/hos-cron`'s `grep -q 'ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED'` block (§2.4) | **`tests/automation/test_hos_cron.py`** | The gate's own block is tested in §6.2; **this** row is the launcher re-emitting it with a prefix. **Revision 7 (RP4-2): two cases, not one.** Gate exits **0** with the block (`complete=yes`) → the prefixed line fires and the `#1395` skip may follow. Gate exits **3** with the block (`complete=no`) → the prefixed line **and** the `WORK SELECTION INCOMPLETE` line both fire, and the skip is **not** taken |
| **AM-12's** `test_bounced_draft_pr_still_reaches_the_worker_and_blocks_the_skip` | `bin/hos-cron`'s PR path + the `#1395` skip | **`tests/automation/test_hos_cron.py`** | **This is the condition DEV-1's approval hangs on.** A coder who satisfies "one test per row" for the CLI rows and quietly drops this one **withdraws an approval nobody notices was withdrawn** |
| **AM-32's** `test_exit_3_does_not_produce_the_no_actionable_work_skip` — **NEW in revision 5, and BINDING** | `bin/hos-cron`'s `case` on the gate's status + the `#1395` skip at `:1158-1163` | **`tests/automation/test_hos_cron.py`** | **AM-32 names this file for exactly this class of call-site contract.** The subject is not the gate — it is the launcher's refusal to read an incomplete answer as an empty one. **It cannot live in `test_select_work_candidates.py`, because the gate is correct in both worlds and the defect is entirely on the caller's side** |

**F26 is different, and "one test per row" over-claims for it.** F26 is *"cycle-context section absent entirely → worker Step 2 falls back to the same gated command"* — which is an **LLM behaviour**, not a code path. **Only the prompt string is pinnable.** §6.3's `test_cron_prompt_invokes_the_entry_point` and `test_cron_prompt_forbids_improvised_fallback` pin the string; **nothing in this plan tests that a model actually obeys it**, and this document must say so rather than implying a behavioural test exists. What stands behind F26 is not a test but a **design property**: the fallback is the *same gated command*, so a model that follows the prompt reaches the gate and a model that ignores it has to actively construct a query the prompt forbids in terms. That is weaker than a test, it is the best available, and pretending otherwise would be the same class of over-claim as F15's "a subset is always safe".

**Binding tests — by ruling rather than by my judgment. A reviewer should treat a proposal to relax any of these as a proposal to reverse a ruling.** §6.1's `test_marker_table_literals_exist_in_emitting_files` and `test_issue_creation_site_count_is_pinned` (AM-7 — the enforcement arm of the declined separate-App structural change, and the detector for D-6's revisit triggers); §6.2's `test_bounced_draft_pr_still_reaches_the_worker_and_blocks_the_skip` (AM-12 — **home now named above**; if it cannot pass, DEV-1 is withdrawn rather than the skip logic adjusted); and §6.3's `test_cron_prompt_fallback_is_sandbox_allowlistable` (AM-11, G11's acceptance mechanism). **Added in revision 4:** §6.3's ship-set import test (AM-26), §6.3's FR8 selection-path agreement test (AM-28(e)), §6.3's `test_labels_doc_states_milestone_route_is_forbidden` (AM-27 point 2), and §6.2's four **prefix-correctness** tests (AM-19 — the property the whole ruling rests on).

> **REVISION 6 — one binding test is RENAMED and its assertion REWRITTEN, and a renamed binding test needs saying out loud.** **`test_emitted_list_is_a_prefix_of_the_complete_walk` becomes `test_admitted_set_is_a_prefix_of_the_complete_walk`**, with a two-part assertion in place of the byte-for-byte one (§6.2). **This is not a relaxation of a binding test — the old assertion was UNSATISFIABLE** on the fixture a sibling binding test mandates (TP-4, CRITICAL), **so what revision 5 made binding was a test that could not pass and would have been "fixed" by whoever hit it first.** **Two more binding tests change their fixtures or keys rather than their standing:** `test_walk_key_is_a_total_order_on_the_candidate_set` (key → `(rank, number DESC)`; the shared-`updated_at` fixture requirement is withdrawn) and `test_emission_order_is_rank_number_even_when_the_walk_order_differs` (fixture simplified — the two orders now disagree by construction). **All three remain BINDING and a proposal to relax any of them is still a proposal to reverse a ruling.**

> **REVISION 7: one binding test is REWRITTEN AGAIN, one test is RENAMED because its assertion was wrong, and five tests are NEW. The reasons are RUN4's.** **(1) `test_admitted_set_is_a_prefix_of_the_complete_walk`** keeps its name and binding status. Its method changes twice (RP4-4). It must run at `--max-candidates` **below** the eligible count, because at default bounds on a three-record fixture it compared a set with itself. And `W` is derived from the fixture and the unbounded run's stdout, **not** from the `gh` stub's call log, which cannot see a trusted record admitted at zero cost. It also compares against `W` **over determinations** (RP4-1(ii)). **This is a strengthening, not a relaxation:** the revision-6 form passed trivially and was falsified by a buildable fixture. **(2) `test_all_gated_still_exits_zero` → `test_all_gated_exit_follows_the_exit_3_rule`** (RP4-2). The old assertion pinned F30's contradiction with §2.2. **(3) New:** `test_budget_clamp_that_bites_is_unevaluated_not_gated_and_stops_the_walk` (TP-2 / RP4-1(i)), `test_a_quarantine_hole_may_outrank_an_admitted_record_and_is_named` (RP4-1(ii)), `test_admission_holds_over_determinations_under_quarantine` (RP4-1(ii)), `test_list_truncated_is_a_flag_outside_U` (TP-3) and `test_trusted_set_tier_of_matches_tier_members` (RP4-6(a), §6.1). **All five are binding in the same sense as the prefix tests:** they pin corrections a panel found by breaking the previous text, and relaxing one re-opens the finding.

### 6.1 `test_requester_trust.py` — the primitive

**Loaders**
- `test_codeowners_humans_parses_individual_owners` / `_skips_team_patterns` / `_empty_when_file_missing` — the three shipped cases, re-pointed at the shared function
- `test_codeowners_humans_ignores_the_path_field` — `/scripts/framework/ @ScottThurlow` yields `{"scottthurlow"}`, never the path token
- `test_codeowners_humans_lowercases_and_dedupes`
- `test_load_trusted_apps_returns_exactly_the_three_hos_roles`
- `test_load_trusted_apps_excludes_copilot` — `copilot[bot]` is never in the result
- `test_load_trusted_apps_does_not_execute_the_file` — a fixture containing `EVIL=$(touch sentinel)` leaves no sentinel
- `test_load_trusted_apps_missing_file_is_empty`
- `test_load_bot_accounts_env_unions_with_file_baseline`
- `test_load_bot_accounts_env_cannot_shrink_the_baseline` — `BOT_ACCOUNTS=""` and `BOT_ACCOUNTS="only-one"` both still yield the four file entries
- `test_roster_is_empty_by_default` — the shipped `trusted-requesters.txt` parses to **`RosterPolicy(frozenset(), frozenset())`** — *both* halves empty (signature changed in revision 5)
- `test_roster_accepts_a_well_formed_entry`
- `test_roster_rejects_entry_missing_provenance` (each of who / when / why, three cases) — **and the tier form gets the same three cases**, because FR3's per-entry provenance is required of both and **the tier form needs it more, not less: a tier entry is a standing delegation to whoever holds that permission tomorrow rather than a decision about one named person**
- `test_roster_rejects_bot_login` (`[bot]` suffix, and a `BOT_ACCOUNTS` member with no suffix)
- `test_roster_missing_file_is_empty` / `test_roster_unreadable_raises`

**The roster's tier form (H3 — NEW in revision 5)**
- **`test_tier_entry_parses_into_tiers_not_logins`** — `tier:write` yields `RosterPolicy(logins=frozenset(), tiers={"write"})`. **The two fields must never cross-contaminate:** a tier token in `logins` would be compared against a login, and a login in `tiers` would be compared against a permission name.
- **`test_bare_tier_name_without_the_prefix_is_a_LOGIN`** — a line reading `write` yields `logins={"write"}` and `tiers=frozenset()`. **This is the one place the human's "no translation layer" instruction needed a design decision and it must be pinned rather than left to a reader's intuition:** GitHub logins and permission names occupy the same string space (`admin` is a valid GitHub login), so a format distinguishing them by *value* would make the roster's meaning depend on GitHub's account namespace.
- **`test_tier_token_matching_is_case_insensitive`** — `tier:Write` yields `tiers={"write"}`, normalised to lowercase.
- **`test_unrecognised_tier_name_is_skipped_with_a_diagnostic_listing_the_five`** (F34) — `tier:owner`, `tier:push`, `tier:collaborator` are each skipped, each emit a diagnostic **naming the line number and listing `read`/`triage`/`write`/`maintain`/`admin`**, and **none is passed through to the API**. Assert the five values appear in the diagnostic: *"a malformed roster grants nothing"* is only half the requirement; the other half is that the operator is told what would have worked.
- **`test_the_rest_of_the_roster_loads_around_a_malformed_tier_line`** — one bad tier line does not discard a valid login entry three lines below it.
- **`test_permission_tiers_is_exactly_githubs_five_names`** — a literal assertion on the module constant. **This is the test that fails if anyone adds a mapping table**, which the human's addendum forbids in terms: *"Don't invent separate names … No translation layer, no ambiguity."*

**The permission-tier resolver (H3, §1.3.2 — NEW in revision 5)**
- **`test_no_tier_listed_means_no_collaborator_fetch_AT_ALL`** — with an empty `tiers`, the `gh` stub records **zero** calls to `/collaborators`. **This is the single most important cost property of the feature** and it is what keeps H3 free for every deployment that does not use it, including this one.
- **`test_one_collaborator_fetch_per_cycle_not_per_candidate`** — with a tier listed and 40 candidates, the stub records **one** `/collaborators` call (up to its page bound), not 40. **Assert the call count, not the result**: the result is identical either way and the cost differs by a factor of 40.
- **`test_membership_is_matched_on_role_name_not_the_permissions_object`** — a fixture whose `role_name` is `write` and whose `permissions` object is `{"push": true, "pull": true}` must match `tier:write`; a fixture with **only** the `permissions` object and no `role_name` must match **nothing**. **This is §0.6 gap 8's unit-test half**, and the failure it guards against is the nastiest kind: a coder who matches the `permissions` vocabulary (`pull`/`push`) **silently fails every `read` and `write` entry in a consumer's roster while passing every test written against a fixture the same coder wrote.**
- **`test_tier_matching_is_exact_not_hierarchical`** — `tier:write` admits `role_name == "write"` and **rejects** `maintain` and `admin`. **A policy wanting "write and above" lists all three.** ~~This is my decision rather than the human's (they ruled the tokens, not the ordering)~~ **— RULED BY THE HUMAN on 2026-09-16T19:55:45Z (TP-5), so this test pins a product ruling rather than a designer's judgement, and a proposal to relax it is a proposal to reverse a ruling (§1.3.2). The assertion does not change; its standing does.** It is the narrower and fail-closed reading, and **pinning it is how a later "obvious improvement" to a permission lattice fails loudly instead of widening the trusted set silently.** *(Revision 7, RP4-6(i): revision 6 left the revision-5 fragment "rather than the human's (they ruled the tokens, not the ordering)" outside the strike-through, so the TP-5 note read backwards. The whole of the superseded clause is now struck.)*
- **`test_bot_login_from_the_collaborators_endpoint_is_rejected`** (F37) — a collaborator record whose login ends `[bot]`, and one in `BOT_ACCOUNTS` without the suffix, are both excluded from `tier_members` even when their `role_name` matches a listed tier. **A GitHub App installed on a repository can appear in its collaborator list**, so without this a `tier:write` entry is a route around AD-2.
- **`test_resolution_failure_yields_an_empty_tier_member_set_and_never_raises`** (F35) — non-zero exit, non-JSON, a JSON object instead of a list, and a timeout: **four cases, each yielding `frozenset()`**, each emitting `WARN tier-resolution-failed`, and **none propagating an exception to the predicate.**
- **`test_page_bound_exhaustion_resolves_over_what_was_fetched`** (F36) — 4 pages offered, 3 consumed, `WARN collaborator-page-bound-reached pages=3`, and the collaborators from the first three pages **do** join `tier_members`. **Not an error, and one-directional: it can only under-trust.**
- **`test_tier_membership_is_never_written_to_disk`** — a sweep asserting no file is created and no cache path is touched. **A cached tier membership is a cached trust determination** (FR7/AD-4), and it would let a revoked collaborator stay trusted until someone re-ran a script.
- **`test_load_trusted_set_performs_no_network_io`** — monkeypatch `subprocess` to raise; `load_trusted_set(collaborators=<payload>)` and `load_trusted_set(collaborators=None)` both succeed. **This is the test that keeps AD-1's no-network-in-the-predicate rule true after H3 introduced a network dependency into the trust path.**
- **`test_collaborators_none_is_the_same_as_a_failed_resolution`** — both yield `tier_members = frozenset()`. **This is why `probe.py` needs no change**: it does not fetch collaborators and does not resolve tiers.

**`is_trusted_requester`**
- `test_codeowner_is_trusted` / `test_roster_member_is_trusted` / `test_trusted_app_returns_trusted_app_reason`
- **`test_trusted_set_tier_of_matches_tier_members`** — **NEW in revision 7 (RP4-6(a)).** For every `TrustedSet` produced by `load_trusted_set`, `frozenset(tier_of) == tier_members`, and each value of `tier_of` is one of the roster's listed tiers. With `collaborators=None` or on a failed resolution, both are empty. **This is the field that makes `roster-tier:<tier>` computable at all.** Through revision 6 no field carried the mapping §1.4 step 3(iii) said was *"passed in with the `TrustedSet`"*.
- **`test_tier_member_is_trusted_with_the_tier_in_the_reason`** (H3) — a login in `tier_members` yields `(True, "roster-tier:write")`, **naming the tier that admitted them** *(the tier is read from `tier_of`; revision 7)*. *"This requester is trusted"* and *"this requester is trusted because your policy trusts everyone with push access"* are different facts, and **only the second is reviewable after the fact.**
- **`test_codeowner_wins_over_tier`** — a login that is both a CODEOWNER and a `write` collaborator reports **`codeowner`**, not `roster-tier:write`. The more specific and more durable provenance is the more useful one in an audit, and the order is fixed.
- **`test_bot_in_tier_members_is_untrusted`** — `(False, "bot-in-human-category")`. **The third disjunct is inside the SAME branch as the other two human categories, not beside it**, so it inherits the bot guard automatically; this test is what proves the arrangement rather than the intention.
- **`test_tiers_field_is_never_consulted_for_membership`** — a `TrustedSet` whose `tiers={"write"}` and whose `tier_members` is empty trusts **nobody**. **Any code path that tests membership against `.tiers` is comparing a login against a permission name**, and it would pass every test written with a fixture where the two happen to differ.
- **`test_tier_members_is_absent_from_verify_codeowner_actor`** — a signature-level assertion (§1.4, §5). **Requester trust never confers authorization authority**, and the human attached that boundary to their own ruling; this is its mechanical form.
- `test_arbitrary_public_user_is_untrusted` — the FR1 acceptance check: `type == "User"`, no `[bot]` suffix, on no roster → `(False, "not-in-trusted-set")`
- `test_copilot_bot_is_untrusted`
- `test_empty_login_is_untrusted`
- `test_bot_listed_in_codeowners_is_untrusted` — `("hos-worker-hos[bot]", "Bot")` with the login in `codeowners` → `(False, "bot-in-human-category")`
- `test_bot_listed_in_roster_is_untrusted`
- `test_matching_is_case_insensitive`
- `test_is_pure` — no filesystem, no network, no env read (monkeypatch `open`/`subprocess` to raise)

**Conformance (these are the rules that keep the next consumer right)**
- `test_no_production_use_of_not_is_bot_reviewer` — the literal `not is_bot_reviewer` appears nowhere under `scripts/` or `bin/`
- `test_is_trusted_requester_has_one_production_caller` — `is_trusted_requester(` appears in `scripts/`/`bin/` only inside `requester_trust.py`
- `test_no_second_codeowners_parser_in_framework` — the `.github/CODEOWNERS` literal appears in exactly one module under `scripts/framework/`; `probe.py` contains no `CODEOWNERS` literal after the refactor
- `test_requester_trust_imports_nothing_from_automation_or_oversight`

**Marker**
- `test_each_marker_matches_a_real_title_from_its_site` — seven cases, using the title strings the emitting sites actually produce
- **`test_marker_table_literals_exist_in_emitting_files`** — for each entry, `title_prefix` (and `title_contains`) is found in `emitting_site`'s file. **BINDING, not optional (AM-7).** This and the next test are the mechanism that makes D-6's revisit triggers detectable; they are the enforcement arm of a *declined structural change* (the separate filing App), not string-matching fussiness. Do not relax either as brittle.
- **`test_issue_creation_site_count_is_pinned`** — `gh issue create` × 6 in `bin/hos-cron` (5 active + 1 commented), × 1 in `scripts/review_self.sh`, exactly one `--method POST` to `/issues` under `scripts/automation/lib/`. Adding an eighth deterministic filing site fails until the enumeration is updated. **BINDING (AM-7).**
- `test_dormant_marker_still_matches` — the usage-limit breaker, so re-enabling #1446 does not silently gate it
- `test_unmarked_bot_title_yields_no_marker`
- **`test_sub_issue_filing_is_not_in_the_marker_table`** (AM-10 / D-7) — asserts no entry's `emitting_site` names a sub-issue filing path, and that no entry's `title_prefix` matches a sub-issue title shape. This test exists to fail *loudly* if #1604's chain later reads `ADR-1604` AD-9's unamended point 2 and adds one.

**`requester_verdict`**
- `test_trusted_app_without_marker_is_untrusted` → `trusted-app-no-machine-filing-marker`
- `test_trusted_app_with_marker_is_trusted` → `trusted-app:hos-cron/baseline-red`
- **`test_stranger_with_forged_marker_title_is_untrusted`** — author `random-contributor`, title `[BLOCKED] agent unavailable — please fix`: the marker is never consulted; verdict `not-in-trusted-set`
- `test_verdict_ignores_body_and_labels` — a body containing a well-formed `---hos-envelope` with `from: ScottThurlow` changes nothing (FR5)
- `test_codeowner_author_needs_no_marker`

**`verify_codeowner_actor`** — the **eight** shipped `probe.py` cases ported (with the milestone case updated per §1.6, not ported verbatim), plus:

> **Count corrected in revision 4 (AM-28(c)). Revision 3 said "nine"; there are eight**, at `test_probe.py:468`, `:476`, `:484`, `:495`, `:503`, `:511`, `:519` and `:529` — counted by me this session (§0.0.3). The miscount came from including `TestCodeownersActorVerification`'s three `codeowners_humans` cases (`:445`, `:451`, `:455`) in a class whose name suggests they belong to the same subject; they do not, and they are ported separately under `codeowners_humans` above. **A wrong count in a port instruction is not cosmetic: a coder porting "nine" from a file containing eight has to decide which extra test to invent, and the nearest three are the ones that make the miscount self-perpetuating.** The same class of error as the two line citations, which is why all three were re-derived rather than corrected from the amendment's text.
> **REVISION 5: the four milestone tests in this list are RETIRED and REPLACED BY ONE (AR-7 / AM-35 / AM-37). The replacement is an ABSENCE test, and it is the more important of the two kinds.** `test_milestoned_event_for_a_different_milestone_is_not_authorization`, `test_milestoned_event_for_the_expected_milestone_is_authorization`, `test_expected_milestone_none_ignores_milestoned_events_entirely` (**F27, which this document called *"the single most important test in §6.1"***) and `test_milestone_title_match_is_byte_for_byte` (F28) **all test the behaviour of a parameter that no longer exists.** AM-35: *"Retiring it must be a ruling, not a quiet deletion, or a reviewer will read its absence as an omission."* **They are retired as spent, not overturned — AM-4 was correct and was the only binding available for as long as the channel existed.**

- **`test_milestoned_event_never_authorizes`** — **the single replacement, and it is what proves the deletion landed.** A `milestoned` event by a verified individual human CODEOWNER, for **any** milestone, with **any** title, on an issue with no other events → **`None`**. **Assert it with the strongest fixture available** (the human CODEOWNER, the matching title, the milestone the gate is querying), because a weak fixture would pass against a function that still had the arm and merely failed to match. **If this test is ever "fixed" to make a `milestoned` event authorize, the fix is the re-introduction of FIND-1.**
- **`test_verify_codeowner_actor_takes_exactly_four_arguments`** — a signature assertion. **`expected_milestone_title` must be absent, not present-and-defaulted:** AM-35 chose deletion over a sentinel precisely because *"a sentinel leaves a live code path that a single call-site edit re-enables."* **A test that only checks behaviour cannot tell a deleted parameter from an ignored one**, and the difference is the whole of the ruling.
- `test_bot_actor_after_codeowner_does_not_erase_authorization` — pins the `continue`-not-`return None` semantics, **and it is now also the test that demonstrates residual R-3** (§7.2, AM-36): a CODEOWNER `labeled` event authorizes forever, even after a bot has removed and re-applied the label. **The test is unchanged; what changes is that its docstring must name R-3**, because AM-35's last binding row requires the consequence to be stated at the code site and this is the code site's test.
- `test_non_list_events_returns_none`
- **`test_only_the_queried_label_authorizes`** — a `labeled` event for a *different* label, by a verified human CODEOWNER, returns `None`. **Not new behaviour** (step 2 has always matched `event.label.name == label_name`), **but with the milestone arm gone this is now the ONLY narrowing left in the function**, and a single-channel function's one discriminator deserves its own test rather than living inside a fixture.

**Named acceptance checks the coder must perform and record (not unit tests):**
- **~~§0.6 gap 5~~ — RETIRED with the channel it was about (AR-7 / AM-35).** The coder no longer observes a live `milestoned` payload, because **the gate never reads a `milestone` object for any authorization purpose and there is no field whose shape matters.** **The panel's measurement is preserved, not discarded** — 97/97 payloads carrying exactly `{"title"}`, the only observation of that payload this chain ever made — and **S3's `demilestoned` rule will need it** (AM-37, RP-8: *"a measurement thrown away is a measurement re-taken"*). It lives in §0.6 gap 5's retirement note.
- **§0.6 gap 8 — NEW, and it REPLACES gap 5 in exactly the same shape: observe ONE live collaborator-permission response and confirm that `role_name` carries GitHub's five documented values.** The human's H3 addendum fixes the roster's tier tokens to `read`/`triage`/`write`/`maintain`/`admin`, which are `role_name` values; the `permissions` object on the same payload is a **different vocabulary** (`pull`/`triage`/`push`/`maintain`/`admin`). **I have not observed this payload, no fixture in this repo contains one, and ~~nothing in the tree calls the endpoint~~ nothing in the tree calls the LIST endpoint** (§0.0.4; *corrected in revision 7, a site revision 6's TP-5/TP-10(i) sweep missed. `provision_agent_account.sh:190` calls the per-user sibling and reads the legacy vocabulary, §1.3.2*). **Binding on the coder: if `role_name` does not carry those values, implement `role_name` matching ANYWAY as §1.3.2 binds it, and raise the discrepancy to `technical-design` — do NOT substitute the `permissions` object on your own judgement.** The failure direction if the check is skipped and the field is wrong is **fail-closed** (an unmatched tier contributes nobody), which is why this is a declared gap rather than a blocker — **and it is revision 5's single most load-bearing unobserved claim**, which is why §9 nominates it.
