- **§0.6 gap 6** — **moved to §6.2 in revision 3, and it is no longer a one-command read.** AM-14 scopes it as a **live mutating re-apply on a throwaway surface, run by the coder under a bot identity, never on a live candidate issue**, which makes it a gate-slice acceptance check rather than a primitive one. Revision 2 listed it here while §0.6 pointed at §6.2; the two are reconciled in §6.2's favour, which is where AM-14 also places it.
- **Amendment §0 gap 3** — confirm live that #1643's `needs-ai` label actor is `ScottThurlow`. The architect flagged this as the load-bearing ergonomic valve in AM-1 and asked that it be confirmed once before S2 merges rather than assumed from §2.6.

### 6.2 `test_select_work_candidates.py` — the gate

**Ordering parity** — the nine cases ported per §4.2, each with a trusted author.

**Trust and authorization** (each drives the CLI with a stubbed `gh`)
- **`test_untrusted_author_with_worker_applied_needs_ai_is_not_a_candidate`** — the core #1539 fix, VF-3's exact shape
- `test_untrusted_author_with_codeowner_applied_label_is_a_candidate`
- **`test_untrusted_author_with_codeowner_applied_milestone_is_NOT_a_candidate`** — **INVERTED in revision 5 (AR-7).** Revision 4's version asserted the opposite and is retired with the channel. The fixture is unchanged and the assertion flips: a human CODEOWNER who milestoned the issue, and did nothing else, authorizes **nothing**. **This is the gate-level twin of §6.1's `test_milestoned_event_never_authorizes`, and both are needed**: one proves the primitive dropped the arm, the other proves no call site re-supplies it.
- **`test_no_fixture_produces_via_milestoned`** — an **absence** assertion over every fixture in this file: no `AUTHORIZED … via=milestoned` line is ever emitted. **The cheapest possible detector for a deletion that missed a call site**, which is exactly what §9's third-run item 5 is instructed to hunt for.
- **`test_human_proxy_bot_authored_issue_is_gated_without_marker`** — author `scottthurlow-claude[bot]`, ordinary title: the #1539/#1340/#1356/#1540 shape from §2.6
- **`test_human_proxy_bot_labelling_does_not_authorize`** — AD-6's named asymmetry: `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` applied `needs-ai` **and** the milestone on an untrusted-authored issue → still gated. This is the case the ADR says "must be tested as its own case."
- **`test_worker_self_labelled_worker_authored_issue_is_rejected`** — #1678's shape: author and both label/milestone actors are `hos-worker-hos[bot]`, title carries no marker → gated
- `test_codeowner_applied_label_releases_an_issue_the_bot_later_relabelled` — #1643's shape
- `test_baseline_repair_blocked_issue_is_selectable_with_zero_events_calls` — the one live machine-filing site: author `hos-worker-hos[bot]`, title `[BLOCKED] inner-loop tests failing on HumanOversightSystem — diagnose and fix`, `needs-ai`+`priority:critical`, milestone set → selectable, and the `gh` stub records exactly one API call
- `test_codeowner_authored_issue_costs_zero_extra_api_calls` — AD-3's cost claim, asserted rather than assumed
- `test_roster_listed_author_is_selectable`
- `test_copilot_bot_authored_issue_is_gated`
- `test_issue_body_claiming_codeowner_identity_is_gated` (FR5)
- **`test_pull_request_records_are_excluded`** (DEV-1)

**AM-12's binding condition on DEV-1 — the bounce loop must be proved intact, not assumed.** DEV-1 is approved *conditional on this test*, and the condition has teeth:

- **`test_bounced_draft_pr_still_reaches_the_worker_and_blocks_the_skip`** — a bounced draft PR carrying the dispatch label, with the candidate list **empty**, must (a) still be surfaced to the worker through `bin/hos-cron`'s PR path and (b) still prevent the `#1395` no-actionable-work early skip.

  I read that path this revision and it is satisfiable by construction, which the coder should know before writing the test: `_OPEN_PR_NUMS` is populated from `gh api repos/<slug>/pulls?state=open` (`bin/hos-cron:1049-1050`) — **a different endpoint from the issues query the gate replaces** — and the bounce detection at `:1085` reads that PR's own `draft` flag and labels (`:1078-1086`). The skip condition at `:1158` requires `-z "$_OPEN_PR_NUMS"` **independently of** `-z "$_GATE_CANDIDATES"`. So excluding `pull_request` records from the *issues* list cannot affect PR surfacing at all. The test pins that independence rather than discovering it.

  **If the test cannot be made to pass, DEV-1 is withdrawn and PR records stay in** — escalate to the architect. Do **not** adjust the skip logic to make it pass; that would be repairing the gate's blast radius by widening it.

**Prefix-correctness — FIVE tests in revision 5, not four (AM-33 adds the fifth limb, and it is an ABSENCE test).**

> **REVISION 7 — THE LIMB COUNT IS STILL FIVE; THE PROPERTY IS NOW STATED OVER DETERMINATIONS (RUN4 RP4-1), AND THE DIFFERENTIAL TEST'S METHOD IS FIXED (RP4-4).** The admission property compares against `W`, the records the complete walk **determines** eligible under the same per-record query outcomes (§2.3 D5.1). The composed differential test runs **below** the eligible count and derives `W` from the fixture, not from the call log. Limb (d)'s fixture now states the precondition RUN4 RP4-6(m) found missing. Two tests below are new for the two mechanisms that broke revision 6's statement: the budget clamp and the quarantine hole. **The revision-6 block that follows is retained. Its attribution *"(the human's TP-4 ruling)"* is corrected by RP4-8:** the human ruled TP-4 a correction, and the remedy is the panel's recommendation.
>
> **REVISION 6 — THE LIMB COUNT IS UNCHANGED AT FIVE; WHAT THE PROPERTY IS ABOUT HAS MOVED, AND THREE OF THE TESTS BELOW MOVE WITH IT (the human's TP-4 ruling).** Prefix-correctness is now an **ADMISSION** property — *the set the walk admitted is the first `k` eligible of the ranked walk* — and **stdout is that set re-sorted by Step E**, so **no test in this file may assert that stdout is a prefix of anything.** The three that change are named at their bullets: **limb (b)'s** key and fixture rationale (TP-1), the **composed differential test**, which is renamed and split into an admission assertion plus a presentation assertion (TP-4), and the **emission-order test**'s fixture (TP-1). **Limbs (a), (c), (d) and (e) are unchanged in substance** — (a) and (c) never mentioned an order, (d) is about admission already, and (e) is about Step C. **Read the vocabulary change as a correction to what the tests were always trying to prove, not as a relaxation of what they prove**; §2.3 D5.1 carries the arithmetic.

§2.3 D5.1 states prefix-correctness as a conjunction of five independent limbs. **A single test named `test_prefix_correctness` would report "prefix-correctness broke" and leave the next reader to find out which limb** — the same objection §6.3 already makes to a combined regex for G11's four conditions, and it applies with more force here because this is the property a CRITICAL finding was closed on. **AM-33's point is sharper than "add a test": *"a four-limb suite stays green while the fifth is violated — that is RP-6's actual cost, and it is a test-suite defect, not a reasoning defect."***

- **`test_rank_is_computed_before_any_authorization_check`** — limb (a) and the **direct P-1 regression test**. A fixture of 40 records in `created desc` order with a single `priority:critical` untrusted-authored record at position 38, authorized by a CODEOWNER `labeled` event, and **`--max-api-requests 3`** (*flag renamed in revision 5*). **The critical record MUST be emitted**, and the `gh` stub must record that its events call was among the **first three** made. Under revision 3's algorithm this test fails; under revision 4's and 5's it passes at any ceiling ≥ 1. **This is #1539's own shape, and the fixture's comment must say so**, because a later reader deleting it as "an arbitrary ordering test" is deleting the regression test for the defect that produced this revision.
- **`test_ranking_reads_only_the_list_payload`** — limb (a). The rank function is called with records whose every field except `labels` is a sentinel that raises on access; ranking must still succeed. A rank that reaches for anything the events call would supply can change between D-rank and emit.
- **`test_walk_key_is_a_total_order_on_the_candidate_set`** — limb (b), **re-aimed at the WALK key in revision 5 and RE-AIMED AGAIN in revision 6 (the human's TP-1 ruling): the key is `(rank, number DESC)`.** Two records never compare equal, because **issue numbers are unique within a repository by construction**, so the key is total **on its own — there is no tie-break term, and none is needed.** Assert the walk order is identical across repeated runs of a shuffled input, and assert it **descends by number within each rank band**.

  > **The revision-5 fixture requirement is WITHDRAWN and its rationale is rewritten rather than left standing, because a stale justification is how a deleted fixture gets re-added.** Revision 5 required *"a fixture with two records sharing an `updated_at`"*, on the reasoning that §0.0.4 measured all 101 live values distinct **today**, so `number ASC` was a genuine tie-break rather than a load-bearing key **on the present population** — and *"a property that holds only on the present population is one a test must hold independently of it."* **That fixture is now unconstructible and the reasoning behind it no longer applies:** the walk key does not read `updated_at`, and its totality is **structural rather than measured** — two distinct issues in one repository cannot share a number, ever, on any population. **So the test loses a fixture and GAINS strength**, which is the unusual direction and is worth saying: revision 5's limb (b) was total by measurement, revision 6's is total by construction. **What the test must still catch is a non-deterministic sort** — e.g. a stable-sort assumption that silently depends on input order — which is why the shuffled-input assertion is retained and is now the whole of the test.
- **`test_free_filters_run_before_ranking`** — limb (c). A PR record and a `needs-human` record, both `priority:critical`, must be absent from the ranked set entirely — **not merely absent from the output**. Assert via the rank function's call log, not the emitted list: a filter applied *after* ranking produces identical stdout and a different set of paid checks.
- **`test_no_record_is_admitted_after_a_stop_including_a_trusted_one`** — limb (d), and **it is the test that pins the specific thing revision 3 got wrong.** A fixture where the record at rank position 30 is **trusted-authored** (zero-cost) and the ceiling is exhausted at position 12. **The fixture MUST place at least one untrusted (paid) record between positions 13 and 29** *(revision 7, RP4-6(m))*. The ceiling stop fires at the next *attempted* request (§2.2.1's stop test). If positions 13–30 were all trusted, they would be admitted legitimately, for free, before any stop fired, and the test would be asserting the wrong thing. **The trusted record MUST NOT be emitted.** *Revision 7 adds a second case: the same fixture with the budget exhausted by a **clamp that bites** at position 12 (§1.4 rule 4). There the stop is at 12 itself, so the trusted record at 13 must not be emitted either, even with no paid record between.* Revision 3's D5.1 would have emitted it, because it skipped only remaining *untrusted* records. Amendment 3's risk register names this as failure mode (iii) — *"the sufficiency stop being read as the gate may stop whenever it likes"* — and it breaks the **admission-prefix** property **silently**, with every membership-based test still green. **Revision 6 note: it is now silent in one more way.** Step E re-sorts the emitted set, so a record wrongly admitted at walk position 30 **does not even appear out of place in stdout** — it lands wherever `(rank, number ASC)` puts it. **This test asserts admission, not print order, and that distinction is load-bearing rather than pedantic.**
- **`test_admitted_set_is_a_prefix_of_the_complete_walk`** — **RENAMED AND RE-AIMED IN REVISION 6 ~~on the human's TP-4 ruling~~ *(as a correction under the human's TP-4 classification, on the panel's recommended limb; RP4-8)*; it was `test_emitted_list_is_a_prefix_of_the_complete_walk` and, as written, it was UNSATISFIABLE.** The composed property, still as a **differential** test rather than an assertion about internals, but comparing the right object.

  > **REVISION 7 REWRITES THE METHOD (RUN4 RP4-4). The revision-6 steps are struck below, and the steps after them bind.** Revision 6's form had two defects, both verified at the line by the panel's orchestrator. **(1) It was vacuous as specified.** It ran *"once with the default bounds"*, and the default `--max-candidates` is 5. On the three-record emission-order fixture the bound never bit, so the test compared a set with itself. **(2) It derived `W` from a log that cannot see trusted records.** D4 admits a trusted-authored record at **zero** API calls, so it never appears in the `gh` stub's call log. On any fixture with a trusted record (limb (d)'s, for one), `W` would be wrong. **And (3), from RP4-1: it compared against the eligible records in walk order, which is false under quarantine.** It now compares against `W` **over determinations**.
  >
  > ~~Run the fixture twice — once with the default bounds, once with bounds high enough that `complete=yes`. Recover the unbounded run's WALK order from the `gh` stub's call log …, and take `W` = the eligible records in that walk order. Assert `set(bounded_output) == set(W[:len(bounded_output)])`.~~

  - **Fixtures, all of them, one parameterised test.** Each fixture declares its records **and** its expected-determined-eligible set `D` (the test author's statement, independent of the gate). **At least 3 and at most 4 records must be determined eligible**, so that one run at default bounds reaches no stop. Mandatory fixtures: **(i)** the emission-order fixture (`#10/#20/#30`, same rank, all authorized); **(ii)** a fixture with a **trusted-authored** record among untrusted authorized ones, the case that broke the log-derived `W`; **(iii)** RUN4's **quarantine** fixture: `#30/#20/#10` same rank, all authorized, `#30`'s events query stubbed to fail on **every** call, plus `#5` (same rank, authorized) to meet the minimum of 3, so `D = {#20, #10, #5}` and `#30` is not in `D`; **(iv)** RUN4's **clamp** fixture: `#30` untrusted with its authorizing event on events page 2, `#20` trusted-authored, `#10` untrusted and authorized on page 1, so `D = {#30, #20, #10}`.
  - **The unbounded run.** Run once at default bounds. **Assert `set(stdout) == D`**, so the reference run is itself checked against the fixture's declaration rather than trusted. Derive **`W = sorted(D, key=walk_key)`**, with `walk_key = (rank, -number)`, **computed in the test from the fixture's own records**, not from the `gh` call log and not from any gate internal.
  - **The bounded runs, which are the point, and which MUST bite.** Run at **`--max-candidates 1`** and **`--max-candidates 2`**, both below `|D|`, so the early exit stops the walk. Run fixture (iv) additionally at **`--max-api-requests 2`**: one list page, then `#30`'s fetch clamped to one page. **Assert every bounded run actually stopped**: `complete=no`, and `unevaluated:sufficient` or `unevaluated:cost-ceiling` is non-zero. A bounded run that did not stop is a vacuous case and **fails the test**, which is the direct guard against RP4-4(1) recurring.
  - **Assert the ADMISSION property, over determinations:** `set(bounded_stdout) == set(W[:len(bounded_stdout)])`. Worked for (iii) at `--max-candidates 1`: `#30` is quarantined in both runs (the stub is deterministic), so `W` starts `#20` and the bounded run admits `{#20}`. **Holds.** It also asserts `unevaluated:query-failed issue=#30` is present. Worked for (iv) at `--max-api-requests 2`: `#30`'s clamp bites and the walk stops, so it admits `{}` = `W[:0]`. **Holds, and the exit is 3.** *Revision 6's rules fail this case.* They gate `#30`, then admit `#20` for free, giving `{#20} ≠ {#30} = W[:1]`. That was RUN4's counterexample 1.
  - **Assert the PRESENTATION property separately:** `bounded_stdout == sorted(that set, key=(rank, number))` — i.e. Step E re-sorted the admitted set and did not reorder it by anything else.

  **Satisfiability, checked by hand before binding it, which is the check whose omission produced TP-4 and RP4-4.** (i) at 1: `{#30}` = `W[:1]`, and stdout `[#30]`. (i) at 2: `{#30, #20}` = `W[:2]`, and stdout `[#20, #30]` (RUN4's completeness lens confirmed both). (ii), (iii) and (iv) are worked above or follow the same way. **Under revision 6's clamp rule, fixture (iv) makes this test fail, which is correct: it is the test that detects RP4-1(i).**

  > **Why the revision-5 form could not pass, carried here because the test's own docstring must say so (TP-4, CRITICAL, blocking).** The old assertion was `bounded_output == unbounded_output[:len(bounded_output)]` **byte for byte**. **With the walk and the emission orders separated — which AM-29 did deliberately — that is false whenever the early exit bites.** Under the binding revision-6 keys, three same-rank records `#10/#20/#30` walk `[#30, #20, #10]`; the unbounded run emits `[#10, #20, #30]`; a bounded run at `--max-candidates 1` admits `{#30}` and emits `[#30]`; the old assertion compares `[#30] == [#10]` and **fails**. At `--max-candidates 2` it fails the same way. **This is not an edge case: the measured population is 102 against a default stop of 5 (M-1), so the early exit bites on every cycle** — the test only ever passed on fixtures smaller than the stop. **Revision 5 made this test BINDING while making its premise false, which is precisely the defect TP-4 exists to name, and the docstring must record that the assertion was rewritten because the document got it wrong — not because someone might.**

  > **What is NOT weakened by the rewrite.** The two-assertion form is **strictly stronger** than the one it replaces, not a relaxation: the old test checked one composite string; the new one checks the admission property (the safety-relevant half, which the old form could not isolate) **and** the re-sort (the fairness half, which the old form conflated with it). **A limb nobody thought to enumerate still fails it** — that was AM-33's reason for wanting the test and it survives the rescoping.
- **`test_no_global_prefix_claim_is_made_when_the_list_bound_binds`** — **limb (e), NEW in revision 5, and it is an ABSENCE test (AM-33 point 3).** With Step C truncated, assert that the gate reports **`complete=no`** and that **no output asserts prefix-correctness over anything but the fetched set** — i.e. the `UNEVALUATED` line carries **`unevaluated:list-truncated=yes`** (*a flag, not a count, outside `U`: revision 7, TP-3*), and nothing in stdout or stderr claims the emitted list is the head of the repository's answer. **(Revision 6: unchanged in substance. The limb is about Step C's INPUT, so the TP-4 rescoping does not reach it — but the absence assertion now has a second string to look for, because "the emitted list is a prefix of the walk" is a claim the gate must not make EITHER, on any input.)** **The reason this limb needs a test at all is the reason it was missed:** Step C truncates by `created desc` **before** ranking, so **an omitted record CAN outrank an emitted one**, and every one of the four limbs above stays green while that happens. ~~**Rank dominance is unconditional; input completeness is not; and the gate must only ever claim the one it has.**~~ **Revision 7 (RP4-1): rank dominance holds over the records the walk determined or left unreached. Its one exception, a quarantine hole, is named per record. Input completeness is not guaranteed at all. The gate must only ever claim what it has, and both gaps force `complete=no`.**
- **`test_emission_order_is_rank_number_even_when_the_walk_order_differs`** — **NEW in revision 5 and BINDING (AM-29 point 2). FIXTURE REWRITTEN IN REVISION 6: the old construction is obsolete.** Revision 5 mandated *"three same-rank records whose `updated_at` order is the reverse of their number order"* — a construction that existed only to *manufacture* a disagreement between the two orders. **Under the binding walk key `(rank, number DESC)` and the emission key `(rank, number ASC)` the two orders are exact reverses within a band, so ANY three same-rank records disagree, by construction and with no fixture engineering at all.** **The fixture is therefore: three same-rank records with any three distinct issue numbers** — say `#10`, `#20`, `#30`. Assert the **walk** visited them in **descending issue number** (`#30, #20, #10`, via the `gh` stub's call log) **and** that stdout emits them in **ascending issue number** (`#10, #20, #30`). **The rewrite removes the one piece of `updated_at` fixture machinery left in this file**, and it removes a fixture whose construction a later reader would have had to reverse-engineer to know what it was proving. **This is the one test that catches a future edit "simplifying" Step E away** on the strength of revision 4's own — now withdrawn — sentence that it is a no-op. **`#901`'s FIFO-within-band is a documented product policy** (`next_candidates.jq:12`), and deleting Step E changes what the worker builds first while every membership test stays green. Amendment 3's risk register warned about optimising the ranking; **this is the same hazard with the opposite sign.**

**The three bounds and their interactions (AM-22), updated in revision 5 for AM-30's unit and AM-31's report rule:**

- **`test_step_c_paginates`** — the `gh` stub asserts `--paginate` is present on the list call and that concatenated page arrays are normalised (two pages of 100 → 200 records, no `][` in the parsed payload). **Include a page boundary that is `"]\n["` rather than `"]["`**, because that is the case `require_human_approval.py`'s own comment records as the reason a naive `replace` is wrong.
- **`test_list_page_bound_truncates_loudly`** (F33) — 6 full pages offered, 5 consumed; asserts `WARN candidate-list-truncated`, **`unevaluated:list-truncated=yes`** (*revision 7*), `complete=no`, **and exit 0 when a candidate is emitted, exit 3 when none is** (*revision 7, RP4-2: two cases, not "exit 0"*).
- **`test_list_truncated_is_a_flag_outside_U`** — **NEW in revision 7 (run 3's TP-3, reinstated by RP4-3).** With Step C truncated, assert that the `UNEVALUATED` line carries `unevaluated:list-truncated=yes`, a **literal `yes`, never a number**. Assert `unevaluated == sufficient + cost-ceiling + query-failed` exactly, and `scanned == evaluated + unevaluated`. **Pair it with an untruncated run:** the same line carries `=no` whenever it is emitted. **The failure it pins** is a count of records the gate never saw, summed into a partition they are not in, and underivable because GitHub's `/issues` returns no total (AM-31). Revision 6 mandated that count while §2.3 Step C forbade it 150 lines earlier.
- **`test_list_truncation_does_not_state_a_count_it_cannot_derive`** — **NEW in revision 5 (AM-31), and it is an ABSENCE assertion.** The truncation report must contain *"at least 500"* and the word *"unknown"*, and **must NOT contain `records=500`** — 500 is the number the gate **fetched**, in the position a reader expects the number that **matched**, and GitHub's `/issues` returns no total. **Revision 4's own `WARN candidate-list-truncated pages=5 records=500` is the defect this test pins**, which is worth saying in the test's docstring: the assertion exists because this document got it wrong, not because someone might.
- **`test_request_ceiling_stops_the_walk_QUIETLY`** (F32) — **REWRITTEN in revision 5 (AM-30).** Asserts `unevaluated:cost-ceiling`, `complete=no`, exit 0 (or 3 if nothing was emitted), `api_requests <= 100` — **and, as an ABSENCE assertion, that NO `WARN` line is emitted for the ceiling.** Revision 4 asserted the opposite. **The inversion is the ruling:** AM-30 withdraws *"must not bind in normal operation"* as unsatisfiable and §0.0.4 measures the population past the ceiling, so a `WARN` here would fire on every cycle from merge onward. **A test asserting the old `WARN` would keep a banner alive that AM-30 removed**, which is why the assertion is inverted rather than deleted.
- **`test_api_requests_never_exceeds_the_ceiling`** — **NEW in revision 5, and applied as a property to EVERY fixture in this file, not as one test** (AM-30, subsuming RP-21). The counter increments **after** a request returns, successfully or not; the stop test runs **before** a request is issued; **so `api_requests <= effective_ceiling` holds at every exit.** RP-21 found an off-by-one in the old `auth_checks` accounting; **a property-style assertion catches it wherever it is reintroduced, which a single test at one boundary does not.**
- **`test_early_exit_is_quiet_but_reported`** (F31) — asserts `unevaluated:sufficient` and `complete=no` are present **and that no `WARN` line is emitted.** The routine stop must not train the operator to ignore WARNs. **Revision 5 makes this the common path rather than the rare one**, which raises the cost of getting it wrong.
- **`test_early_exit_stops_at_max_candidates_and_admits_nothing_after`** — **NEW in revision 5 (the human's directive 1).** A fixture with 20 eligible records and `--max-candidates 5`: exactly **5** are emitted, the `gh` stub records **no events call after the fifth eligible record was found**, and **a trusted-authored record at walk position 12 is NOT emitted** even though evaluating it would have been free. **The last clause is the one that matters** — it is limb (d) at the stop that now fires on nearly every cycle.
- **`test_the_gate_keeps_no_resume_cursor`** — **NEW in revision 5.** Two consecutive runs over an identical fixture produce **byte-identical stdout**, and no file is written. *"The next cycle continues from where this one left off"* is a property of the **world**, not of the gate: **a resume cursor would be a cached selection determination**, the same class of object FR7 forbids for authorizations.
- **`test_pagination_and_ceiling_interaction_is_ordered_not_arbitrary`** — **the panel's §3(c) item 2, answered in code rather than in prose.** 500 records across 5 pages, 3 `priority:critical` records among the last 100 (i.e. the oldest, the region a single page would have dropped), ceiling 100. Assert **all three criticals are evaluated**. This is the constructive answer to *"is there a population where the combination is worse than either alone?"*: **pagination widens the ranked set, the ceiling truncates the ranked tail, and because ranking sits between them the widening can only ever move a high-priority record INTO the evaluated region, never out of it.** Under revision 3 those three records were unreachable at any cap; under revision 4 they are evaluated first.
- **`test_max_list_pages_is_not_a_flag`** — the asymmetry in §2.2: the cost ceiling may be lowered, the page bound may not, because one truncation is ordered and the other is not.

**The `complete=yes` invariant (AM-20) — NEW in revision 4. Tested as an invariant, not as a happy path.**

- **`test_budget_clamp_that_bites_is_unevaluated_not_gated_and_stops_the_walk`** — **NEW in revision 7 (run 3's TP-2, reinstated by RP4-3; the clamp half of RP4-1).** RUN4's counterexample 1, as a fixture. Same rank: `#30` untrusted with its authorizing `labeled` event on **events page 2**, `#20` trusted-authored, `#10` untrusted and authorized on page 1. Run at `--max-api-requests 2`. Assert all of the following. **`#30` is counted under `unevaluated:cost-ceiling`, NOT `gated`** (`gated=0`). `select_work_candidates: unevaluated:cost-ceiling issue=#30` is present. `WARN budget-clamped-events-fetch issue=#30` is present and **`WARN events-page-bound-reached` is ABSENT**. **`#20` is NOT emitted**, although it is free: the bite is the stop. `#10` is not reached. `complete=no`, `eligible=0`, **no `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` block** (`gated=0`), **exit 3**, and `api_requests == 2`. **Three companion cases:** the same fixture where `#30`'s event history **ends** on page 1 with no match, which is a complete fetch and so a determination: gated, `no-codeowner-actor`, and the walk continues. The same fixture where `#30`'s match is **on page 1**: eligible, emitted. And a stub whose last page gives **no `Link` information**, which must be treated as a bite (§1.4 rule 4(a)).
- **`test_a_quarantine_hole_may_outrank_an_admitted_record_and_is_named`** — **NEW in revision 7 (RP4-1(ii); RUN4 §1 asked for it, because "nothing in §6.2 currently exercises a hole").** RUN4's counterexample 3, as a fixture: `#1678` `priority:critical` and authorized, with its events query failing; `#1600` `priority:medium` and authorized. Assert that `#1600` **is emitted**, that **`WARN unevaluated:query-failed issue=#1678` is present**, that `query-failed=1` on the `UNEVALUATED` line, `complete=no` and exit 0. **This test pins the restated rank dominance in both directions:** the higher record *may* be omitted, and when it is, the gate *must* name it.
- **`test_admission_holds_over_determinations_under_quarantine`** — **NEW in revision 7 (RP4-1(ii)).** RUN4's counterexample 2: `#30/#20/#10` same rank, all authorized, `#30`'s query failing, at `--max-candidates 1`. Assert `stdout == [#20]` and `unevaluated:query-failed issue=#30`. **The property is stated over determinations, so `{#20}` is the correct admitted set, not a violation.** The assertion is on the named hole, not on `#30`'s absence alone.
- **`test_complete_is_yes_only_when_nothing_was_skipped`** — a **parameterised sweep** over every path that can end a walk early (sufficiency, cost ceiling, **a clamp that bites** (*revision 7*), **a quarantine** (*revision 7*), list truncation, and all in combination) asserting `complete=no` in each, plus the unbounded case asserting `complete=yes`. **This is the failure the AM-20/AM-21 pair exists to make impossible** — the panel's §3(c) item 3 is *"is there a state where `complete=yes` is emitted and the determination is not complete?"* — so it is tested exhaustively over the state space rather than at one point in it.
- **`test_summary_counts_are_internally_consistent`** — `scanned == evaluated + unevaluated`, `evaluated == eligible + gated`, `authorized <= eligible`, **and (revision 7, TP-3) `unevaluated == sufficient + cost-ceiling + query-failed`**, in every fixture in this file. A property-style assertion applied to all cases, not a separate fixture.
- **`test_unevaluated_records_are_not_counted_as_gated`** — the core of AM-20. A ceiling-truncated walk with 5 determinations-of-held and 40 never-reached records must report `gated=5 unevaluated=40`, **not** `gated=45`. Counted as gated, the line mislabels a **starved** issue as *"held for human authorization"* — a false statement about a person's obligation.

**AM-31's quarantine and AM-32's caller contract — NEW in revision 5, and the first of these is the one behavioural reversal in the ruling set:**

- **`test_failed_events_query_quarantines_ONE_record_and_the_walk_CONTINUES`** (F13) — a fixture of 10 untrusted records in which record 4's events query fails. Assert: the walk **evaluates records 5 through 10**; record 4 is counted under **`unevaluated:query-failed`**; **`gated` does NOT include it**; `complete=no`; and **exit is 0**, because candidates were found. **Revision 4 exited 2 here and emitted nothing.** The docstring must say so, because *"a coder who finds an older document saying F13 exits 2 has found the row that was replaced."*
- **`test_query_failed_is_reported_WITH_the_issue_number`** — a count alone tells an operator a number without telling them which issue to look at, and **their remedy is per-record**. This is the only unevaluated reason that is per-record, and the only one that is an anomaly rather than a routine.
- **`test_every_events_query_failing_yields_exit_3_not_exit_2`** — the pathological case: all 10 records' queries fail. Assert `eligible=0`, `complete=no`, **exit 3**, and that the process did **not** exit 2. **This is the state AM-32 exists for**, and the security reading must be stated in the docstring: an attacker who can fail every query causes fail-closed-for-everything, loudly reported — **there is no input to this path that produces an authorization.**
- **`test_exit_3_requires_BOTH_incomplete_AND_empty`** — the exact condition (§2.2), tested from both sides: a walk that is `complete=no` **with** candidates exits **0**; a walk that is `complete=yes` with **no** candidates exits **0**. **Only the conjunction exits 3.** **This is the test that prevents the most damaging plausible misimplementation** — exit 3 on `complete=no` alone, which at `bin/hos-cron`'s call site, where every non-zero arm of the §2.4 `case` clears `_GATE_CANDIDATES` (*caller description corrected in revision 7, RP4-6(g)*), would **discard usable candidates on the routine early-exit path.** *Revision 7 adds a third case (RP4-2): all-gated with `complete=no` exits **3**, and all-gated with `complete=yes` exits **0**.*
- **`test_tier_resolution_failure_alone_does_not_discard_candidates`** (F35) — collaborator resolution fails **and** a usable candidate is found: assert exit **0**, the candidate **is** emitted, `WARN tier-resolution-failed` is present, and `complete=no`. **This is §2.2's reconciliation of §1.3.2's "exit 3 (degraded)" shorthand, pinned as behaviour** rather than left as a cross-reference two sections apart.
- **`test_unevaluated_tokens_never_appear_in_the_gated_reasons_breakdown`** — AM-20 point 3, asserted as an **absence** on the `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED reasons:` line.
- **`test_all_gated_block_declares_incompleteness`** — F30's new fourth line: when `eligible==0`, `gated>0` **and** `complete=no`, the `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED INCOMPLETE` line is present and names `<U>`. Amendment 3's failure mode (iv) is *"`complete=no` being dropped as a field nobody parses"*; this test is what makes that a red suite rather than a quiet regression.
- **`test_sufficiency_stop_and_all_gated_are_mutually_exclusive`** — AM-21's structural claim, pinned: no fixture can produce both, because the stop requires `eligible ≥ 1` and the block requires `eligible == 0`.

**AM-24's authorization lines — NEW in revision 4:**

- **`test_authorized_count_distinguishes_d5_from_d4`** — a fixture with one trusted-authored candidate and one CODEOWNER-authorized candidate → `eligible=2 authorized=1`.
- **`test_each_d5_authorized_candidate_emits_its_actor`** — one **`AUTHORIZED issue=#<n> actor=<login> via=labeled`** line per such candidate, on stderr. ***`via=` has one legal value in revision 5*** (AR-7; the `milestoned` arm is deleted), and the paired **absence** assertion `test_no_fixture_produces_via_milestoned` above is what makes that checkable rather than assumed. **In the S2 era these lines are the entire machine-readable record that a human's unaided judgement was the only judgement in the loop** (§2.3 Step F) — and they do **not** satisfy FR17, which is **PERMANENTLY DEFERRED in revision 5** (the human's H1 item 3 defers the assessor track indefinitely) and is not testable here because the condition it warns about is universal. **The deferral's permanence raises these lines' significance without changing a byte of them**: they are now the only durable record of the one judgement in the loop, with no successor scheduled.

**AM-13 visibility (F30) — the gate must be loud when it holds everything:**
- **`test_all_gated_emits_the_distinct_held_line`** — `eligible=0, gated>0` produces the `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` block on stderr, with the count, the reason tokens, and the issue numbers
- **`test_empty_milestone_does_not_emit_the_held_line`** — `eligible=0, gated=0` produces the summary line and **nothing else**. This is the test that gives the previous one its meaning: a token emitted in both cases distinguishes nothing.
- `test_held_line_issue_list_is_bounded` — more than 20 gated issues renders `(+k more)` rather than an unbounded line
- ~~`test_all_gated_still_exits_zero` — the held condition is never an exit code and never an error~~ → **`test_all_gated_exit_follows_the_exit_3_rule`** — **RENAMED AND CORRECTED IN REVISION 7 (RUN4 RP4-2).** The old assertion pinned F30's contradiction with §2.2. Two cases: `eligible=0, gated>0, complete=yes` → **exit 0**; `eligible=0, gated>0, complete=no` → **exit 3**. In both, the `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` block is present, the `INCOMPLETE` line is present only in the second, and the exit is never 2. **The held condition is never its own exit code and never an error. Completeness decides the exit.** Build the `complete=no` case the way the cutover produces it: a fully-gated walk that stops on the request ceiling.
- `test_gate_never_creates_an_issue` — the `gh` stub records no `POST` to `/issues` under any input (AM-13 point 3; also FR-scope: §5's "never writes to GitHub")

**Fail-closed** — one test per **live** row of §3, each asserting the exit code, empty stdout where applicable, and the stderr reason token. **Four qualifications, updated for revision 5:**

- **The live row count is 34** (F1, F2, F3, F4, F4b, F4c, F5–F14, F14b, F16, F18, F19, F20, F22–F26, F30–F37) and **six rows are retired stubs with no test**: **F15** (replaced by F31/F32) and **F17, F21, F27, F28, F29** (retired as spent by AR-7 / AM-35). **A coder who writes tests for the stubs is testing code that must not exist**, and the stubs say so.
- **F1, F2, F3 and F4 must assert their DISTINCT tokens AND their remediation lines** (AM-34 point 1). A single test asserting "exit 2 on a bad CODEOWNERS" passes against the shared token that was the defect. **F2's test is the load-bearing one: a CODEOWNERS containing only `@org/core-devs` must produce `codeowners-no-individual-human` and a line saying team handles confer no authorization authority** — not `codeowners-file-absent`, and not a message telling a consumer their valid file is broken.
- **F34–F37 are new and are H3's** (tier parsing, resolution failure, page bound, bot exclusion). Their unit-level halves live in §6.1 with the loader and resolver; **their gate-level halves live here**, because what §3 asserts is the gate's exit and report, not the primitive's return value.
- **F25, F30 and F26 do not live in this file.** F25 and F30's `$LOG_PREFIX` half are `tests/automation/test_hos_cron.py`'s (see §6's test-homes table), **as is AM-32's new exit-3 call-site test**; **F26 is not a behavioural test at all** and §6's preamble says why rather than implying one exists.

**Fail-closed loaders (AM-28(e)) — §1.3.1's table, one test per cell, NEW in revision 4.** The four propagation paths are tested **individually**, not as a group: `codeowners_humans`, `load_trusted_apps`, `load_bot_accounts` and `load_trusted_requesters` each get a file-absent case (→ empty, no exception) and a present-but-unreadable case (→ propagates). **Plus one test the table exists to make possible:** `test_no_loader_catches_bare_exception` — a source-level assertion that `requester_trust.py` contains no `except Exception` and no bare `except:`. That single line, written once, would convert all four propagating failures into silent empties at a stroke — **and for `load_bot_accounts` an empty result is an empty denylist, which promotes every bot to "human"**. It is the most consequential line a coder could write in this module, and a source assertion is the only thing that catches it, because the behavioural tests would all still pass on a *readable* file.

**Named acceptance check the coder must perform and record (not a unit test) — §0.6 gap 6, the decisive live check (AM-14, scoped and binding).**

The premise under E-5 — that re-applying an already-present label emits no new `labeled` event — has been probed read-only as far as it can be (AF-7: 78 issues, 0 duplicates, 0 above one event page, max 17), and no read-only scan of any width can go further, because absence of a duplicate may reflect absence of the *attempt*. The decisive check is a **live mutating re-apply**, and its scope is part of the requirement, not a formality:

- **What:** from the acting identity, apply a label the issue already carries, then read that issue's event list — **paginated, and from the newest end** — for a second `labeled` event with no intervening `unlabeled`.
- **Where: a throwaway surface only** — a scratch issue created for the purpose, or a scratch label on an already-closed issue. **Never on a live candidate issue.** After S2, a stray `unlabeled`/`labeled` pair by a human account on a real issue is **indistinguishable from a cutover authorization act**: the probe would write the very signal the gate reads, before the gate exists to make it legible. Contaminating the evidence base of an authorization mechanism in order to test that mechanism is the kind of self-defeating experiment that is obvious only after it has been run.
- **Who: the `coder`, under a bot identity** — not the human, and not the worker on live state. The behaviour under test is a property of the GitHub API, not of the actor, so a bot-actor probe answers it at strictly lower cost and with no human time.
- **Status: a confirmation, not a precondition.** No ruling depends on the outcome, because §2.7.4's instruction is worded to be correct either way. If a bare re-apply *does* emit a fresh event, the second act becomes unnecessary and the human's burden halves; AM-14's lazy-drain default is unaffected either way. **Report the result on #1539 whichever way it lands** — a gap that has been closed should stop being carried as open.

**Anti-knob (AD-13)** — *updated in revision 5 for the renamed fifth flag, the lowered `--max-candidates` default, and H3's new temptations*
- **`test_parser_exposes_exactly_five_flags`** — `--repo`, `--milestone`, `--label`, `--max-candidates`, **`--max-api-requests`**. **The count does not change in revision 5; the fifth flag's name does.** A parser still exposing `--max-authorization-checks` fails this test, which is the intended migration signal.
- `test_no_repo_root_flag` / `test_no_exclude_label_flag` / **`test_no_max_list_pages_flag`** (§2.2's asymmetry: the ordered truncation may be lowered, the unordered one may not)
- **`test_no_tier_or_collaborator_flag`** — **NEW in revision 5 (H3).** No `--trusted-tier`, `--collaborators`, `--resolve-tiers` or equivalent. **This is the flag a well-meaning implementer will want, and it is AD-13's forbidden widening knob in its purest form:** one command-line argument, no commit, no review, and every `write` collaborator becomes a trusted requester for that invocation. **The roster file is the only place a tier may be named**, and it is a protected surface precisely so that naming one requires a human-gated merge.
- `test_no_environment_variable_grants_trust` — a sweep setting `TRUSTED_REQUESTERS`, `HOS_TRUSTED_APPS`, `HOS_SKIP_GATE`, `BOT_ACCOUNTS=""`, `CODEOWNERS=…` **and, new in revision 5, `HOS_TRUSTED_TIERS` / `TRUSTED_TIERS`** — never makes an untrusted record selectable
- `test_label_flag_cannot_widen_trust` — `--label anything` still gates an untrusted-authored record
- **`test_max_api_requests_can_only_lower`** — `--max-api-requests 10000` clamps to **100**; `--max-api-requests 0` is **legal** and is not clamped upward
- **`test_max_candidates_can_only_lower_and_never_to_zero`** — `--max-candidates 10000` clamps to **5** (*was 10 through revision 4*); `--max-candidates 0` clamps to **1**, not 0. An early exit of zero would emit an empty list on every cycle while exiting 0 — a gate that does nothing while claiming to have worked
- **`test_lowering_a_bound_cannot_change_which_records_are_evaluated_first`** — the anti-knob property specific to AM-19: for any legal `--max-api-requests` **and any legal `--max-candidates`**, the set ~~evaluated~~ **REACHED** (determined, quarantined, or clamped; *wording corrected in revision 7, since a quarantined record is reached but not evaluated*) is a **prefix of the same walk order**. A flag that reordered the walk would be a widening knob wearing a cost-control costume. **Revision 5 extends it to `--max-candidates` because the early exit makes that flag the one an operator will actually tune.**

**No caching (FR7/AD-4)**
- `test_two_consecutive_runs_both_query_live` — no state file is written and the second run makes the same API calls
- `test_authorization_revoked_between_runs_is_not_carried_over`

### 6.3 `test_selection_call_sites.py` — the collapse, **and G11's acceptance mechanism (AM-11: required, not optional)**

This file carries two jobs that revision 1 ran together without saying so. The first is the collapse itself — proving the second implementation is gone. The second is new in revision 2: **it is the mechanism by which `ADR-1542` G11's requirement is satisfied**, and AM-11 makes that mechanism *required*. G11 is superseded rather than merely overlapped (§2.5), which means S2 has taken on G11's acceptance condition; an intention about a prompt string, unpinned by a test, survives exactly until the next person needs a second milestone.

**The collapse**

- `test_next_candidates_jq_is_gone`
- `test_hos_cron_invokes_the_entry_point` / `test_hos_cron_has_no_inline_jq_selection` (no `--jq` on the candidates query, no `$(cat`)
- `test_cron_prompt_invokes_the_entry_point` / `test_cron_prompt_has_no_gh_api_candidate_query` (no `labels=needs-ai` `gh api`, no `--jq`, no `$(`)
- `test_cron_prompt_forbids_improvised_fallback` — the §2.5 "do NOT construct your own query" instruction is present verbatim
- `test_worker_agent_doc_does_not_cite_next_candidates_jq`
- `test_only_one_selection_entry_point_exists` — `select_work_candidates` is referenced from exactly the two call sites plus tests and docs

**G11's four conditions, as one test (AM-11, binding)**

- **`test_cron_prompt_fallback_is_sandbox_allowlistable`** — asserts of the *rendered* Step 2 fallback line, after `_build_prompt`'s `@@MILESTONE_NUMBER@@` substitution, that it contains **no command substitution** (`$(`, backtick), **no variable expansion** (`$`), **no inline `jq`** (`--jq`, `jq -f`, `jq '`), and **no backslash continuation** (a trailing `\`). Those are G11's four conditions verbatim from AM-11, written as four assertions with four distinct failure messages so a regression names which one broke. A single combined regex would report "the fallback is wrong" and leave the next reader to find out how.

  **HOW the test obtains the rendered prompt — specified in revision 4 (AM-28(a), binding).** Revision 3 said "the rendered Step 2 fallback line, after `_build_prompt`'s substitution" and **did not say how a Python test gets that string out of a bash function inside a 2,000-line shell script.** *"An unspecified mechanism in a test that pins a security-relevant string is a test that gets written the easy way"* — and the easy way here is to `read_text()` the prompt **template** and assert against `@@MILESTONE_NUMBER@@`, which passes while proving nothing about the rendered form. **Binding mechanism, and it is already most of the way built:**

  > The test lives in **`tests/automation/test_hos_cron.py`** and uses the existing `CronEnv` harness, which invokes the **real `bin/hos-cron`** as a subprocess against a stubbed world with a `claude` stub on a pinned `PATH`. **The prompt reaches `claude` on stdin** — `_build_prompt | _run_claude` (`bin/hos-cron:1932`, read by me this session). **The stub currently drains stdin to `/dev/null`** (`test_hos_cron.py:102`) and records only its argv and environment. **One line changes: the stub records stdin to a new `HOS_TEST_CLAUDE_PROMPT_LOG` path instead of discarding it**, exactly as it already records its argv to `HOS_TEST_CLAUDE_LOG`. The test then runs a cycle with `HOS_TARGET_MILESTONE_NUMBER` set and asserts G11's four conditions against **the bytes the launcher actually piped to the model**.
  >
  > **This is strictly stronger than unit-testing the bash function**, and it is why the mechanism is worth a paragraph: it exercises substitution, template assembly and the launcher's own quoting in one pass, so a regression anywhere in that chain fails the test. It also needs **no new harness**, no `declare -f` extraction, no sourcing of `hos-cron` into a test shell — all of which are fragile against a script that runs logic at load time — and it introduces no command substitution or heredoc into the test's own shell usage. **The stub extension is a required part of the S2 change set**, not an implementation detail the coder may skip by testing the template instead.

  This is the one test in the plan whose *absence* would leave an accepted ADR item (G11) marked superseded by a change that does not actually satisfy it. **Note what the justification does NOT rest on (AM-28(g), §2.5):** not on the claim that a non-conforming command fails to execute unattended — that premise is unresolved — but on `CLAUDE.md` requiring allowlistability of **every** command in this repo. The test is required either way, and under the `bypassPermissions` reading it is required *more*, because there the failure is silent **execution** rather than silent skipping.

**AM-6 point 3's shipping condition, as tests (`/approve` must stop claiming authorization)**

AM-6 point 3 is *"S2 MUST NOT ship while the repo tells a human that `/approve` authorizes work."* A shipping condition that lives only in prose is a shipping condition nobody can fail. Both artefacts are repo literals, so both belong in this file rather than in §6.2:

- **`test_labels_doc_states_approve_does_not_authorize`** — `docs/LABELS.md`'s `needs-ai` row (or an adjacent note it links) contains the statement that in the S2 era `/approve` **changes no labels and** does **not** authorize autonomous selection (§4.3, §2.7.2). *Wording updated in revision 4: revision 3 asserted "performs a label swap and does not authorize", which is no longer true of the shipped command.*
- **`test_approve_branch_performs_no_label_write`** — **NEW in revision 4, and it is the mechanical form of AM-25.** `.github/workflows/label-swap.yml`'s `/approve` branch (`:73`-`:77`) contains **no `gh issue edit`** and no `--add-label` / `--remove-label`. This is the test that keeps the ruling from being undone by *"someone who reads AM-25's basis as the wording fix rather than the inert one"* — Amendment 3's failure mode (vi), and a live risk precisely because revision 3 argued the opposite for two revisions and stated three reasons for it.
- **`test_label_swap_confirmation_does_not_claim_authorization`** — the `/approve` confirmation body no longer contains the literal `authorized by` (`:75`). This is an *absence* assertion deliberately: the replacement wording is the coder's (§2.7.2 fixes the facts it must carry, not the phrasing), so pinning the new string would be pinning a choice this document did not make, while pinning the old string's absence pins exactly the lie AM-6 forbids.
- **`test_label_swap_confirmation_states_it_does_not_authorize`** — **NEW in revision 4.** The **presence** half, which revision 3 deliberately omitted and which Amendment 3 §4 now asks for: the body contains a "does not authorize" statement, asserted on a load-bearing token rather than the full prose. **This is §9/R4's hole, closed in one direction and left open in the other**: R4 asked whether a replacement could stop claiming authorization while still misleading. Pinning the absence of the lie and the presence of the denial narrows that gap without the architect or me writing the coder's sentence.
- **`test_label_swap_decline_path_is_unchanged`** — **`:79`**'s `/decline` sibling is untouched — its `gh issue edit --add-label wontfix`, its `gh issue close` (`:80`) and its comment (`:81-82`) all survive. *Line number corrected in revision 4: revision 3 cited `:83`* (AM-28(c); re-derived by me, §0.0.3). S2's edit is one command's branch, not a workflow rewrite, and this test says so mechanically.

**AM-25's runbook requirements, as tests — NEW in revision 4.** `docs/OVERSIGHT-RUNBOOK.md` joins the conformance set for the same reason `docs/LABELS.md` did: it is a repo literal, and AM-1 makes a documented approval path that silently does nothing worse than no gate.

- **`test_runbook_intervention_states_the_act_is_from_your_own_account`** — the "Intervening" control 1 passage carries the own-account qualifier.
- **`test_runbook_intervention_forbids_edit_issue_app_human`** — it names `edit_issue.sh --app human` as **not** an approval path. The prohibition must be where the instruction is; a reader following control 1 will not go looking in `docs/LABELS.md` for a caveat.
- **`test_runbook_intervention_covers_the_already_labelled_case`** — the remove-then-re-add case is present.

**AM-27's documentation requirements, as tests — NEW in revision 4.**

- **`test_labels_doc_states_milestone_route_is_forbidden`** — **AM-27 point 2, binding.** ~~`docs/LABELS.md` carries the prohibition on the milestone route **with the Step 0 race as its stated reason**.~~ **CORRECTED IN REVISION 7 (RUN4 RP4-6(c)) to match §4.3 statement (5), which revision 5 rewrote and this test did not follow.** `docs/LABELS.md` states that **a milestone assignment authorizes nothing** (AR-7), **with that as the reason the route is refused**. If the Step 0 race appears, it MUST be labelled as the historical reason the route was attractive, **not** as the reason it is refused (§4.3 (5)). Assert the statement **and** its reason, and assert the race is not presented as the reason. The test name is kept because §6's binding list cites it. Assert both: a prohibition with no reason attached is the first thing a later editor removes as fussy. Revision 3 left this rule with **no documentation home and no test** — its only home was H1's clearance text, and **a prohibition whose only home is one-shot clearance text expires the moment the human answers.** This is the rule the design's own failure mode (ix) is about.
- **`test_the_files_s2_edits_do_not_use_the_phrase_cutover_material`** — **AM-27 point 1, RE-SCOPED BY AM-38 (binding).** **The assertion's scope is the files S2 actually edits, and nothing else.** Revision 4 specified it as *"a repo-wide absence assertion over `docs/v0.7.0/**`"*; **the phrase survives 11 times across 6 files, including the immutable run-1 panel artifact and `REQUIREMENTS-1540-AMENDMENT-1`'s FR29 text — so as specified the test cannot pass without editing other authors' accepted rulings and a historical artifact, which AM-38's immutability rule forbids.** *"AM-27 point 1's purpose — that the phrase be **defined** and not used **loosely in new text** — is met by that scope; a repo-wide sweep was never what it needed."* **The occurrences in accepted historical documents stay exactly where they are.** Retiring a term is durable if re-introducing it **into new text** fails; a test that can only pass by rewriting history is a test that will be satisfied by rewriting history.
- **`test_excluded_labels_is_defined_in_exactly_one_place`** — **NEW in revision 5 (AM-37 / RP-11, binding: the divergence test is REPLACED, not deleted).** §4.1 deletes `next_candidates.jq`, so ~~`test_jq_label_literals_match_hos_labels`~~ — which guarded the `.jq` file against its inlined prompt twin — **has nothing left to guard, and the seam it protected becomes a single-site invariant instead.** *(Corrected in revision 7, RUN4 RP4-6(k), run 3's TP-9: `test_jq_label_literals_match_hos_labels` does not exist. It is a test `TECHNICAL-DESIGN-1604` planned and never built, and a grep of `tests/` this session returns nothing. The guard that actually exists is `tests/automation/test_next_candidates.py`'s `TestBothSelectionPathsAgree` (class at `:137` as read this session; run 3 cited `:127` before #1805 moved it). It is ported and retired per §4.2. The replacement assertion below is sound on its own terms, and only its premise named a phantom.)* A repo-wide assertion that the `needs-human` exclusion literal appears in **exactly one** definition site (`select_work_candidates.py`'s `EXCLUDED_LABELS`) and in no second implementation — not in `worker-cron-prompt.md`, not in `bin/hos-cron`, not in a second module. **One test in, one test out.** **This is the seam `ADR-1604` AD-5 will write its entries into** (AM-9, §4.5), and *"the protected-file constant stops being unguarded the moment its guard is removed"* — which is the whole of RP-11's finding: deleting a guard in the same change as deleting the thing it guarded leaves a window nobody opened deliberately.

**AM-26's ship-set requirements, as tests — NEW in revision 4.**

- **`test_new_framework_modules_are_shipped_to_consumers`** — `select_work_candidates.py` and `requester_trust.py` are present in `scripts/framework/framework_consumer_files.txt`.
- **`test_trusted_requesters_roster_is_not_shipped`** — the live `scripts/framework/trusted-requesters.txt` is **absent** from that list. **An absence test with a reason in its docstring**, because Amendment 3 names *"the roster being added for completeness by a later contributor"* as failure mode (v), and the failure it produces — an HOS upgrade silently deleting a consumer's trusted contributors — is invisible from this repo.
- **`test_trusted_requesters_example_is_shipped_and_documents_both_entry_forms`** — **NEW in revision 5 (RP-19 + H3).** `scripts/framework/trusted-requesters.txt.example` is **present in `framework_consumer_files.txt`** *and* **exists in the tree** (revision 4 ruled it into the ship list and gave it no home, so `test_every_listed_file_exists_in_source` would have failed the moment the list named a file nothing created — §1.1 fixes that and this test pins it), and its content carries **both entry forms**, the **`tier:` prefix rule**, and the statement that **matching is exact rather than hierarchical** (§4.3). **A consumer cannot be expected to guess that `write` is a login and `tier:write` is a tier.**
- **`test_one_consumer_shipped_artefact_carries_FR29s_three_statements`** — **NEW in revision 5, and it is `pm-agent`'s AR-9 verify clause made mechanical (FR29(b), a BLOCKING shipping condition per AR-10).** Assert that **at least one path listed in `scripts/framework/framework_consumer_files.txt`** contains FR29's statements (i) that the act authorizes autonomous work on that specific issue, (ii) that this **includes issues the CODEOWNER did not author and whose content they do not control**, and (iii) what the act does *not* do. **Plus AM-34 point 3's two preconditions** (an individual human in CODEOWNERS; `machine-accounts.env` must exist) **and AM-37/RP-9's personal-access-token route.** *"The test fails if the list is edited to drop that path, and it fails if the statements are edited out of it."* **`coder` satisfies FR29(b) when this test exists and passes; the overseer checks FR29(b) by running it.** **Note what it does NOT require** (AR-9, and I am carrying the limit as well as the obligation): it does **not** require `docs/` to ship, does **not** require the installer to change, and does **not** fix the repo-wide documentation-shipping defect — that defect is pre-existing, is **not S2's**, and stays a follow-up upheld twice (AR-9 and Amendment 4 §4). **Q12 is the human's ratification of exactly this scope and is unanswered; FR29(b) is a MERGE condition rather than a design gate, so revision 5 specifies the artefact and its test and records that the choice of scope remains theirs.**
- **`test_every_shipped_framework_module_has_its_framework_imports_shipped`** — **AM-26's binding detector, and the one that generalises.** Parse each `scripts/framework/**` entry in the ship list, collect its `scripts.framework.*` imports, and assert each imported module is itself on the list. For this change set that is `select_work_candidates.py` → `requester_trust.py` → `require_human_approval.py`. **The existing guard test checks only that *listed* files exist, so it cannot see a listed file whose dependency is unlisted** — which is exactly how P-4 arose. Without this test P-4 recurs the next time a shipped module grows an import, and it recurs **invisibly from this repo**: the symptom is a consumer's worker going quiet with a `ModuleNotFoundError` that carries no `$LOG_PREFIX` and no `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` token.

**FR8's own verify clause, as a test — NEW in revision 4 (AM-28(e), binding).** FR8 states: *"a test enumerates the selection paths and asserts agreement."* **Revision 3 had no such test** — §6.3 asserted *reference counts* (`test_only_one_selection_entry_point_exists`), which proves there is one implementation, not that the surviving paths **agree**. Those are different claims, and FR8 asks for the second.

- **`test_all_selection_paths_agree_on_an_unapproved_untrusted_issue`** — one fixture (untrusted author, `needs-ai` applied by the worker bot, in-milestone), evaluated through **both** surviving selection paths: the gate's CLI, and `probe.py`'s milestone strategy. **Both must return a negative verdict.** FR8 names three paths; after §4.1's deletion the worker's Step-2 fallback **is** the gate's CLI, which the test must assert rather than assume — so the test also pins that `worker-cron-prompt.md`'s fallback command is character-identical to the one `bin/hos-cron` invokes.
- **Why this matters beyond the requirement:** FR8 is one of the four FRs `ADR-1540` §3's slice table says S2 **closes**. **Closing a requirement whose own verify clause has no test is how FR4 came to be cited zero times** in 1,363 lines — and P-2 is what that looks like after three revisions.

**AM-15's documentation requirements, as tests — new in revision 3.** AM-15 makes two wording requirements binding, and §0.0.2 records why they need pinning rather than prose: `docs/LABELS.md` declares itself doc-only, and "no conformance test" is one of its own acknowledged gaps, so an unpinned sentence there survives exactly until the next tidy-up. Both artefacts are repo literals, so both belong here:

- **`test_labels_doc_states_the_authorizing_act`** — `docs/LABELS.md` carries §2.7.4's instruction in substance: the act is performed from the human's **own GitHub account**, `edit_issue.sh --app human` is named as **not** an approval path, and the **remove-then-re-add** case for an issue that already carries the label is present. Assert the load-bearing tokens, not the full prose — a test that pins every word makes the sentence unmaintainable and will be deleted rather than updated.
- **`test_labels_doc_states_the_event_pair_is_the_authorization_record`** — the registry contains AM-15's answer to the churn misreading (the `unlabeled`/`labeled` pair by the human's own account **is** the authorization record, not an undo-redo). This is the one sentence standing between a future reader and "this looks like noise, let's document a quieter act."
- **~~`test_labels_doc_states_approve_can_consume_the_additive_act`~~ → REPLACED IN REVISION 5 BY `test_labels_doc_states_approve_cannot_consume_the_additive_act` (RP-16, with the architect's constraint applied).**

  > **This is RP-16, and it is the clearest self-contradiction the panel found in revision 4.** §6.3 required **two** tests of `docs/LABELS.md`: `test_approve_branch_performs_no_label_write` (AM-25 — the command writes **no** label) and `test_labels_doc_states_approve_can_consume_the_additive_act` (AM-15 — the command **consumes** the human's additive act by writing a label). **Both cannot be true of the shipped command, and a registry row asserting that `/approve` both can and cannot consume the additive act fails FR29(a)(iii) on its face.**
  >
  > **The architect's constraint is the whole of the resolution: *"whichever test is deleted, the surviving statement MUST be the true one."*** **After AM-25, `/approve` writes no label, so it CANNOT consume the additive act. The true statement is the negative one, and it is the one that survives.**
  >
  > **The replacement is an assertion, not a deletion, and the reason is AM-15's harm rather than AM-15's wording.** AM-15's finding — that `label-swap.yml:29` has no label-state precondition, so `/approve` applied the dispatch label as `github-actions[bot]` and **consumed the one genuinely additive act the human still had** — is *why the command was made inert*. **A reader who finds AM-15's "actively harmful" language in this chain must be able to see that it is the diagnosis that motivated AM-25, not a live property of the shipped command**, and `docs/LABELS.md` is where they will look. **Binding: the registry states that `/approve` changes no labels, authorizes nothing, and therefore cannot consume the authorizing act — and names the removal of the label write as the reason.** Pinning the *negative* is what stops a later contributor restoring the write and leaving the documentation describing the world it created.

### 6.4 `test_probe.py` — **no longer "additive only"** (AM-4)

**Revision 1's acceptance criterion — "all fifteen existing tests pass unmodified" — is WITHDRAWN**, and this section is where that shows up as work. §1.6 explains why: behaviour-preservation was the wrong goal, and it is what produced the unsafe default AM-4 removed. **A test that must change is the honest signal that behaviour changed.**

> **REVISION 5: THE OBLIGATION IS ONE TEST, NOT SIX — and it is a finding, not a saving. §1.6 carries the full reasoning; this is the test-plan statement of it.**
>
> **All five of revision 4's fixture rows existed for one reason between them: the milestone-object skip rule (AM-18), which AM-35 retires.** With the skip gone, records built by `_make_issue()` (**`:42-46`**) reach the patched `_verify_codeowner_actor` again, `mock_verify_codeowner.assert_called_once()` passes, and **none of the five needs an edit.** **AM-28(d)'s shared-`_make_issue()` remedy is therefore NOT to be applied** — a coder who applies it "for completeness" is repairing a fixture for a rule that no longer exists.
>
> **The sixth row un-vacuums itself, and that is the consequence worth checking.** `TestProbeRepoMilestone::test_skips_issue_with_no_verified_codeowner_actor` (**`:318-335`**) is **#1539's own regression test** — its docstring says so: *"#1539: an issue whose needs-ai/milestone assignment was not applied by a verified human CODEOWNER must not be returned as a candidate."* Under AM-18's skip it passed **for the wrong reason**, because the record was dropped before the authorization check ran. **With the skip retired, the record reaches `_verify_codeowner_actor`, the patched `None` is consulted, and the test exercises exactly the subject in its docstring.** AM-28(d) was right that the test was vacuous; **a different ruling in the same change set removes the cause rather than the symptom, which is the better outcome and was nobody's plan.**
>
> **What must be CHECKED rather than assumed, and it is the coder's first task in S1:** run `test_probe.py` unmodified against the refactored module **before** editing any fixture. **Five of revision 4's six rows should pass; exactly one should fail** (the row below). **If more than one fails, a premise above is wrong — escalate to `technical-design` rather than fixing fixtures until the suite is green.** Fixing fixtures until green is precisely how the vacuous sixth arose in the first place.

**The ONE existing test that must change:**

| Test | Kind of change | Assertion changes? |
|---|---|---|
| `TestCodeownersActorVerification::test_verified_when_milestone_applied_by_codeowner_human` (`:476-483`) | **INVERTED and renamed to `test_milestoned_event_never_authorizes`.** The `milestoned` arm is **deleted** (AR-7 / AM-35), so this input returns `None`. **`_milestoned_event()` (`:465`) is RETAINED as its fixture and is NOT extended to emit a `milestone` object** — revision 4 required that extension to exercise AM-4's title binding, and there is no longer a title to bind | **YES — inverted, not weakened.** AM-37 names this test specifically: it *"tests a channel that is gone."* **Do not delete it — deleting it removes the only mechanical statement that the channel was removed**, and §1.6's acceptance criterion requires the PR body to carry "this test changed because the behaviour changed" for it |

**Two line-number facts re-derived by me this session and retained even though the fixture work is retired**, because the coder must still *find* `_milestoned_event()` to feed the inverted test, and because a retired obligation carrying a wrong citation is how a future reader concludes the retirement was careless: `_milestoned_event()` is at **`:465`** (revision 3 cited `:466`) and `_make_issue()` at **`:42-46`** (revision 3 cited `:41-46`).

**Revision 1's acceptance criterion was already false for those four `TestProbeRepoMilestone` fixtures, and that remains my defect** (§1.6, §7.3) even though a later ruling has made the symptom vanish. I wrote a skip rule into revision 1's §1.6 and did not check the file's fixtures against it. **A defect that stops manifesting is not a defect that was found**, and AM-18's endorsement of the widening as *"coverage recovery rather than a tax"* is retired with the rule that caused it rather than claimed as a benefit.

**The `WARN milestone-object-absent` test is RETIRED with the diagnostic (AM-35).** Revision 4 required `test_probe_skips_issue_with_no_milestone_object` to assert a new **stderr** line from a module that writes none and does not import `sys` (verified again this revision, §0.0.4). **The skip, the diagnostic, the new output surface, and the coder's obligation to audit whether `multi_customer.py` captures `probe.py`'s stderr all go together.** **This is a real reduction in S1's blast radius** and the one place in revision 5 where less work is unambiguously better work.

**The replacement acceptance criterion** (from §1.6, restated as a check a reviewer can run): every existing assertion either passes unmodified or is changed only in its fixture; every changed test is listed in the PR body with the sentence *"this test changed because the behaviour changed"*; and **any test whose assertion must *weaken* is a defect in the change, not in the test.** The same applies to any test asserting the exact `_run_gh` argument list, which changes when `--paginate` is added.

**Added tests:**
- `test_codeowners_humans_is_the_shared_function` — `probe._codeowners_humans is requester_trust.codeowners_humans`
- `test_verify_codeowner_actor_delegates_to_shared_predicate`
- **`test_probe_call_site_passes_no_milestone_title`** — **REPLACES revision 4's `test_probe_passes_expected_milestone_title`, which asserted the opposite (AR-7 / AM-35).** `probe.py:428-431` calls the shared predicate with **four arguments** and no milestone title. **Revision 4 was going to ADD an argument here; revision 5 asserts the call site is not edited at all** (§1.6), which makes this the cheapest available detector for a deletion that missed this call site.
- **~~`test_probe_milestoned_event_for_a_different_title_does_not_authorize`~~ — RETIRED (AM-35).** It tested AM-4's title binding at `probe.py`'s own call site; the parameter and the channel are both gone. **Replaced by `test_probe_milestoned_event_does_not_authorize_at_the_shipped_call_site`** — a `milestoned` event by a verified human CODEOWNER, for the milestone being queried, returns no candidate. **This is the test that proves the shipped defect is closed in the module consumer deployments inherit**, and it is why §7.3 routes `_verify_codeowner_actor`'s approvals to re-review rather than letting them stand — **with the re-review's CONTENT changed: it is now a review of a function whose milestone arm is absent, not one whose milestone arm is title-bound.**
- **~~`test_probe_skips_issue_with_no_milestone_object`~~ — RETIRED with AM-18 (AM-35).** The skip no longer exists, so a test asserting it would pin a branch the design removed.
- `test_probe_events_fetch_is_bounded_paginated` — the `--paginate` argument list and the 10-page bound (AM-5, **confirmed unchanged by AM-30**), including that the bound-hit path returns `None` rather than raising.
- **`test_probe_does_not_construct_a_trusted_set_or_resolve_tiers`** — **NEW in revision 5 (H3).** `probe.py` calls `codeowners_humans` and `load_bot_accounts` directly, exactly as it does today; it does **not** call `load_trusted_set` and does **not** call `fetch_collaborators`. **H3's tier machinery is the gate's**, and `load_trusted_set(collaborators=None)` is why `probe.py` needs no change at all (§1.3).

---

## 7. Recorded deviations, residuals, and the startup-gap analysis

### 7.1 Named deviations from current behaviour

- **DEV-1 — pull-request records are excluded. APPROVED by AM-12, conditional on one test.** The `issues` REST endpoint returns PRs (they share the number sequence, #1236). `next_candidates.jq` has no `select(.pull_request == null)`; `bootstrap/query_issues.sh --list` and `bin/hos-cron`'s milestone-less gate both do. Without the filter, a bounced worker PR (`needs-ai`, in-milestone, draft) would be run through the *issue* gate, found untrusted, and dropped for want of a CODEOWNER actor — a silent behaviour change either way. The architect's reason for approving is sharper than mine and is worth carrying: leaving PR records in would mean the *issue* gate silently adjudicates PR eligibility, which contradicts AD-14's scope boundary far more than filtering them does. **The condition has teeth:** §6.2's `test_bounced_draft_pr_still_reaches_the_worker_and_blocks_the_skip` must demonstrate that a bounced draft PR carrying the dispatch label is still surfaced through `bin/hos-cron`'s PR path (`:1085-1099`) and still prevents the `#1395` no-actionable-work early skip, **with the candidate list empty**. If that test cannot be made to pass, **DEV-1 is withdrawn and PR records stay in** — escalate to the architect. Do **not** adjust the skip logic to make it pass; that repairs the gate's blast radius by widening it.
- **DEV-2 — `2>/dev/null` removed from the `bin/hos-cron` candidates call. REQUIRED, not optional (AM-13 point 1).** Revision 1 filed this as "logging only", which was true and beside the point. It is no control-flow change *in the gate*, and it is the **carrier** for AM-13's `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` block (§2.3 Step F, §2.4): with `2>/dev/null` in place, the one signal distinguishing "every candidate is held for a human" from "the milestone is empty" is written to a stream nobody reads, and the most likely operational failure of this whole mechanism becomes invisible for as long as nobody looks. A fail-closed gate that says nothing is indistinguishable from an empty backlog.
- **DEV-3 — `probe.py`'s `bot_accounts` default becomes file-baseline ∪ env** instead of env-only. Strictly stricter; identical when the env is set, as it is in every current invocation.
- **OBS-1 — `probe.py` does not gain `requester_verdict`.** Its milestone strategy already requires a verified CODEOWNER actor for *every* issue, which is stricter than the gate's trusted-author fast path. Adding requester-trust there could only widen it. Consequence worth recording: in a consumer deployment, a worker-filed `[BLOCKED]` issue is **not** auto-selectable through `probe.py` — existing behaviour, unchanged here.
- **OBS-2 — `probe.py`'s milestone strategy applies no `needs-human` exclusion.** Unchanged by S1/S2, and the reason the marker table's non-live entries are not dead weight (§1.5.1(iii)).
- **DEV-4 — NEW in revision 4 (AM-26). `/approve` stops writing labels, and `machine-accounts.env` becomes a precondition for work selection.** Two consumer-visible behaviour changes that revision 3 did not carry as deviations, because one did not exist yet and the other was never noticed.
  - **`/approve` no longer performs the `needs-human` → `needs-ai` swap** (§2.7.2, AM-25). This is **user-visible behaviour for the one human this system has**, so it is disclosed at H1 rather than absorbed — and the human may decline it, in which case the wording-only mitigation is taken instead. The security properties are identical either way.
  - **After S2, an absent or empty `scripts/framework/machine-accounts.env` stops work selection entirely** (Step B1/B3 → exit 2). **Today it does not**: that file is not shipped, not generated by the installer, and is needed only by the merge gates — so a consumer can and does run cron cycles without it. It fails closed, it is visible via DEV-2, and it is **a real change to the consumer contract that no document stated** until AM-26. §4.3 gives it two homes (the S2 work item and the PR body's upgrade note), because a precondition discovered by watching a worker go quiet is a precondition that was not documented.
- **DEV-5 — RESTATED IN REVISION 5 (AM-30 + the human's early-exit directive). The gate's per-cycle GitHub API cost rises from ≈26 requests to AT MOST ≈100 requests, and to as few as ≈2 in the typical case.** Recorded as a named deviation rather than left in §2.2.1's prose, because it is a change to the **operating envelope** of a path that runs every five minutes.
  - **Revision 4 recorded this as "≈105 requests" and that figure is WITHDRAWN as a category error, not corrected as an estimate.** The bound was denominated in **authorization checks**, and one check is **1–10 requests**, so the true worst case was **up to 1,000 requests per cycle — roughly 12,000/hour against a 5,000/hour limit.** AM-30's phrasing is the one to carry: *"a bound whose unit is not the rationed resource cannot bound the rationed resource."* **≈105 was a floor described as a ceiling.**
  - **After AM-30 the bound is denominated in requests and held at ≈100, so it is a REAL ceiling** — ≈1,200 requests/hour, ≈24% of the installation limit. **The number did not move; its unit and its truth did.**
  - **After the human's early exit, the *typical* cost is a small multiple of the distance to the first usable candidate** — 1–5 list requests plus one events fetch per untrusted record walked before it, as few as **2** when the head of the queue is usable. **The worst case is unchanged and is reached exactly when nothing is usable**, which is the post-merge drain state AM-14's lazy drain creates. **So the ordinary week is now cheap and the expensive weeks are still expensive**, which is a genuine improvement and not a resolution.
  - **Provenance: the ceiling is a hard bound; the BUDGET SHARE is reasoned and not measured** (§0.6 gap 9) — ~~the overseer's own cron consumption is unaccounted for, the architect says so, and Amendment 4 §3(c) item 4 asks the third panel run to attack it~~ *corrected in revision 7 (RUN4 RP4-6(h)): run 3's M-5 measured the overseer as a separate GitHub App with its own 5,000/hour bucket, so it is not a consumer of this budget. The share is still a choice rather than a measurement, now about a bucket shared only with the rest of the worker App's cycle, at a cadence run 3 measured at 6 cycles/hour rather than the 12 assumed (M-4)*. **§8/E-8 is CLOSED by AM-30** and is no longer the open half of this row.
- **DEV-6 — NEW in revision 5 (H3). The trust determination acquires a LIVE DEPENDENCE on GitHub's collaborator-permission graph, and a network call enters the trust path for the first time.** Recorded as a deviation because it changes the **inputs** to a security determination, which is a larger category of change than a cost or a log line.
