  - **What changes:** a roster entry may now be a **GitHub permission tier**, resolved at check time against `repos/<repo>/collaborators`. **Who is a trusted requester therefore becomes a function of repository settings that live outside the repository**, and can change with no commit, no review and no CODEOWNERS approval. **That is the human's explicit, deliberate trade** (*"the reviewable artifact is the policy decision, not the individual names"*) and it **overrides the architect's recommended "no"**, which rested precisely on keeping the boundary inside a committed file.
  - **What does NOT change, and it is the half a reviewer must confirm rather than assume:** the committed roster still records the **policy**; the roster remains a **protected surface**, so *naming* a trusted tier still requires a human-gated merge; **no tier entry confers authorization authority** (`tier_members` is absent from `verify_codeowner_actor` by construction); and **HOS ships zero trusted tiers**, so the feature is inert on every deployment until a project owner opts in.
  - **Cost: 0 requests when no tier is listed** — every deployment today, including this one — **1–3 when one is** (§1.3.2). **Failure direction: fail-closed and loud** — an empty resolution can only shrink the trusted set, and it reports `WARN tier-resolution-failed` with `complete=no` rather than proceeding silently.
  - **The declared gap that travels with it: the `role_name` field is asserted, not observed** (§0.6 gap 8). **This is revision 5's single most load-bearing unobserved claim**, §6.1 carries it as a named coder acceptance check, and §9 nominates it to the panel.

### 7.2 Residuals accepted for S2

> **REVISION 5 — THE DISPOSITION OF THIS WHOLE SECTION CHANGES, and it is the largest consequence of the human's rulings anywhere in this document.** H1 item 3 defers the assessor agent (Layer 3 / S5) **indefinitely** — *"the standing state, not a temporary bridge to a near-term S5"* — and H1 item 5 adds *"no digest-binding fix is being built either, consistent with deferring the assessor track."* **Two residuals therefore stop being "closed later" and become PERMANENTLY ACCEPTED, and a third loses its schedule without losing its closure rule.** Every *"closed by S3"* and *"arrives in S5"* claim in this document has been swept against those two sentences, and the results are stated per residual rather than in a note, because **a residual whose closure date silently evaporates is exactly the object this chain keeps finding.**
>
> | Residual | Revision 4 | Revision 5 | Why |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | **R-1** | *"Closed by S3 (AD-5's digest binding)"* | **PERMANENTLY ACCEPTED** | **The human states no digest-binding fix is being built.** The mechanism that was to close it is not on any schedule |
> | **R-2** | *"closed by S3's invariant 4 (approval newer than the assessment)"* | **PERMANENTLY ACCEPTED** | **Its closure was defined in terms of an ASSESSMENT.** With no assessor, there is no assessment for an approval to be newer than — the invariant is not merely unscheduled, it is **undefined** |
> | **R-3** | *"ACCEPTED for S2, CLOSED in S3"* | **ACCEPTED for S2; its S3 closing rule STANDS and has NO SCHEDULED DATE** | AM-36 confirms AM-8 without change, and the rule does not depend on an assessor — **it is unscheduled, not undefined.** The distinction from R-1/R-2 is real and is the reason R-3 is not downgraded with them |
> | **FR17** | *"deliberately deferred to S5"* | **PERMANENTLY DEFERRED; Q4 is moot** | §2.3 Step F carries the full statement |
>
> **What this changes for the human, and it is why they were asked rather than told:** in the S2 era their judgement at the moment they apply the label is the **only** judgement in the loop, they make it with no assessment in front of them, **and that is now the permanent arrangement rather than an interim one.** They accepted it in those terms: *"Accepted, given item 3's deferral reframes this as permanent, not temporary. Both named consequences are accepted."* **Nothing here is a design change; it is the same mechanism with a truthful expiry date, which is the difference between a residual and a plan.**

- **R-1 (ADR-named, required to be recorded) — PERMANENTLY ACCEPTED in revision 5 (the human's H1 items 3 and 5).** An untrusted author can edit the issue title or body **after** a CODEOWNER authorizes it. S2's authorization binds to the issue, not to its content. **Revision 4 recorded this as "closed by S3 (AD-5's digest binding)". The human has ruled that no digest-binding fix is being built**, consistent with deferring the assessor track, **so there is no mechanism scheduled to close it and this document must stop implying one.** It reaches the human directly in H1 item 5, in `pm-agent`'s words — *"what you read is not guaranteed to be what gets built"* — and they accepted it knowing it is permanent. **Still strictly smaller than today's "no check at all", and that remains true and remains worth saying.** **This is a change in significance without any change in substance, which is the kind of change that is easiest to miss when a residual's text stays the same** — and it is the second consecutive revision in which R-1's weight has risen for a reason outside the design.
- **R-2 — PERMANENTLY ACCEPTED in revision 5, and its closure is now UNDEFINED rather than merely unscheduled.** AD-4 accepts a CODEOWNER `labeled` event from any point in the issue's history, including one that predates the content's current form. Same class as R-1. **Revision 4 closed it by "S3's invariant 4 (approval newer than the assessment)" — and with the assessor deferred indefinitely there are no assessments, so there is nothing for an approval to be newer than.** The invariant cannot be stated, let alone scheduled. **I record that as the sharper of the two downgrades: R-1 lost its mechanism; R-2 lost the term its closure was defined in.** *(Note also that R-2's text loses its `milestoned` half under AR-7: there is one signal, so the residual is now about `labeled` events alone.)*
- **R-3 — RESTATED FOR THE DISPATCH-LABEL CHANNEL IN REVISION 5 (AM-36, binding), with its live instance named. ACCEPTED for S2 by AM-8, whose closing rule STANDS and now has NO SCHEDULED DATE.**

  > **Why a restatement was required rather than an edit.** Revision 4's R-3 read *"a CODEOWNER `milestoned` event that was later reversed and re-applied by a bot"* — **which, after AR-7, describes a channel that no longer exists.** `pm-agent` flagged it (AR-8) and the architect rules it: *"a residual describing a nonexistent channel is one the next reader closes as obsolete."* **The residual is real; only the channel it pointed at moved.**
  >
  > **The restatement, binding (AM-36 point 1):** *the gate authorizes on **history**, not on current state — `probe.py:315-322` walks the whole event list and `continue`s past bot actors rather than stopping at them, so **a CODEOWNER `labeled` event for the dispatch label authorizes forever, even after a bot has removed the label and re-applied it.***
  >
  > **The live instance is named: #1542** (AM-36 point 2, `pm-agent`'s §0.2 measurement, cited not re-derived). A human applied `needs-ai` on **2026-09-10T18:35:18Z**; a bot removed it fifteen minutes later; a bot re-applied it on **2026-09-15**. **The gate authorizes it on the 2026-09-10 human event.** This also discharges the panel's §5 item 2 re-check: **#1542 is an R-3 instance, not an RP-1 instance.** *(A residual with a named live instance is a residual a reviewer can inspect; three revisions carried this one without one.)*
  >
  > **Explicitly NOT licence to add a de-authorization rule to S2** (AM-36 point 3, and `pm-agent` warned of exactly this misreading). Doing so would add unmeasured availability risk to a `priority:critical` slice on round 5, against AM-8's explicit acceptance, **and S3 is already required to measure that impact before enabling it.**
  >
  > **FR28's tension with R-3 is RECORDED, not resolved** (AM-36 point 4). FR28 says an authorization *"is re-derived live at every selection"*; **an authorization derived from an event whose effect has been reversed is not re-derived in the sense FR28 means.** `pm-agent` declined to convert that into a new S2 requirement and the architect agrees. **It goes into the S2 work item (#1539) as a stated tension with its S3 closure named** — not discovered later by a reader.

  The architect's original reason for not folding it into S2 is the availability risk, not the security judgment: honouring reversal events changes *when* authorization evaporates, and nobody has measured how often bots toggle labels in normal operation. Getting that wrong stalls all autonomous work behind a human re-click. **Binding for S3, verbatim from AM-8, and note that it already covers BOTH channels, which is why AR-7 does not require it to be rewritten:**

  > Authorization from an event source is evaluated against the issue's **current** state of that signal: a `demilestoned` (for the matching title) or `unlabeled` (for the matching label) event **clears** any authorization derived from a prior matching event, regardless of which actor performed the reversal. Re-establishment requires a fresh qualifying event by a verified human CODEOWNER. A bot re-applying a signal a human once applied does not restore the human's authorization.

  **S3 has no scheduled date, and R-3's closure travels with it.** The human's H1 item 3 defers the **assessor** track (S5) indefinitely and is silent on S3's comment channel; **AM-8's clearing rule does not depend on an assessor, so it is unscheduled rather than undefined** — the distinction that keeps R-3 out of the R-1/R-2 downgrade above. **A reader must not infer a date from the rule's existence.** S3 must **measure the availability impact** (how many live issues the rule would de-authorize on the day it ships) before enabling it, and report it in the PR body. One interaction worth recording now, because it is easy to miss when S3 is written: §2.7.4's cutover route — the human removes and re-adds the dispatch label from their own account — **survives this rule**, because the re-add is itself "a fresh qualifying event by a verified human CODEOWNER". The cutover work is not invalidated by S3.

  > **Reachability argument and re-check trigger — NEW in revision 4 (AM-28(f), binding on R-3's S3 entry).** The panel verified twice, independently, and the architect verified the PR-side half a third time, that **the only site adding the dispatch label to an *existing* issue was `label-swap.yml:74` (`/approve`, human-triggered)**; `merge_authority.py`'s dispatch-label writes (`:633` on the oversight-verdict path, `:1223` in `record_pr_bounce`'s default) are **PR-side**, and `docs/LABELS.md:29` documents them as such. **AM-25 strengthens this from "currently unreachable" to "no scripted writer exists at all":** with `/approve`'s label write removed, **the last scripted writer of the dispatch label onto an existing issue is gone.**
  >
  > **The qualifier *"in this repo, today"* is exactly what §9's decay warning is about, so the re-check trigger is stated rather than left implicit: any new automation that writes the dispatch label to a milestoned issue re-opens R-3**, and nothing mechanical detects it. That is the same question §9 puts to the panel as a conformance-test problem — a rule of the form *"no present or future automation adds this label to a milestoned issue"* is a property of the whole repo and is strictly harder to pin than a call site. **This argument and this trigger MUST travel into R-3's S3 entry**, not only live here: an acceptance whose reachability argument is left behind in a superseded design document is an acceptance the next reader has to re-derive or take on faith.
- **R-4 — ACCEPTED by AM-7 with three named revisit triggers.** Marker forgery by an induced bot session (§1.5.2 D-4). Requires an LLM under a trusted App identity to reproduce a deterministic template byte-for-byte *and* apply the target milestone *and* apply the dispatch label — and on this repo exactly one machine-filing site can reach the candidate set at all (§1.5.1(iii)). Bounded, and strictly smaller than today's no-check-at-all. **Any one of D-6's three triggers reopens the separate-App question (§8/E-2) as a required decision, not an option**, and §6.1's `test_marker_table_literals_exist_in_emitting_files` and `test_issue_creation_site_count_is_pinned` are what make triggers 2 and 3 detectable rather than noticed-later. Those two tests are **binding** (AM-7): they are the enforcement arm of a declined structural change, and relaxing either as "brittle string tests" silently converts an accepted residual into an unbounded one.
- **R-5.** The 10-page (1000-event) pagination bound means an extraordinarily churned issue can never be authorized. Availability only, fails closed. **AM-5 adds the part that keeps it a residual rather than a permanent invisible stall:** hitting the bound MUST emit `WARN events-page-bound-reached issue=#<n>` to stderr (§1.4, §2.3 D5.3, F14). Without that line, R-5 lands on precisely this repo's most-relabelled — and therefore most important — issues, with no signal anywhere that it has.
- **~~R-6~~ — CLOSED in revision 4 by AM-22 / AF-8. It is NOT carried, and the closure is the point rather than a housekeeping note.** Revision 3 recorded it as *"the 100-issue single-page list ceiling is preserved from today; a milestone with more than 100 open dispatch-labelled issues truncates silently. Unchanged, recorded, not fixed here."* **Every word of that was true and it was still the wrong disposition**, for a reason nobody measured: **the ceiling was three records from binding.** 97 non-PR records on a 100-record page (measured by the architect 2026-09-15, re-derived by me 2026-09-16 — §0.0.3), moving toward the edge **monotonically** as new issues are filed, and dropping **the oldest** — which is exactly the region P-1 starved. **A P-1 fix that changed the cap and left the page would have fixed the instance and left the class**, and the failure would have returned in weeks with an identical user-visible signature (*#1539 is authorized and never gets built*), a different cause, and no diagnostic. **Step C now paginates at 5 pages / 500 records** (§2.2.1), the truncation case is **F33** with the loudest report class, and reaching it is an operational emergency rather than a residual. **Revision 5 adds the fact that vindicates the closure rather than merely justifying it: the 100-record ceiling R-6 preserved HAS NOW BEEN CROSSED — the population is 101** (§0.0.4). **Had R-6 been carried one revision longer, it would have fired.** **The lesson recorded against this row, because it generalises past it: a residual recorded without its distance to firing is a residual nobody can prioritise.** R-6 was honestly recorded for three revisions; what nobody did was run the one query that would have shown it was about to bind.
- **~~R-7~~ — RETIRED AS SPENT in revision 5 (AR-7 / AM-35). Retired, NOT closed, and NOT overturned.** Revision 4 recorded it as *"renaming a milestone de-authorizes every issue authorized under the old title until a human re-acts"* — the accepted price of AM-4's byte-for-byte title equality (F28/F29). **A milestone now authorizes nothing, so renaming one de-authorizes nothing.** **AM-4 was correct and the panel's 97/97 `{"title"}` measurement showed title equality was the only binding available; the residual is retired because its subject is gone.** *(Note the pattern, because it is the strongest argument that AR-7 is the right narrowing and it is a design observation rather than a ruling: **FIND-1, R-3's different-milestone half, R-7, §0.6 gap 5's unobserved payload, and AM-18's skip with its new stderr surface were ALL consequences of one channel, and all five went together.** The second door on the vault did not merely have a weaker lock — it had five of this design's residuals hanging off it.)*
- **R-8 — NEW in revision 5 (AM-29); ITS SUBJECT IS REPLACED IN REVISION 6 by the human's TP-1 ruling, and the replacement is recorded as a replacement rather than as an edit, because the old R-8 and the new one are different objects and a reader who conflates them will attack a closed surface.**

  > **WHAT REVISION 5's R-8 SAID, struck and retained (AM-38):** ~~*An untrusted author can raise their own record's WALK position by editing their issue, because an edit bumps `updated_at` and the walk key is `(rank, updated_at DESC, number ASC)`.* Three mitigations: (1) the record must already be in the target milestone AND carry the dispatch label; (2) rank dominates recency absolutely; (3) **the human's authorizing act is itself an update, so it always lands at the head of its band.**~~
  >
  > **WHY IT IS REPLACED, and the finding is larger than the residual it corrects.** The panel's **TP-1** — raised independently by **all four** chunks of the adversarial lens, and verified live by the orchestrator against **#1709**, whose `updated_at` exactly equals its newest comment's timestamp — established that **`issue.updated_at` is advanced by ANY GitHub account posting a comment**, requiring no permission at all on a public repository. **Two things followed, and revision 5 had neither.** (a) **Mitigation (3) was never exclusive to the human:** their authorizing act promoted the record, and one comment on each of the gated records in the same band pushed it back down, **for free, indefinitely, from a zero-privilege account.** (b) **The severity is not "an author can advance their own record" but "anyone can control which records the gate reaches"** — against R7 (*the gate is a single point of failure for all autonomous work*) that is **a denial of service on the whole autonomous pipeline, at a cost of one comment per issue.** It **cannot authorize anything** — every gated record still gates and the fail-closed direction is untouched — which is why the panel classed it HIGH rather than CRITICAL, and it is loud (`ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED`, `unevaluated:cost-ceiling`, `complete=no` all fire). **The human ruled: *"Switch to AM-29's own stated fallback `(rank, number DESC)` — immune to the comment-bump attack. Accept losing the promotion-on-authorization property as the cost of closing a zero-privilege, indefinitely-repeatable starvation vector in a security-fix mechanism. Security posture over convenience here."***

  **R-8 AS IT NOW STANDS — a deterministic, permanent within-band starvation of the LOW-NUMBERED (oldest) tail, with no act that moves a record within its band.** Under the binding walk key `(rank, number DESC)`, `number` is assigned by GitHub at creation and never changes, so within a rank band the walk order is **fixed for the life of the repository**. If the head of a band never clears, the early exit re-evaluates that same head every cycle and the records behind it are never reached — **not transiently, and not because anyone attacked anything.**

  **What bounds it — TWO mitigations, not three, and the third is withdrawn rather than missing:**
  1. **The record must already be in the target milestone AND carry the dispatch label to be in the candidate set at all**, which requires a triage write. **Unchanged, and now doing more work than before**, because it is one of only two.
  2. **Rank dominates position-in-band absolutely.** `priority:critical` is never displaced by anything in a lower band — the walk visits every rank-0 record before any rank-1 record, for any second key. **This is the same property the early exit rests on**, which is why the two compose without interacting (§2.2.1), and **it is the reason R-8 is a reachability residual and never a safety one.**
  3. ~~**The human's authorizing act is itself an update, so it always lands at the head of its band.**~~ **WITHDRAWN (TP-1).** The key it rested on was publicly writable. **In its place, a weaker statement that is at least true: the walk key is not influenceable by anyone — there is no act, by an attacker or by a human, that moves a record within its band.**

  **REVISION 7 (RUN4 RP4-5, documentary part) — the paragraph below overclaims, and this correction governs it.**
  - **Recovery act: none within a band. For rank 0 (`priority:critical`), none at all.** There is no higher rank to move a critical to. An old critical behind `--max-candidates` or more newer eligible criticals is never reached by any act.
  - **For ranks 1–3, a re-rank is at best partial.** The record enters its new band at its own (low) number, so under `number DESC` it lands at that band's **tail**. It is reached only if fewer than `--max-candidates` eligible records precede it in the walk (every higher band, plus every newer record in its new band) and the request budget lasts that far. **In practice it works only when the destination band is nearly empty.** RUN4 F4 measured this: promoting a record to `high` puts it at about walk position 83 today, which is reached, but it would not be once band 1 exceeds ~96.
  - **The tail, stated as a bound instead of the word "coarser".** In a fully-gated walk (the cutover state), the walk reaches at most `F + (C − P)` records, where `C` is the effective request ceiling, `P` the Step C list pages, and `F` the trusted-authored (free) records it passes. Each paid record costs at least one events page. **So at least `N − F − (C − P)` records are never evaluated on that cycle.** While the head of the queue does not clear, they are the same records every cycle: the oldest in the lowest bands. The bound is exact when every paid record needs exactly one events page. **At RUN4 M-1's measurement** (`N = 149`, `F = 4`, `P = 2`, `C = 100`) it is **47**. RUN4 counted about 40 `medium`, 7 `low` and every unlabelled record, and the formula agrees.
  - **The gate cannot name that tail.** The only per-record `unevaluated` lines are `query-failed` and, in revision 7, the one clamp-bitten `cost-ceiling issue=#<n>`. `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED issues:` names gated records only. **RUN4's remedy for this part** is a bounded `UNEVALUATED issues:` line (first N plus "k more"), or at least the boundary record. **It changes the output grammar, so it is NOT documentary, and revision 7 does not apply it.** Which remedy (if any) is wanted depends on the human's answer below, and is **deferred to that answer, not declined**.
  - **PENDING, NOT ANSWERED HERE: the human's question.** The worker put it on #1540 at 2026-09-25T07:16:34Z: *"I'm not asking you to reverse the ruling. I'm asking whether this cost was part of what you accepted."* The options it named, all already in the chain: keep `DESC`; walk `ASC` (AM-29's objection: starves the newest instead, and makes Step E a no-op); or raise the early-exit stop. **Read live this session: no human reply.** This document does not answer it and builds nothing on an assumed answer.

  **The one recovery act that survives, with its cost stated — this is mine, not the ruling's** *(superseded as a general claim by the revision-7 correction above)*. Because rank is the first key, **a starved record can still be moved forward by changing its rank** (applying a higher `priority:*` label). That is a **triage-privileged** write — the same privilege class as mitigation (1)'s precondition — so it is a recovery act and **not** a second gaming vector. **Its cost is real and is recorded rather than glossed: it is coarser than mitigation (3) was (a whole band, not the head of one), and using it means MISSTATING a record's priority to work around a walk order.** **A workaround that requires falsifying data is a residual, not a mitigation**, and it is carried here as one.

  **What COMPOUNDS it, recorded because the two bounds now fail in the same direction** (§2.3 Step E): Step C's list bound truncates by `created desc`, i.e. it drops **the oldest**, and the walk now reaches **the oldest last**. **The same region is disadvantaged by both.** Under revision 5's key an old record could at least be pulled forward by activity on it; under revision 6's it cannot. ~~**Rank dominance still holds absolutely across both**, so nothing omitted outranks anything emitted either way.~~ **Corrected in revision 7 (RP4-1).** Within the fetched set, nothing the walk left unreached outranks anything admitted. A list truncation can omit a record that outranks an admitted one (that is input completeness, AM-33), and so can a quarantine hole. Both force `complete=no`, and the hole is named per record.

  **The trade, restated as a trade because it has now been made twice in opposite directions and a reader needs both legs.** AM-29 exchanged *a deterministic, permanent starvation with no recovery act* for *an attacker-influenceable, transient one with a recovery act.* **The human's TP-1 ruling exchanges it back** — with one thing gained that AM-29 did not have (**the key's totality is now structural rather than measured**, §2.3 D5.1 limb (b)) and one thing lost that AM-29 valued (**promotion on authorization**). **Neither key is a claim of liveness.** Under a population past the ceiling a record nobody can re-rank sinks and stays sunk. ~~**The population (101) is already past the ceiling (100)** (§0.0.4)~~ **A fully-gated walk over the present population costs more requests than the ceiling allows** *(corrected in revision 7, RUN4 RP4-6(j): revision 6 compared 101 RECORDS with 100 REQUESTS. At 101 records a full walk cost `2 + 97 = 99` requests and the ceiling did not bind. At RUN4 M-1's 149 it costs `2 + 145 = 147 > 100`, and it does. §2.2.1's correction paragraph)*, **so the sinking is a present-tense property of this repository rather than a future one**, and the early exit makes the walk reach *fewer* records per cycle, not more. **§9 hands R-8 to the panel to attack IN ITS NEW FORM**, with that number attached; **the old form is closed and attacking it would be attacking a retired surface.**
- **R-9 — NEW in revision 5 (H3). The requester-trust boundary acquires a dependence on live GitHub repository settings that no committed artefact records.** Once a project owner adds a `tier:` entry, **who is a trusted requester is a function of the repository's collaborator-permission graph**, which changes with no commit, no review and no CODEOWNERS approval — and **this repository's live settings have been declared uninspected in all six documents of this chain** (§0.6 gap 3).
  - **This is the architect's own stated objection to ESC-2, and the human overrode it deliberately with a compromise that keeps half of it:** the roster stays committed and reviewable and records the **policy**; only *membership* moves. *"The reviewable artifact is the policy decision, not the individual names."*
  - **What bounds the residual today: HOS ships zero trusted tiers and this repository's roster ships empty**, so **R-9 is inert on this deployment and on every consumer until someone opts in.** It is a residual on a feature that is off by default, which is a materially smaller thing than a residual on the live path — **and that is a reason to record it plainly, not a reason to omit it.**
  - **What does NOT bound it: the failure direction on the trust side.** Tier *resolution* failing is fail-closed (it under-trusts). **Tier membership being wider than the project owner believes is NOT** — an over-broad tier (`tier:read`, on a repo where read access is granted liberally) makes every such collaborator's issues skip the authorization check. **That is a configuration hazard, not a code defect**, which is precisely why §4.3 requires the `.example` file to state that matching is exact and that HOS endorses no tier.
  - **Re-check trigger:** if this repository ever adds a `tier:` entry, its live collaborator list stops being an uninspected curiosity and becomes an input to a security control — **and §0.6 gap 3 must be closed before that entry merges, not after.**
- **R-10 — NEW in revision 6, and it is the first residual in this document that is about the EVIDENCE rather than about the artefact. The design gate that cleared this design was not measuring what it read (#1718, `priority:critical`).**
  - **The defect.** `scripts/vendor_invoke.sh` reports success on exit 0 with non-empty stdout and **never checks how much of the prompt the vendor actually consumed**; `VENDOR_INVOKE_BYTES` counts bytes **written**, not bytes **read**. Measured ceiling on `agy 1.2.3`: **≈68k input tokens / ≈270KB**. **On run 3 the adversarial lens silently received ~34% of the bundle and reported SUCCESS** (PANEL-RUN3 §0.0), and the run's own worked example shows the failure produces **confident wrong findings, not obvious silence**: a HIGH finding was refuted by text sitting in the unread 66%.
  - **Why it is a residual of THIS document and not only of the tooling.** **All three panel runs — run 1, run 2 and run 3 — used that unmeasured path.** Run 3 recovered by re-reading the bundle in four measured chunks, **a degraded mode that by construction cannot read the documents against each other** — which is standing instruction 1 (§9.4), the single most productive instruction in this chain and the source of P-2, RP-2, RP-4, AF-9 and R5's second false reason. **So this chain's finding set is a FLOOR, not a ceiling, and it is a floor on every one of the three runs rather than only the last.** A correctly instrumented run could find more; **that is a statement about this document's assurance level, which is why it is recorded here and not only in an issue.**
  - **Why it is not confined to design panels.** **`vendor_invoke.sh`'s production caller is `scripts/run_second_review.sh`** — the pre-PR cross-vendor second review at MEDIUM+/HIGH+ — **which sends `git diff` with no size guard.** Every second-review verdict in this repository's history rests on the same unmeasured path. **That is outside S2's scope and is #1718's, not this document's to fix.**
  - **Not accepted, and the distinction from every other row here matters.** R-1 … R-9 are things the design accepts and ships with. **R-10 is not accepted; it is DISCLOSED.** It has a named owner (#1718), it is `priority:critical`, and **it must land before #1136 builds `run_spec_panel.sh`** — otherwise the automated design-chain gate inherits the defect that invalidated the hand-run one. **It is not added to #1539's residual list** (§7.2's closing paragraph) because it is not a property of what S2 builds, **and I have not padded it on to look thorough.**
  - **The related fact, corrected here because three documents in this chain carry it wrong (TP-11): `scripts/run_spec_panel.sh` IS tracked — by #1136** (open, `needs-ai`, `priority:high`, milestone **v0.7.8 — Design-Chain Panel (ADR-033)**), which names it in terms: *"S4 — C — `scripts/run_spec_panel.sh` — The single deterministic gate invocation site (AD-2)."* **Run 1, run 2, `pm-agent` §5 and Amendment 4 §4 item 2 all assert that no open issue tracks it, and all four are wrong.** Prior searches missed it because the script name is in the issue's **body table, not its title**, and `query_issues.sh --list` is **page-capped at 100 with no pagination** — which is the same limitation that bounds §1.3.2's search for a `provision_agent_account.sh` issue, and is worth noticing as a repeat. **The live consequence is a sequencing question rather than a gap: the panel's invocation site is scheduled for v0.7.8 while the gate it automates is being hand-run for a `priority:critical` security fix in v0.7.0.** **That is a decision for someone to make deliberately; it is not mine and I do not make it.**

**Recorded in the S2 work item — which is `#1539` (AM-27 point 3).** The original ADR §3 carries a standing requirement that named residuals be written into the work item. **The set CHANGES in revision 5: R-7 leaves it (retired as spent) and R-8 joins it (Amendment 4 §3(b) H6 names it explicitly).** The residuals under that requirement are now **R-1** (ADR-named, and now permanently accepted), **R-3** (AM-8, with AM-28(f)'s reachability argument, AM-36's restatement and **#1542** as its live instance) and **R-8** (AM-29). **FR28's tension with R-3 goes with them** (AM-36 point 4), as does **AM-26's `machine-accounts.env` consumer precondition**. **R-9 is not on the list and I have not padded it on to look thorough** — it is inert until a project owner opts in, and it is carried here.

> **Revision 3 named "the S2 work item" as the carrier of three residuals and never identified it.** The panel is right that a binding instruction with no named landing site lands nowhere. **It is `#1539`**, ~~subject to **H6**'s confirmation that S2 is the fix that closes it~~ — **H6 IS ANSWERED, YES** (the human, #1540, 2026-09-16T19:56:31Z), which was already the architect's recommendation in ESC-1 and the panel's own finding that *"S2 subsumes the Step 0 self-triage half"*. **Binding, and no longer conditional: R-1, R-3 (with AM-28(f)'s reachability argument, AM-36's restatement for the dispatch-label channel, and **#1542** named as its live instance) and R-8 are posted as a comment on #1539**, together with AM-26's `machine-accounts.env` consumer precondition and FR28's recorded tension with R-3. **R-7 leaves the list, retired as spent with the channel it was about.** ~~**If H6 comes back negatively, the work item is whatever issue the human names and the same obligation follows it.**~~ *(That branch is closed by the ruling.)* **REVISION 6 adds one binding qualification to the R-8 half: R-8's CONTENT was replaced by the human's TP-1 ruling, so the comment must carry R-8 as §7.2 now states it — a deterministic starvation of the oldest tail with two mitigations and ~~one costly recovery act~~ *(revision 7: no recovery act within a band, none at all for rank 0, and a partial re-rank act for ranks 1–3, with the tail bound `N − F − (C − P)` and the human's pending cost question)* — and NOT revision 5's attacker-influenceable version, which is closed.** Filing and commenting are worker actions, not this document's — but *that* they happen, and *where*, and *which text*, is binding.
>
> **R-1's weight is raised, not merely carried** (`REQUIREMENTS-1540-AMENDMENT-1` AR-6, ratified by AM-23 point 3). With FR4 narrowed to trust and the authorization model governing, **R-1 is now a residual on the *only* live barrier**: an untrusted author can edit the issue's title or body after a CODEOWNER authorizes it, and S2 will not notice. It reaches the human directly in **H1 item 5**, in pm-agent's words: *"what you read is not guaranteed to be what gets built."* That is a change in significance without a change in substance, and it is the kind of change that is easy to miss when a residual's text stays the same.

**R-2, R-4, R-5, R-9 and R-10** are recorded here and carried by this document; they are not separately required in the work item, and I have not padded the list to look thorough. **R-10 is the one row whose exclusion needs its own sentence, because it is `priority:critical`: it is excluded because it is not a property of what S2 builds — it is a property of the gate that reviewed the design — and it already has a named owner in #1718.** **R-6 is CLOSED** (above) and **R-7 is RETIRED AS SPENT**; both are therefore carried nowhere, and the difference between the two dispositions is deliberate — **a closed residual was fixed, a retired one had its subject removed by a ruling**, and a reader who cannot tell them apart will re-open the wrong one.

### 7.3 Startup-gap recovery and affected-sign-offs analysis

FIND-1 and FIND-2 are defects in code that shipped for #1539 (`abef062e`) and that S2 now depends on. Asking the required question — *should this have been settled before code was written against it?* — the answer is **yes**: #1539 went from human ruling to merged code with **no requirements and no technical-design stage**, so the contract `_verify_codeowner_actor` had to meet was never written down anywhere, and two defects (FIND-1 HIGH, FIND-2 LOW) plus one unstated impossibility (AF-5) were found by the first design document to look at it, five days later.

The architect reached the same conclusion independently in the amendment's §2 and **widened it**: the gap covers **AM-4, AM-6 and AM-10** — three instances of one class, an ADR asserting a mechanism without checking it against the mechanism's own constraints. AM-6's instance is the sharpest: AD-4 said "events **and comments**" and AD-6 assumed `/approve` was reusable as an authorization channel, and a five-minute read of `label-swap.yml`'s `env:` block — which AD-6 already cites for three other properties — would have caught it. AM-10's is a contradiction *inside one decision* (`ADR-1604` AD-9 points 1 and 2).

**One `startup-artifact-gap` issue covering AM-4 / AM-6 / AM-10 is required**, and it is **Amendment 1 §3(a) item A11 — a worker action, explicitly not a gate on the build.** Neither the architect nor I have filed it: the architect does not open issues from an architecture ruling, and this task is design-document-only. It is recorded here so that the absence of the issue is a visible omission rather than a silent one.

**Updated in revision 3 (Amendment 2 §2 and §3(a) A18), still a worker action and still not a gate.** The issue was filed **twice** — **#1692 and #1693 are the same issue** — so one should be closed. The survivor must be **annotated**, not supplemented with new issues, with three further instances of the *same* root cause (a load-bearing claim asserted rather than derived, in a document whose job is to be the thing others rely on): **AM-14/AF-6** (the "far smaller than 46" volume claim, wrong by measurement, asserted in the same document whose §3 flagged that exact defect class); **AM-17/E-6** (Amendment 1 contradicting itself about `.github/workflows/**` in two sections, caught by reading the document against itself); and **AF-7** (the architect's first reading of the duplicate-`labeled` scan overstating a blind spot that measurement showed to be empty — the same defect with the opposite sign, and understating evidence is not the safe direction, because it misleads H1 and misdirects the panel just as surely). **Nothing was built or designed against any of the three**, so nothing is orphaned by them; the analysis is in §7.3's sign-off table above. A separate, narrower follow-up is also requested by AF-7 and is **explicitly not folded into S2**: land the paginated duplicate-`labeled`-event scan in `scripts/` with a test, per `CLAUDE.md`'s "the second time you need it" rule, so the panel and any consumer deployment can re-run what they are handed. S2 must not acquire a sixth thing to carry.

**Updated in revision 4 (Amendment 3 §2 and §3(a) A30) — still a worker action, still not a gate, and still an ANNOTATION rather than a new filing.** Amendment 3 asks that the surviving `startup-artifact-gap` issue (#1692/#1693, one to be closed) be annotated with **four further instances**, and they are worth listing with their *shapes* rather than their names, because the shapes are what recur:

1. **AM-19 / AM-22 / AF-8 — a load-bearing number asserted rather than derived.** The authorization-check cap was introduced by *this document*, at self-rated MEDIUM confidence, explicitly "not derived from measurement" — and it reached the human's clearance text **inside an assurance it falsified**. One `--list` query, which the architect ran twice in under a minute and which I ran again this session, was the whole of the missing evidence. **The same one-command omission produced AF-6 and AF-8.** The architect records the architecture half as theirs (AD-3 bound the gate's algorithm and never asked what bounds its cost or in what order it spends it); **the design half is mine**, and the honest statement is that I introduced a bound whose value decided whether a `priority:critical` issue could ever be selected, and did not measure the population it applied to.
2. **AM-23 / P-2 — a claim correct in the place nobody reads and wrong in the place the decision-maker reads.** AD-4's header asserted FR4 compliance while its body specified the opposite of FR4 as written, 36 lines apart in one document. **The architect records this as the third instance of that class in this chain** (AM-17's four-versus-five was the first, P-7(b)'s uncorrected BLAST RADIUS line the second). **It has a design half too, and it is AR-5's finding: FR4 appeared zero times in revision 3's 1,363 lines.** A design that implements a requirement without naming it is one revision away from implementing its opposite without noticing — which is exactly what happened.
3. **AM-25 — accepting a stated justification without checking it, in a document that records the acceptance as a ruling.** I stated three reasons for leaving `/approve` live; the architect read them, found them sound, and declined to overrule. **Two were false and both were checkable in one `grep` each.** Amendment 2 §4 records "the architect read the reasons" as though that were verification. **It was not — and the half that is mine is worse: I wrote two of them without checking either**, and both were false in the direction that supported the conclusion I had already reached (§2.7.2).
4. **AF-9 — an internal contradiction inside one ADR section.** AD-3 says an untrusted candidate costs one events call *"only until it is approved or declined"*; AD-4, in the same section, says eligibility is **never cached** and is re-verified every cycle. Both cannot be true, and **the cost model that P-1's fix turns on was the one that was wrong.** Nothing was built on it, but H1's cost disclosure would have been wrong had the architect not caught it.

**Updated in revision 5 (Amendment 4 §2 and §3(a) A43) — still a worker action, still not a gate, still an ANNOTATION rather than a new filing. Two further instances, and the second is the one this chain should find uncomfortable:**

5. **H3/ESC-2 — a trust-boundary question left open through four amendments while the artefact it governs was designed, reviewed and panelled.** §1.2, §1.3 and §1.4 specified the roster as a flat list of logins across four revisions and two adversarial panel runs, **with ESC-2 open the whole time.** The human's H3 ruling changes the loader's return type, the roster's file format, the `.example` file's content, `TrustedSet`'s shape, and adds a network dependency to the trust path. **Nothing is orphaned only because no code was written** — had `coder` been cleared at revision 3, every one of those would have been built against a superseded contract. **The shape: an unanswered question about a mechanism's INPUTS is not a question that can wait behind the mechanism's design, and treating it as one was safe here by accident.**
6. **`ADR-1540-AMENDMENT-4` §0.1's *"no live GitHub read was performed this session"*, which caused it to escalate as the critical path a human ruling that had been posted ~8 hours earlier.** The architect read the documents and the working tree and missed the answer. **Same class as AF-6, AF-7, AF-8, AM-19's cap and AM-30's derivation: a load-bearing claim asserted from available artefacts rather than checked against the live system** — and this instance is the one where the artefact that would have settled it was **a comment on the very issue the document is about**, obtainable through the sanctioned read path in one command. **I record it as an instance and not as a criticism of the ruling**, every one of which stands: §0.0.5 enumerates what the human's answers overtake, and nothing in Amendment 4's §1 depends on H1's status.

**Do not file new issues for these** — Amendment 3 §3(a) A30 and Amendment 4 §3(a) A43 are both explicit. **And the §4 follow-ups Amendment 3 recommends are recommendations to the human, not filings** (filing is the intake path, and #1539's whole subject is what may enter it). The five it names: `scripts/run_spec_panel.sh` does not exist despite ADR-033 AD-2 binding it as the single deterministic invocation site for **this very gate** — *"D41's own failure class inside the mechanism built to catch it"*, and it has a cost already paid, because **this panel must now re-run and there is no script to re-run it with** — **CORRECTED IN REVISION 6 (TP-11): the script's ABSENCE is real and unchanged, but the accompanying claim that nothing tracks it is FALSE. `#1136` tracks it** (open, `needs-ai`, `priority:high`, milestone **v0.7.8 — Design-Chain Panel (ADR-033)**), naming the path explicitly in its body table. **Run 1, run 2, `pm-agent` §5 and Amendment 4 §4 item 2 all carry the false version, and Amendment 4's recommendation to fold the work into #1696 is therefore misrouted.** **This item is consequently NOT one of the five unfiled follow-ups — it is a scheduled work item two milestones out, which is a different thing and a sharper one: the gate is being hand-run for a `priority:critical` security fix in v0.7.0 while its automation sits in v0.7.8** (§7.2 R-10). **Why four documents missed it is worth one sentence, because the cause recurs: the script name is in the issue's BODY, not its title, and `query_issues.sh --list` is page-capped at 100 with no pagination** — the same limitation that bounds §1.3.2's search and that this document must therefore stop treating as an exhaustive check; agy's empty-`SUCCESS` envelope hazard; FIND-2's unfixed `_verify_label_actor` half, correctly out of S2's scope but diagnosed and left live; **the `CLAUDE.md` / `bin/hos-cron:1764` `bypassPermissions` contradiction (§2.5)**; and **`query_issues.sh` emitting no `.user` field**, which is why P-1's central authorship measurement is not re-derivable by any later reviewer through the sanctioned read path (§0.0.3).

> **THE AFFECTED-CLEARANCE ANALYSIS, which is new in kind rather than in degree — a clearance is not a sign-off and this document has never had to analyse one before (§0.0.5 row 3).** The human answered **Amendment 3 §3(b)'s H1**, not Amendment 4 §3(b)'s. **Amendment 4 then superseded Amendment 3 §3(b) IN FULL** (AM-38), changing exactly two bullets of item 2. **So the text that was cleared is not the text that now stands, and the required question is whether the clearance still covers what will be built.** I analyse both changed bullets rather than asserting a conclusion.

> | **Item 2's cost bullet (AM-30).** They cleared *"the ~105-request worst-case API cost … accepted as designed."* Amendment 4 then established that **≈105 was a floor described as a ceiling** — the true worst case under the old denomination was **up to 1,000 requests/cycle** — and re-denominated the bound to **≈100 REQUESTS**, a real ceiling | **YES — it holds, and it holds on the only ground that matters: the corrected envelope is NO WORSE than the one they accepted.** They accepted ≈105 requests/cycle; the design now delivers **≤100 requests/cycle by construction**, and the human's own early-exit directive pushes the typical case far below that. **A clearance given for a number is not invalidated by the design coming in under it.** **But the disclosure defect is real and I record it rather than let the favourable arithmetic bury it: the human accepted a figure that was false when it was put to them, and nobody has told them so.** §0.0.5 and this row are that telling, and the S2 PR body must carry it too (§4.3) — **"it turned out fine" is not the same as "they were told" ** |
> | **Item 2's milestone bullet (AR-7).** Amendment 3 told them *"do not USE the milestone route"*; Amendment 4's replacement says *"a milestone assignment carries no authorization weight at all"* | **YES — it holds, and the change is in the direction that REDUCES what they are being asked to accept operationally.** Amendment 3 already instructed them not to use the route, so **their day-to-day obligation is identical**; what changed is that the route is now inert rather than discouraged, which removes a failure mode (a benign planning re-milestone silently authorizing work) rather than adding one. **`pm-agent` measured the cost at ZERO of 98 candidates de-authorized.** ~~**The residue is Q11**… **and it is unanswered**~~ — **CLOSED IN REVISION 6: Q11 is ANSWERED, YES** (the human, #1540, 2026-09-16T19:56:31Z): *"The milestone assignment stops counting as authorization by itself; only the dispatch label counts going forward."* **The human has now ratified the narrowing of their own #1539 ruling item 1 directly, so there is no residue: the clearance stands AND the ratification is in hand.** The "AR-7 fails closed, so a late no restores a channel rather than un-building one" argument is **retained as the record of why revision 5 was safe to build on an unanswered question** — it is no longer load-bearing, and it should not be cited as though it were |

**Why `_verify_codeowner_actor`'s approvals are orphaned, stated plainly because revision 1 argued the opposite.** Revision 1 recorded them as *"stand for what they approved — with `expected_milestone_title=None` the shipped behaviour is bit-for-bit preserved and the nine existing tests pass unmodified; the contract is extended, not changed."* **Both halves of that justification are withdrawn.** AM-4 inverts the default, so `None` now means "ignore `milestoned` events entirely" and the milestone path's behaviour **genuinely changes**; and §1.6/§6.4 show five existing tests must change, so "pass unmodified" was false on its own terms — for four of them independently of the ruling. Behaviour-preservation was the thing that made the old approvals transferable, and behaviour-preservation is exactly what the ruling removed. The approvals are therefore orphaned **for the milestone-authorization path**, and re-review is routed to `code-reviewer` and `security-reviewer` at S1.

**Reviewer independence note:** these are re-reviews of *shipped* code against an amended contract, not re-approvals of the same diff. The reviewer must read `probe.py`'s milestone strategy as it will exist after S1 — with the inverted default, the title binding, and the bounded pagination — and must not treat the `abef062e` approval as a starting point to be confirmed.

**The honest closing statement, corrected.** Revision 1 wrote *"the only behavioural change on an approved path is DEV-3 (strictly stricter)."* **That is no longer true.** AM-4 changes `probe.py`'s milestone-strategy authorization semantics on a path that shipped, was approved, and that **every consumer deployment inherits** — the change is strictly stricter in the direction that matters (a forgotten argument now gates rather than authorizes), but "stricter" is not "unchanged", and a consumer running the milestone strategy will see issues stop being selected. What remains true is the property the CORE contract actually requires: **no code approved against the superseded contract is left unaudited against the fix.** The one shipped path this amendment changes is routed to named re-reviewers in the table above, and §6.4 names the tests that will demonstrate the change rather than hide it.

The three revision-2 escalations did exactly what they were raised to do, and it is worth recording which effect each had, because "escalate rather than absorb" is otherwise an unfalsifiable habit: **E-5 changed the architect's recommendation and the price the human is shown**; **E-6 caught the architect's own document contradicting itself and prevented a four-surface clearance for a five-surface change set**; **E-7 was answered rather than accepted, and the answer improved the design by one required diagnostic.** **E-8 now joins them and is the strongest instance yet: it was ruled LARGER than it was raised** — I found a broken derivation and the architect found a broken unit underneath it. **Four escalations, four effects, one refusal with reasons.** That is the distribution a working escalation path should produce, and it is the evidence for a habit that would otherwise be unfalsifiable.

### E-1 — **RULED AND CLOSED by AM-1 and AM-2 (backfill by AM-3, option C's text corrected by AM-6).** Recorded in full because the subset argument below was adopted as binding reasoning and now governs more than this question.

> **Disposition.** Option **C ADOPTED**, with **D ADOPTED as the ergonomic wrapper**. Option **A REJECTED** as vacuous by the subset argument. Option **B REJECTED and permanently forbidden** — AD-13, as clarified by AM-2, now forecloses any creation-time flag, argument, body token or HTML marker that confers trust, under any identity, with any documented human-confirmation convention attached (see §1.5.2 **D-5**). The **backfill** is ruled by AM-3: **a grandfathering list is forbidden**, and the cutover set is bounded by the gate's own query rather than by the open backlog — see §2.7.3, and then §8/E-5, which is what that cutover actually costs. **AD-2 stands verbatim; nothing is widened.** The routing question I raised was answered rather than forwarded: see "the routing ruling" at the end of this entry.

**The tension, stated precisely.** ADR line ~163 says `scottthurlow-claude[bot]` "is a trusted *requester* under AD-1(b) and is explicitly **not** a valid *approver*." AD-2 then narrows category (b) to bot-authored issues that also carry an enumerated machine-filing marker. The human-proxy App is not a machine-filing site: per `CLAUDE.md`, its default path is *"file an issue for the autonomous worker to pick up"*, composed interactively by an LLM. Both statements are correct and together they gate it. Live counts: 46 of 100 open issues are `scottthurlow-claude[bot]`-authored, and four of the five current candidates are gated by this alone (§2.6).

**Why I cannot resolve it with a marker.** A marker is only meaningful when the set of call sites that can emit it is a **strict subset** of the set of call sites the identity can reach. For the worker/overseer Apps that holds: deterministic filings (class (i)) are a strict subset of all their filings (class (i)+(ii)). For the human-proxy App **every** filing is class (ii), so any marker `create_issue.sh --app human` stamps would appear on all of them and be **isomorphic to trusting the identity outright** — the laundering path AD-2 closes, under a new name. That is the analysis, not a preference.

**The options, as proposed and as ruled:**

| Option | What it does | Cost | **Ruling** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** — stamp a marker on `--app human` filings | Trusts all human-proxy filings | Vacuous narrowing; reopens AD-2's laundering path | **REJECTED.** Vacuous by the subset argument — it reopens the laundering path AD-2 closes, under a new name, **while every test passes**. |
| **B** — add a `--confirmed` flag to `create_issue.sh --app human` (the `submit_pr.sh --app human --confirmed` precedent) and mark only those | Narrows *within* the identity to filings the human authorized per-instance | The LLM types the flag, so it is a prose guarantee with extra steps — D53's failure class | **REJECTED AND FORBIDDEN (AM-2).** It is a command-line flag whose presence marks a request approved, which AD-13 already prohibits; AM-2 makes that explicit so it is not re-proposed each time the queue feels slow. **The `submit_pr.sh --confirmed` precedent does not transfer:** there the flag gates *a mutation the human is watching*, conferring no standing authority on any later autonomous decision; here it would confer autonomous-build authorization on a future cycle, read by a machine, with no human present at read time. See §1.5.2 **D-5**. |
| **C** — keep AD-2 exactly as written; close the live-path gap through **AD-4, not AD-1** | A human-proxy-filed issue is untrusted-*authored* and becomes selectable when `ScottThurlow` personally applies the dispatch label or the target milestone **from their own GitHub account**. Uses only mechanism the ADR already binds. | One human act per issue — but see E-5: for the cutover set it is **two** | **ADOPTED.** Adds no trust concept and widens nothing. |
| **D** — gate human-proxy filings and let the human-proxy session stop applying `needs-ai` itself, always ending a filing by telling the human the exact act required | C, plus an ergonomic path | Needs a `CLAUDE.md` human-proxy-section edit, a protected surface and a separate human decision | **ADOPTED as the ergonomic wrapper**, and it is *also* the correct hygiene fix independent of ergonomics: a human-proxy session applying `needs-ai` to its own filing is the same self-authorization shape as the worker's Step 0 (VF-3), one identity removed. It should stop regardless. Not pre-authorized here (AD-14), and deliberately **not** in S2's file list — §2.7.1. |

**Revision 1's option C and option D each contained one false clause, and AM-6/AF-5 removed both.** C said an issue becomes selectable when the human applies the label or milestone *"or comments `/approve`"*; D reduced the act to *"one comment"*. **Both are wrong.** `label-swap.yml` performs the `/approve` label edit under `GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}`, so the resulting `labeled` event's actor is `github-actions[bot]`, excluded unconditionally by `is_bot_reviewer` layers 1 and 2. A CODEOWNER commenting `/approve` produces **no authorization** in S2. The correction and everything that follows from it are §2.6 and §2.7.2; the acts that *do* authorize are stated verbatim in §2.7.1. The table above has been rewritten accordingly rather than annotated, because a false approval path left visible in a table is the exact artefact AM-1 says is worse than no gate.

**The subset argument, now binding rather than my opinion.** The architect adopted it as binding reasoning and credited it to this document: *a marker is meaningful only when the set of call sites that can emit it is a strict subset of the set of call sites the identity can reach.* For the worker/overseer Apps that holds — deterministic filings (class (i)) are a strict subset of all their filings — so the marker carries information. For the human-proxy App **every** filing is class (ii), so the emitting set equals the reachable set and the predicate carries zero bits. It is not a preference; it is the definition of a vacuous predicate. It now governs D-5, D-6 and D-7 as well as this question.

**The routing ruling — the architect answered the question I asked rather than forwarding it, and the reasoning matters more than the answer.** I asked whether *"are human-proxy filings trustworthy?"* is a product/operations question that belongs with the human. The ruling: **in its raw form it is, but it is not load-bearing, so it does not need the human's answer to unblock this work** — because even a maximally favourable answer ("the human reads every filing before it is made") **has no sound mechanism to express it**. Expressing it requires either A (vacuous) or B (forbidden by AD-13). There is no third construction. The architecture question therefore collapses to a single admissible answer and was ruled, not routed. What **is** the human's is the *consequence*, and it was already on record as **ESC-6 item 2** — so E-1 opened no new escalation; the architect amended ESC-6 item 2's text instead of creating ESC-7. **AM-1 binds as the technical call and takes effect only once ESC-6 clears**, which was already true of every decision in the original ADR.

**The honest cost, carried forward so nobody is surprised.** This converts a frictionless path into a per-issue-act path, and the mechanism's most likely real-world defeat — named in the original ADR's own closing section — is a single overloaded CODEOWNER clicking through without reading. Architecture cannot prevent that. AM-3 bounds the volume; it does not remove the obligation. **And the volume was mis-stated twice, in opposite directions, which is why §2.7.3 now carries a measurement rather than a framing.** Revision 1's "~46 issues at cutover" was wrong because the cutover set is bounded by the gate's own query — open, dispatch-labelled, non-PR, in the active milestone — since everything else costs one act at the moment it would otherwise have been selected. Revision 2 then inherited AM-3's inference that the bounded set was therefore *small*; **that is also wrong, and AF-6 corrected it: the set is ≥93, a page-limited floor.** Bounding a set by a query says nothing about its cardinality. §2.7.3 states the measured figure, the query that produced it, and both of its error bars.

**The backfill — ruled, not open.** Revision 1 asked for a ruling between a bulk relabel, a lazy drain, and a grandfathering list. **AM-3 forbids the grandfathering list outright**: a static list of pre-approved issues is two violations at once — a trusted-set widening (AD-13) *and* a cached eligibility determination (AD-4/FR7, where an authorization verified at time T must be re-verified at T+n and an earlier approval is evidence to re-check, never a stored grant). §2.7.3 implements the ruling. The remaining choice was framed in revision 2 as the human's, between a pass and a lazy drain, with the architect recommending the pass. **Revision 3 corrects that: AM-14 withdraws the single-pass recommendation, and the default is the lazy drain plus a single-digit primed head** (§2.7.3). The human retains the right to do a pass anyway and may want a larger head, but it is no longer put to them as an equal option — **what they are asked to clear in ESC-6 item 2 is the standing per-issue obligation at its true price (two acts), not a cutover plan.** AM-13's `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` line is no longer a backstop for a pass that was not done; it is **load-bearing** for the default. **§8/E-5 is what produced that correction, and it is why E-5 existed.**

### E-2 — **RULED AND CLOSED by AM-7 (the separate App) and AM-8 (R-3).** The marker's anti-forgery ceiling.

> **Disposition.** The separate deterministic-filing App is **declined for now, as recommended** — R-4 becomes an accepted, recorded residual with **three binding revisit triggers**, any one of which reopens this as a *required decision, not an option*. **R-3 is accepted for S2 and closed in S3**, with the clearing rule stated now so S3 does not redesign it, and it **must be recorded in the S2 work item alongside R-1**.
>
> **REVISION 5 ANNOTATION (AM-36 + the human's H1 item 3).** Two things about this disposition have moved and §7.2 is where the current form lives. **(i) R-3 is RESTATED for the dispatch-label channel**, because after AR-7 the `milestoned` event it described no longer exists — *"a residual describing a nonexistent channel is one the next reader closes as obsolete"* — **and its live instance is named: #1542.** **(ii) "Closed in S3" now carries NO SCHEDULED DATE.** The human deferred the assessor track indefinitely; **AM-8's clearing rule does not depend on an assessor, so R-3 is UNSCHEDULED rather than undefined** — which is what keeps it out of R-1 and R-2's permanent-acceptance downgrade. **AM-8 itself is CONFIRMED and not overturned, and AR-8 is explicitly NOT licence to add a de-authorization rule to S2.**

Within one bot identity, **no in-band marker can be unforgeable against induction**: the inducing agent has the same write authority and can read the marker table out of the repo (§1.5.2 D-4). The only construction that is genuinely unforgeable is a **separate GitHub App identity used exclusively by deterministic filing code** (`bin/hos-cron`'s five `gh issue create` sites plus `self_review_source.py`), so that class (ii) prose filings *cannot* author under it. The architect confirmed the analysis and declined the build: it is structural (an App to provision, rotate and document in `machine-accounts.env` and `AGENT-IDENTITY.md` §7) and S2 is the live-path fix for an open `priority:critical`.

**The three revisit triggers are binding and are written into the design at §1.5.2 D-6**, with §6.1's `test_marker_table_literals_exist_in_emitting_files` and `test_issue_creation_site_count_is_pinned` as the mechanism that makes triggers 2 and 3 detectable rather than noticed-later. Those two tests are therefore **binding, not optional** — they are the enforcement arm of a *declined structural change*, and a reviewer tempted to relax either as "brittle string tests" is proposing to convert a bounded residual into an unbounded one without saying so.

**R-3 — ruled, and not as I framed it.** Revision 1 offered R-3 as "your call whether it belongs in S2 or a follow-up". **AM-8 chose neither of those framings:** it is accepted for S2 and **closed in S3 by a rule stated now**, which is stronger than "a scoped follow-up" because it removes the redesign. The reason for not folding it into S2 is availability, not a weaker security judgment: with AM-4's title binding in place the surviving case requires the human to have genuinely placed the issue in the *work* milestone at some point — materially weaker than FIND-1, where the human's act meant the opposite — while honouring reversal events changes *when* authorization evaporates, and nobody has measured how often bots toggle labels and milestones in normal operation. Getting that wrong stalls all autonomous work behind a human re-click, the failure mode AD-15 warns about. The rule, the S3 measurement obligation, and the interaction with §2.7.4's cutover route are in **§7.2 (R-3)**. **§7.2 now also carries the ADR §3 requirement that R-1, R-3 and R-7 be written into the S2 work item.** *(Revision 7, RUN4 RP4-6(d): the set is now **R-1, R-3 and R-8**. R-7 was retired as spent with the milestone channel (AM-35) and R-8 replaced it (AM-29), as §4.3 and §7.2 state. This sentence is revision 2's record and is corrected here rather than edited.)*

**One thing the panel should press on, which neither the amendment nor I resolved:** AM-8's own confidence on this deferral is recorded as **MEDIUM**, explicitly because the availability frequency that determines the trade was not measurable from the architect's position. If the panel can measure it, the amendment invites the deferral to be revisited on evidence rather than on judgment. I am not re-litigating it; I am recording that it is the one ruling in the amendment that asked to be re-examined.

### E-3 — **RULED AND CLOSED by AM-9.** `ADR-1604` / `TECHNICAL-DESIGN-1604` Component H owns the file S2 deletes.

> **Disposition.** **S2 ships first**, as recommended. But the ruling went further than my recommendation in a way I had no standing to propose: **`ADR-1604` AD-5's *mechanism* bullet is amended** — the queue exclusion is expressed **once**, as an entry in `select_work_candidates.py`'s `EXCLUDED_LABELS` named module constant, with **no inlined twin and no lock-step divergence test**, because after S2 there is no second implementation to diverge from. AD-5's *semantics* (one narrow marker, authoritative for both the count and the exclusion; the generic human-attention label stays advisory and is never counted) are **unchanged and still binding**. Component H's sign-offs are **orphaned**. The full sequencing contract is **§4.5**; the sign-off dispositions are **§7.3**.

`ADR-1604` AD-5 and `TECHNICAL-DESIGN-1604-worker-self-split-isolation.md` §4.6 bind changes to `scripts/automation/lib/next_candidates.jq` (new `timeout-stuck` / `blocked` exclusions), to its inlined twin in `worker-cron-prompt.md`, and to `tests/automation/test_next_candidates.py` — including a `test_jq_label_literals_match_hos_labels` conformance test *(revision 7, RUN4 RP4-6(k) / run 3's TP-9: a test that design **plans** and never built. It does not exist in `tests/` (grep, this session), and §6.3 no longer treats it as existing)*. S2 deletes all three artefacts. Neither design has shipped.

Three consequences now binding, all restated in §4.5 so the coder does not have to read §8 to build:

1. **Whoever picks up #1604 next must carry this amendment into that document as the *first step of the work*,** not as a cleanup afterwards: re-point §4.6, §4.7's Component H row and the `test_next_candidates.py` test row, and note AD-5's amended bullet.
2. **The other admissible ordering is stated in advance** rather than improvised: if #1604 somehow reaches build before S2 merges, it ships against `next_candidates.jq` unchanged and S2 absorbs the two exclusions during its deletion. That ordering is permitted, is the **only** other admissible one, and **must be stated in whichever PR lands second**. What is not admissible under either ordering is both artefacts existing at once — the #1135 duplicate-implementation defect this design exists to remove.
3. **`EXCLUDED_LABELS` is bound as the landing site.** My instinct to make it a named module constant rather than an inline tuple was adopted as the seam, which means it is now a contract surface: it lives in `select_work_candidates.py` (selection policy), **not** in `requester_trust.py` (trust) — see §5's explicit note — and #1604's entries are added to it rather than to a second list.

### E-4 — **RULED AND CLOSED by AM-11 (G11) and AM-12 (DEV-1).** Two smaller collisions, and one of them was larger than I filed it as.

> **Disposition.** **G11 is SUPERSEDED, not merely overlapped** — a stronger word than "should be marked superseded rather than built twice", and it comes with an **acceptance condition binding on S2**. **DEV-1 is APPROVED**, with a binding added test.

1. **`ADR-1542` §4.9 (G11) — superseded, with a condition S2 must *meet*, not merely intend.** G11 planned a sandbox-allowlistable wrapper around `next_candidates.jq` for the worker's Step 2 fallback (`REQUIREMENTS-1542` VF-7's specific complaint about `--jq "$(cat …)"` at `worker-cron-prompt.md:101`). S2's entry point **is** that wrapper, built for a different reason, and building G11 separately would produce the duplicate-gate defect (#1135) in its purest form: two wrappers over the same selection decision, one of them trust-gated. G11's line item in `ADR-1542`'s slice 3 is satisfied by S2's merge; its sign-offs are orphaned **for that item only**, and `ADR-1542`'s other slice-3 items are untouched (§7.3). **The condition:** the rendered fallback invocation MUST contain **no command substitution, no variable expansion, no inline `jq`, and no backslash continuation** — and **§6.3's conformance test is the required mechanism**, not an optional nicety (§2.5, §6.3). By taking G11's supersession, S2 has taken G11's acceptance obligation with it; an intention about a prompt string, unpinned by a test, survives exactly until the next person needs a second milestone.
2. **DEV-1 — approved, and the architect's reason is better than mine.** I filed the PR-record exclusion as a deviation I was recording rather than absorbing, offering to revert it. The ruling approved it on a ground I had not stated: leaving PR records in would mean the **issue** gate silently adjudicates **PR** eligibility, which contradicts AD-14's scope boundary far more than filtering them does. Both behaviours are a change; the filtered one is intentional, testable, and cheaper. **The condition:** §6.2's `test_bounced_draft_pr_still_reaches_the_worker_and_blocks_the_skip` must prove the bounce loop intact (§7.1 DEV-1). If it cannot be made to pass, **DEV-1 is withdrawn and PR records stay in — escalate back to the architect rather than adjusting the skip logic.** I read that path this revision and it is satisfiable by construction (`_OPEN_PR_NUMS` comes from a different endpoint, and the skip at `:1158` requires `-z "$_OPEN_PR_NUMS"` independently); the test pins the independence rather than discovering it.
