## Human Review Required

**RISK: HIGH.** This document is the implementation contract for a control that decides whether autonomous work begins at all, on a public repository where §0 of the ADR confirms the current answer is *no check at all* on the live path. The specific ways it can fail while looking correct: (i) a consumer reading `is_trusted_requester` as an eligibility test and skipping `requester_verdict`, which would restore the full AD-2 laundering path while every test still passes — §6.1's sole-caller conformance test exists only because of this; (ii) `not is_bot_reviewer(...)` reappearing anywhere, which admits every anonymous member of the public; (iii) a `--repo-root`, `--exclude-label` or **`--trusted-tier`** flag being added later for testing convenience, any of which is a complete bypass through an ergonomics affordance; (iv) the worker improvising a `gh api` fallback when the gate exits non-zero, which is why §2.5's prohibition is a tested prompt string and not advice; (v) **AM-13's `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED` block being dropped as logging polish**, after which a fully-gated queue is indistinguishable from an empty backlog for as long as nobody looks. The gate is also, by construction, a **single point of failure for all autonomous work**: every fail-closed row in §3 is a row in which the worker does nothing.

> **Revision 4's failure mode (iv) — *"AM-4's inverted default being restored by a coder chasing green tests"* — is RETIRED with the parameter (AR-7 / AM-35), and its retirement is itself a hazard worth naming.** The property AM-4 protected is now **structural rather than defaulted**: there is no argument to forget and no default to invert. **But that is only true if the deletion reached every call site**, which is why §9.1 item 5 exists and why §6.1 asserts the function's **arity** rather than only its behaviour — *a test that checks behaviour cannot tell a deleted parameter from an ignored one.*

**Seven failure modes carried from revision 4, every one a way for a correct-looking later edit to undo a ruling that closed a CRITICAL finding.** They are Amendment 3's risk register, carried into the design deliberately because **the amendment is not the artefact a future contributor will be reading when they make the edit**:

- (vii) **The ranking being "optimised" back below the authorization check** by someone who observes — correctly — that ranking a set the gate may not fully evaluate looks wasteful. **That single edit restores P-1 exactly, with every test that does not assert order still green.**
- (viii) **The request ceiling being lowered back toward 25** on cost grounds without the FR7 paragraph in view — which buys the saving back in exactly the coin P-1 was paid in. §2.2.1 requires any such proposal to cite that paragraph and state what it is trading.
- (ix) **The early exit being read as "the gate may stop whenever it likes."** It may stop only when what remains **cannot outrank** what it has. **A stop that admits a later trusted record breaks the ADMISSION-prefix property silently** (*wording corrected in revision 6 ~~per the human's TP-4 ruling~~ as a correction under the human's TP-4 classification, on the panel's recommended limb (RP4-8); the hazard is unchanged, and in revision 7 a budget clamp that bites is one of the stops it covers*) — and **revision 5 raises this from a hazard to a near-certainty of exposure, because the stop now fires on nearly every cycle rather than rarely.** **Revision 6 adds that it is now silent in one further way: Step E re-sorts the emitted set, so a wrongly-admitted record does not even appear out of place in stdout.**
- (x) **`complete=no` being dropped as a field nobody parses** — after which AM-13's held-everything line resumes making a claim it cannot support, **and exit 3 stops firing, because exit 3 is computed from it** (§2.2). In revision 5 that field carries control flow at the caller, not only meaning for an operator.
- (xi) **The roster being added to `framework_consumer_files.txt` "for completeness"**, which converts every HOS upgrade into a **silent trust-boundary edit** — deleting every trusted contributor a consumer added. **AM-26's reason must travel with the list entry** (§4.3).
- (xii) **`/approve` being restored to a label-writing form** by someone who reads AM-25's basis as the wording fix rather than the inert one — a live risk precisely because *this document argued that position for two revisions and stated three reasons for it.*
- (xiii) **H1 being answered from a superseded §3(b).** **This one is now HISTORY rather than a live risk, and how it resolved is worth recording: the human answered Amendment 3's §3(b), which Amendment 4 then superseded in full — so the hazard fired, in the mildest available form.** §7.3's affected-clearance analysis is the response, and it concludes the clearance holds on both changed bullets **with one re-disclosure owed.** The general rule is AM-38's: **amendments supersede; they do not overwrite.**

**Six failure modes are NEW in revision 5, and all six are ways to undo a ruling made after the last panel run saw this document:**

- (xiv) **The walk key being "simplified" into the emission key, or Step E deleted as redundant.** Revision 4 called Step E *"a no-op on a correct implementation"* and told the coder to keep it as a tripwire; **AM-29 makes it load-bearing and that sentence is withdrawn.** Deleting it now **changes what the worker builds first** — reversing `#901`'s documented FIFO-within-band — while every membership test stays green. §6.2's `test_emission_order_is_rank_number_even_when_the_walk_order_differs` is the only mechanical defence, **and it is one test against a sentence this document itself wrote.** **REVISION 6 MAKES THIS THE MOST DANGEROUS ROW IN THIS LIST, and the reason is arithmetic rather than emphasis: under the human's TP-1 ruling the two keys are `(rank, number DESC)` and `(rank, number ASC)`. They differ by ONE TOKEN.** The "simplification" is now a single-character edit, it looks like a typo fix, and it silently reverses a documented product policy. **Nothing else in this document has a blast radius that large behind an edit that small.**
- (xv) **The `WARN` being restored to the cost ceiling "for symmetry" with the other unevaluated tokens.** **The asymmetry IS the ruling** (AM-30 point 3): the ceiling binds in normal operation — §0.0.4 measures the population past it — so a warning there fires every cycle and is invisible by week two. §6.2 asserts its **absence**, which is the kind of test a well-meaning contributor deletes as strange.
- (xvi) **Exit 3 being "simplified" to fire on `complete=no` alone.** At `bin/hos-cron`'s call site, where every non-zero arm of §2.4's `case` clears the candidates (*caller description corrected in revision 7, RP4-6(g)*), **that discards usable candidates on the routine early-exit path** — an incompleteness *report* turned into a work-selection *failure*, on the most common path in the design. §6.2 tests the condition from both sides.
- (xvii) **The trusted-author fast path being widened to "the author is an HOS App identity"** — the literal reading of the human's directive, which **skips verification on 97 of 101 live records and makes the gate inert on this repository's entire present queue**, exempting the exact 49-record block AM-1 says must be authorized. §8/E-10, unanswered, with the numbers attached. **This is the single most consequential misreading available in revision 5.**
- (xviii) **Tier matching being made hierarchical, or moved to the `permissions` object.** The first widens the trusted set silently (`tier:write` suddenly admitting admins) **and, as of revision 6, reverses a human ruling rather than a designer's choice** (TP-5, §1.3.2); the second **silently fails every `read` and `write` entry while passing every test written against a fixture the same coder wrote** — **and `scripts/framework/provision_agent_account.sh:190` is a shipped, live instance of exactly that second failure, in the same directory S1 lands in.** §0.6 gap 8 and §6.1's named acceptance check.
- (xix) **A tier membership snapshot being cached "to save a request."** It is 1–3 requests per cycle, and **a cached tier membership is a cached trust determination** — it would let a revoked collaborator stay trusted until someone remembered to re-run a script. §1.3.2, §6.1.

**Two failure modes are NEW in revision 6, and both are ways to undo a ruling the human made AFTER the third panel run:**

- (xx) **`updated_at` being restored to the walk key** — by a contributor who finds AM-29 point 1 in the amendment chain, sees the primary binding and the fallback side by side, and "restores" the primary on the strength of §0.0.4's measurement that the field is present on 101/101 records. **That measurement is real, it is not the objection, and reading it as an argument for the key inverts the entire finding.** The objection is that **`updated_at` is advanced by anyone posting a comment**, which makes the gate's walk order publicly reorderable and hands any GitHub account an indefinitely-repeatable denial of service on all autonomous work (TP-1, §7.2 R-8). **The amendment chain still contains the withdrawn key and does not contain the reason it was withdrawn** — that reason lives here, in §7.2 R-8 and §8/E-11, **and that asymmetry is exactly why this row exists.** §6.2's walk-key test is the mechanical defence and it asserts `(rank, number DESC)` by name.
- (xxi) **The admission-prefix property being "tightened" back into a claim about stdout** — by a reader who finds §2.2's exit-0 row wordy and restores the crisper *"stdout is an exact prefix of the ranked walk."* **That sentence is arithmetically false and it was BINDING for a full revision before the panel caught it** (TP-4). **It is false for a reason that reads as a technicality and is not one:** taking the top-`k` under one order and re-sorting by another yields a permutation, not a prefix. **The tell that it has happened is `test_admitted_set_is_a_prefix_of_the_complete_walk` being "fixed" by comparing byte-for-byte output again — which is the exact form that could not pass**, and a contributor who makes that edit will find the test fails on the very next fixture and may then adjust the fixture instead of the assertion. **§6.2's docstring requirement exists to stop that: the test must say the assertion was rewritten because this document got it wrong.**

**Three failure modes are NEW in revision 7. Each restores a defect run 3 found and run 4 had to find again:**

- (xxii) **A budget-clamped record being counted `gated` again "because it is the same as the page bound".** It is not. The page bound is a determination and a clamp bite is the absence of one (TP-2, §1.4 rule 4). **The tell is a record that a complete walk authorizes showing up in `ALL-CANDIDATES-GATED issues:`**, which is a false statement about the human's queue. §6.2's `test_budget_clamp_that_bites_is_unevaluated_not_gated_and_stops_the_walk` is the defence. **A second tell is a trusted record admitted after a clamp bite, because "it was free".**
- (xxiii) **`list-truncated` being turned back into a count "for symmetry" with the other three fields.** GitHub returns no total, so the count cannot be derived (AM-31). The records are outside `N`, so summing them breaks `N = E + U` (TP-3). §6.2's `test_list_truncated_is_a_flag_outside_U` asserts the literal `yes`.
- (xxiv) **F30 being "restored" to exit 0 on the grounds that "the held condition is never an exit code".** That half-sentence is true, and it is exactly how revision 6 arrived at the wrong exit. Completeness decides the exit. At the present population a fully-gated walk is always incomplete, so exit 0 there is AM-32's *"no actionable work"* misreading on the cutover's main path (RP4-2).

**CONFIDENCE (revision 7): MEDIUM-HIGH on the RP4-1 repair, and I rate it below HIGH for the same reason revision 6 rated its repair MEDIUM: no lens has read it.** The clamp half is removed at its cause. After a bite no record is stepped over, and I have worked RUN4's counterexample 1 through the new rule by hand. I am **MEDIUM** on the quarantine half. "Over determinations" is the correct statement of what the walk guarantees, but it is precisely the kind of restatement that can make a property unfalsifiable, and §9.5 names that as the first thing to attack. **HIGH on RP4-2 and TP-3**: each is one consistent rule where there were two contradictory ones, checked at every site `grep` finds. **MEDIUM on RP4-4's rewritten differential test**, which I checked for satisfiability on all four mandated fixtures by hand, the check whose absence produced TP-4 and RP4-4. **LOWER on anything touching `bin/hos-cron` line numbers**, which #1805 has invalidated (§2.4's revision-7 note).

**CONFIDENCE: HIGH** on §0.1, §1.5.1's enumeration, and §2 — each re-derived from the working tree. **HIGH** on FIND-1's analysis and its closure-by-deletion. **HIGH** on AF-5's consequences (§2.6, §2.7.2). **HIGH** on the five-surface count (§2.1) — checked against `protected_surfaces.txt` in revision 2, re-verified by the architect before AM-17, confirmed by Amendment 3, confirmed again by AM-34, **and now cleared by the human at H1 item 4.**

**HIGH, and NEW in revision 5, on the three things I read live this session that nobody upstream had:** the human's three #1540 comments (§0.0.4), `next_candidates.jq` in full — **`#901`'s FIFO-within-band is a documented product policy and AM-29 turns on it** — and `probe.py`'s corrected line ranges at `:257-277` and `:280-324`, where **every one of Amendment 4's citations is confirmed at the line** and the `:254-345`/`:283-345` this document carried since revision 1 were wrong.

**HIGH on AM-29's central claim** — rank dominance is a property of `(rank, X)` for any total `X`, which is structural rather than a judgement *(revision 7: over the records the walk determined or left unreached. A quarantine hole is the named exception, RP4-1)* — **and HIGH on the early exit composing with it safely**, for the same reason: a stop truncates the tail of an order and cannot reorder it. **HIGH on AM-31's quarantine being security-neutral** (one query per record, no shared state, failure direction *not authorized*), which `probe.py:298-303` already ships.

**NEW IN REVISION 6 — HIGH on the walk key's TOTALITY and on the attack it closes; MEDIUM on the repair I wrote for TP-4; and the gap between those two ratings is the honest summary of this revision.** **HIGH** that `(rank, number DESC)` is a total order by construction (issue numbers are unique within a repository — structural, needing no measurement, unlike revision 5's key) **and HIGH that it closes TP-1 completely** (nothing writes an issue number after creation, so there is no act by any actor that reorders the walk within a band). **MEDIUM on the TP-4 repair** — the rescoped admission property, its arithmetic, and the three rewritten §6.2 tests — **for the plain reason that no lens has read a word of it and the thing it replaces was BINDING and unsatisfiable for a whole revision.** §9.1 item 9 hands it over and §9.5 puts it at the top of my own list.
